final answer
Stock and COGS are booked in exactly one place. The Delivery Note books them when it ships, or the Sales Invoice books them when `update_stock=1`. When a Delivery Note has already shipped the goods, the Sales Invoice books no stock or COGS entries. I read the code paths below. I didn't run any of this or look at the tests. ## 1. Sales Order - The Sales Order (`erpnext/selling/doctype/sales_order/sales_order.py`) is a commitment document. It posts no stock ledger entries and no GL entries. - It affects stock only by reserving it: `update_reserved_qty` and the Stock Reservation Entry logic. - The Delivery Note and Sales Invoice map from it through `make_delivery_note` and `make_sales_invoice`. Each child row carries `so_detail`, which links delivered and billed quantities back to the order. ## 2. Delivery Note: where stock and COGS are booked `DeliveryNote` is in `erpnext/stock/doctype/delivery_note/delivery_note.py`. Its `on_submit` runs in this order: 1. `update_prevdoc_status` updates `delivered_qty` and `per_delivered` on the Sales Order. 2. `update_billing_status`, backed by `services/billing_status.py` (`BillingStatusService`), updates `per_billed`. 3. `update_stock_reservation_entries`. 4. `update_stock_ledger`, inherited from `SellingController` (`selling_controller.py`, around line 676). This creates the Stock Ledger Entries. 5. `make_gl_entries`, which calls `get_gl_entries`. That delegates to `DeliveryNoteGLComposer` in `services/gl_composer.py`, a subclass of `BaseStockGLComposer` (`erpnext/stock/services/base_stock_gl_composer.py`). 6. `repost_future_sle_and_gle`. The composer debits the item's expense account and credits the warehouse's stock account, both at valuation. That is the COGS entry, and it is booked at delivery time. `validate_expense_account` shows how the account is chosen. If the company has `enable_stock_delivered_but_not_billed` and a `stock_delivered_but_not_billed` account (SDBNB, account type "Stock Delivered But Not Billed"), the Delivery Note sets `item.expense_account` to the SDBNB account. The delivery debit then lands in that interim account rather than in COGS. Items with `against_sales_invoice` are barred from using SDBNB. ## 3. Sales Invoice: how COGS is not booked twice `SalesInvoice` is in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/sales_invoice/sales_invoice.py`. `on_submit` calls `update_billing_status_in_dn` and `update_prevdoc_status`. These update `billed_amt` on the Delivery Note and Sales Order rows through the `dn_detail` and `so_detail` links. It then calls `update_stock_ledger` only `if self.update_stock == 1`, and finally `make_gl_entries`. `get_gl_entries` delegates to `SalesInvoiceGLComposer.compose` in `services/gl_composer.py`. Three mechanisms stop the double booking: 1. **The `update_stock` flag.** - `make_item_gl_entries` adds the stock and COGS entries (`gl_entries += super(SalesInvoice, doc).get_gl_entries()`) only when `cint(doc.update_stock)` is set and perpetual inventory is enabled. - An invoice created from a Delivery Note has `update_stock = 0`, so it books revenue, receivable and tax entries but no stock or COGS entries. - Validation reinforces this: `validate_delivery_note` and `check_prev_docstatus` require the linked Delivery Note to be submitted. `validate_multiple_billing` (`BillingValidationService`) stops the same `dn_detail` from being billed more than once. The `update_stock` and `dn_required` checks (around line 861) prevent an invoice that updates stock from also being linked to a Delivery Note. 2. **Reversing the interim SDBNB booking** (`stock_delivered_but_not_billed_gl_entries`). - This runs when SDBNB is enabled and the invoice has `update_stock = 0`. - `_get_sdbnb_booking_for_item` finds the Delivery Note account through `dn_detail`. `_is_sdbnb_reversal` confirms that account is of type SDBNB and differs from the invoice item's expense account. - It then reads the Delivery Note's Stock Ledger Entry (matched on `voucher_no`, `voucher_detail_no` and `item_code`). The valuation amount is the SLE rate multiplied by the invoice's `stock_qty`. - `_append_sdbnb_gl_entries` credits the SDBNB account and debits the real COGS account, `item.expense_account`. - So with SDBNB on, COGS is recognised at invoicing, in the amount delivered and billed. The Delivery Note's entry only parked the cost in the interim account, and the invoice moves it out. - Without SDBNB, the Delivery Note already debited COGS directly and the invoice adds nothing. 3. **Returns.** `disable_sdbnb_in_sr` and `is_return` control whether the SDBNB reversal applies to a return. Cancellation goes through `make_gl_entries_on_cancel` and `make_reverse_gl_entries`. ## Summary | Document | Stock Ledger Entries | Stock and COGS GL entries | |---|---|---| | Sales Order | None | None (reservation only) | | Delivery Note | `update_stock_ledger` | `DeliveryNoteGLComposer`: debit COGS (or SDBNB), credit Stock | | Sales Invoice with `update_stock=0` | None | Revenue and receivable only. If SDBNB is on, it moves the cost from SDBNB to COGS. | | Sales Invoice with `update_stock=1` | `update_stock_ledger` | Revenue, receivable, and COGS and Stock, with no Delivery Note involved |

final answer
I've traced the flow and have enough to answer. I didn't read the Sales Order submit code or `BaseStockGLComposer` in full, so those points are marked as inferred. ## Lifecycle **1. Sales Order** (`erpnext/selling/doctype/sales_order/`) - The Sales Order is non-stock and non-accounting. It creates no Stock Ledger Entries (SLE) or GL entries. - `mapper.make_delivery_note` (about lines 232-421) and `mapper.make_sales_invoice` (about lines 425-608) build the downstream documents. They carry `so_detail` and `against_sales_order` links. - `make_sales_invoice` only bills the unbilled quantity. In `update_item`, `get_billed_qty` and `billed_amt` are subtracted from the Sales Order row. - Progress flows back through the `StatusUpdater` machinery (`erpnext/controllers/status_updater.py`): - `DeliveryNote.__init__` sets `status_updater` to update `delivered_qty` and `per_delivered` on the Sales Order. - Its first entry has a `second_source_dt` of Sales Invoice Item, restricted to invoices with `update_stock = 1`. That means invoices that deliver stock themselves also count toward delivery. **2. Delivery Note** (`erpnext/stock/doctype/delivery_note/`) - It inherits from `SellingController`. - `on_submit` calls `update_prevdoc_status()` and `update_billing_status()`, which delegates to `services/billing_status.py::BillingStatusService`. It then calls `update_stock_ledger()` (`selling_controller.py` line 676), which writes the SLEs (stock goes out at valuation rate). Finally, `make_gl_entries()` runs. - `get_gl_entries()` delegates to `services/gl_composer.py::DeliveryNoteGLComposer`, a subclass of `BaseStockGLComposer`. That composer posts credit Stock In Hand and debit the item's `expense_account`. This is the only place the stock-out accounting is booked. - `validate_expense_account()` (about line 438) is the key step. For stock items not linked to a Sales Invoice, if the company has `enable_stock_delivered_but_not_billed` and a `stock_delivered_but_not_billed` account, it sets `item.expense_account` to that SDBNB account. - So at delivery time the debit goes to a **"Stock Delivered But Not Billed"** liability-type account, not to COGS. This is a temporary holding account. - If SDBNB is not enabled, the Delivery Note debits COGS directly and the invoice must not. **3. Sales Invoice** (`erpnext/accounts/doctype/sales_invoice/`) - `mapper.make_sales_invoice` in `delivery_note/mapper.py` creates the invoice from a Delivery Note. It sets `dn_detail`, `delivery_note` and `so_detail` on each invoice row. This is the link used to avoid double counting. - `update_stock` is left off for these invoices. The Sales Invoice only posts SLEs when `update_stock == 1`. The `on_submit` and `on_cancel` code paths check this flag (lines ~443, 460, 534, 541), calling `update_stock_ledger()` only when it is set. - Without the flag, the invoice creates no SLEs and no inventory credit, so there is no second stock movement. - `get_gl_entries()` delegates to `services/gl_composer.py::SalesInvoiceGLComposer.compose`. It always posts the receivable, tax and income entries, plus `make_item_gl_entries`. - When SDBNB is enabled and it is not a return with `disable_sdbnb_in_sr`, it calls `stock_delivered_but_not_billed_gl_entries`. - That method returns early if `doc.update_stock` is set or perpetual inventory is off. Otherwise, for each item with `dn_detail` or `delivery_note` it uses these helpers: - `_get_sdbnb_booking_for_item` finds the original Delivery Note's SLE by `voucher_no`, `voucher_detail_no = dn_detail` and `is_cancelled = 0`. It computes `valuation_rate × item.stock_qty`. - `_is_sdbnb_reversal` checks that the Delivery Note row's expense account has `account_type = "Stock Delivered But Not Billed"` and differs from the invoice item's expense account. - `_append_sdbnb_gl_entries` credits the SDBNB account and debits the item's expense account (COGS) for that amount. ## Why COGS is booked exactly once - **Stock movement** happens once, at the Delivery Note. The invoice has `update_stock = 0`, so it adds no SLE. - **COGS expense** is booked once, at invoicing. The Delivery Note parks the cost in SDBNB, and the invoice reverses it into COGS. - **Valuation amount** is taken from the Delivery Note's own SLE, not recalculated. The amount moved out of SDBNB therefore equals what the Delivery Note put in, even if the cost changed in between. - **Each Delivery Note row is invoiced only once.** - `BillingValidationService.validate_multiple_billing("Delivery Note", "dn_detail", "amount")` is called from `sales_invoice.py` (line 353). - `update_billing_status_in_dn` (line 1084) and `update_billed_amount_based_on_so` write `billed_amt` on Delivery Note Items and `per_billed` on the Delivery Note, so a row cannot be over-billed. - Billing a Sales Order directly (`so_detail` only, `update_stock = 0`) is spread across Delivery Notes first-in-first-out in `update_billed_amount_based_on_so`. - **If the invoice has `update_stock = 1`** (no Delivery Note, or invoice-first), the invoice posts its own SLE and COGS, and the Delivery Note is linked as `against_sales_invoice`. In that case `validate_expense_account` forbids the SDBNB account. - **Reposting:** `repost_item_valuation._update_post_delivery_billed_vouchers` adds the Sales Invoices that were created after a Delivery Note to the repost set. A valuation change on the Delivery Note therefore also updates the SDBNB reversal.

final answer
COGS is booked once because the Delivery Note books it to a "Stock Delivered But Not Billed" (SDBNB) account, and the Sales Invoice then reverses that booking into the real expense account. I read most of this in the code. Two parts I inferred without opening the code: where the Sales Order's `delivered_qty` and `billed_amt` are updated, and `BaseStockGLComposer`. The Sales Order → Delivery Note → Sales Invoice mapping functions were also not opened. **1. Sales Order** (`erpnext/selling/doctype/sales_order/sales_order.py`) - It has no stock or GL impact. It only reserves stock (`update_stock_reservation_entries`) and raises the Bin's reserved quantity. - Its `delivered_qty`, `billed_amt` and `per_delivered` fields are filled in by the status-updater mechanism. The Delivery Note and Sales Invoice do this through `update_prevdoc_status`. - The `make_delivery_note` and `make_sales_invoice` mapping functions copy `so_detail` onto the child rows, which is how later documents link back. **2. Delivery Note** (`erpnext/stock/doctype/delivery_note/delivery_note.py`, class `DeliveryNote`, which inherits `SellingController` and `StockController`) - `validate_expense_account` runs before submit. For stock items on a Delivery Note not linked to a Sales Invoice, it sets `item.expense_account` to the company's `stock_delivered_but_not_billed` account. This only happens when `enable_stock_delivered_but_not_billed` is on. Otherwise the expense account is the default one. - `on_submit` does the following in order: - `update_prevdoc_status` updates the Sales Order's delivered quantity. - `update_billing_status` calls `BillingStatusService`. - `update_stock_reservation_entries`. - `update_stock_ledger` (`SellingController.update_stock_ledger`, `selling_controller.py` ~L676) writes the Stock Ledger Entries (SLEs) that take stock out. - `make_gl_entries` calls `get_gl_entries`, which uses `DeliveryNoteGLComposer` in `delivery_note/services/gl_composer.py`. That class builds on the base stock composer in `StockController.get_gl_entries`, `BaseStockGLComposer.compose`, and so on. It posts Dr the item's expense account (SDBNB here) and Cr the Stock-in-Hand account. - `on_cancel` reverses all of this through `make_gl_entries_on_cancel`. **3. Sales Invoice** (`erpnext/accounts/doctype/sales_invoice/sales_invoice.py`, class `SalesInvoice`) - Each item row keeps `delivery_note`, `dn_detail` and `so_detail`. `update_billing_status` and the status updater feed back into the Delivery Note's `per_billed` and the Sales Order's `billed_amt`. - `SalesInvoice.make_gl_entries` calls `get_gl_entries`, which uses `SalesInvoiceGLComposer.compose` in `services/gl_composer.py`. That builds the customer receivable entry, tax entries, income entries (`make_item_gl_entries`), discount entries, and then the SDBNB step below. **How COGS is booked once** There are two mutually exclusive paths. - **Invoice with `update_stock = 1` (no prior Delivery Note):** the invoice posts its own SLEs and its own stock and COGS GL entries. This is the "Dr COGS, Cr Stock" posting. - **Invoice billed from a Delivery Note (`update_stock = 0`):** - The Delivery Note already posted Dr SDBNB, Cr Stock. - `SalesInvoiceGLComposer.stock_delivered_but_not_billed_gl_entries` returns immediately if `doc.update_stock` is set or perpetual inventory is off. - Otherwise, for each item, `_get_sdbnb_booking_for_item` looks at the item's `delivery_note` and `dn_detail`: - It skips non-stock items and rows with no Delivery Note link. - It reads the Delivery Note Item's `expense_account` and checks that this is an SDBNB account that differs from the invoice item's expense account (`_is_sdbnb_reversal`). - It then loads the Delivery Note's own Stock Ledger Entry, matched by `voucher_no` and `voucher_detail_no`. It computes the valuation rate as `stock_value_difference / actual_qty`, and the amount as that rate × `item.stock_qty`. - `_append_sdbnb_gl_entries` posts Cr SDBNB and Dr the item's real COGS expense account for that amount. - The net effect is that the liability-like SDBNB balance clears at invoicing and the expense lands in COGS exactly once. The SDBNB entries are skipped for returns when `disable_sdbnb_in_sr` is set. - The amount comes from the Delivery Note's actual SLE valuation, not a recalculated rate. The invoice therefore matches the true stock outflow and posts no extra stock movement. - The invoice is a separate voucher, so cancelling it simply reverses its own GL entries through `make_reverse_gl_entries`. If the company does not enable SDBNB, the Delivery Note books COGS straight to the expense account, and the invoice adds no COGS entries for those lines. **Guards that keep the paths separate** - `DeliveryNote.validate_expense_account` throws if an item linked to a Sales Invoice (`against_sales_invoice`) uses the SDBNB account. - Sales Invoice validation (`validate_dn_required` / `update_stock`, around L861 and L906) controls whether delivery is required, and stops `update_stock` being combined with a Delivery Note link. - `Company.validate_outstanding_sdbnb_transactions` in `setup/doctype/company/company.py` stops the SDBNB account being changed or disabled while Delivery Notes are still unbilled.

final answer
COGS is booked once because the Delivery Note books it and the Sales Invoice only does so when its own `update_stock` flag is on. An invoice made from a Delivery Note leaves that flag off. I traced this through the code and did not run it. I did not open `SalesInvoice.validate_delivery_note` (`sales_invoice.py:904`) or the `DeliveryNoteGLComposer` body. ## 1. Sales Order (`erpnext/selling/doctype/sales_order/sales_order.py`) - `SalesOrder` extends `SellingController`, which extends `StockController`, which extends `AccountsController`. - A Sales Order creates no stock ledger entries and no GL entries. - `validate` calls `validate_warehouse`, which requires a source warehouse for stock items unless `delivered_by_supplier` is set. It also calls `validate_drop_ship`. - `on_submit` calls `update_reserved_qty`, which sets the Bin's reserved quantity. It also calls `check_credit_limit` and, if `reserve_stock` is set, `create_stock_reservation_entries`. - The Sales Order is turned into a Delivery Note or Sales Invoice by the mappers in `sales_order/mapper.py`, including `make_sales_invoice` (line 425). - Those mappers fill the link fields `so_detail` and `sales_order` on the child rows. ## 2. Delivery Note (`erpnext/stock/doctype/delivery_note/`) - `DeliveryNote.on_submit` (`delivery_note.py:481`) runs these steps: - `validate_packed_qty` - `update_pick_list_status` - `update_prevdoc_status`, which updates `delivered_qty` and `per_delivered` on the Sales Order through the `status_updater` mechanism - `update_billing_status`, which calls `BillingStatusService` in `services/billing_status.py` - `check_credit_limit`, or `make_return_invoice` for credit notes - The stock ledger is written by `SellingController.update_stock_ledger` (`selling_controller.py:676`). It builds the source-warehouse entries through `get_sle_for_source_warehouse` and `make_sl_entries`. - The GL entries come from `DeliveryNote.get_gl_entries` (line 433). It calls `DeliveryNoteGLComposer.compose` in `delivery_note/services/gl_composer.py`, which extends `BaseStockGLComposer`. - `BaseStockGLComposer.compose` (`erpnext/stock/services/base_stock_gl_composer.py`) loops over each item's stock ledger entries. For each one it books a debit to the item's expense account (COGS, or "Stock Delivered But Not Billed" when that company setting is on) and a credit to the inventory account. The amount is `stock_value_difference`. - So this is the one place the cost is booked. ## 3. Sales Invoice (`erpnext/accounts/doctype/sales_invoice/`) - `make_sales_invoice` in `delivery_note/mapper.py:62` creates the invoice from a Delivery Note. It sets `delivery_note` and `dn_detail` on the invoice rows, and `update_stock` stays 0. - `SalesInvoice.get_gl_entries` (line 1070) calls `SalesInvoiceGLComposer.compose` in `sales_invoice/services/gl_composer.py`. - `compose` calls these in order: - `make_customer_gl_entry` - `make_tax_gl_entries` - `make_item_gl_entries`, which books income - `stock_delivered_but_not_billed_gl_entries` - The guard on cost is in `make_item_gl_entries` (line 374): ```python if cint(doc.update_stock) and erpnext.is_perpetual_inventory_enabled(doc.company): gl_entries += super(SalesInvoice, doc).get_gl_entries() # StockController COGS/inventory entries ``` The invoice books COGS and inventory entries only when `update_stock` is on. - Likewise, the invoice only writes stock ledger entries when `update_stock` is on, in `SalesInvoice.on_submit`/`make_gl_entries`. ## How the cost is booked once - **Delivered first, invoiced later:** - The Delivery Note books the cost. - The invoice has `update_stock=0`, so it books receivable, income and tax but no cost. Stock and cost are not touched twice. - If SDBNB is enabled, `stock_delivered_but_not_billed_gl_entries` (`gl_composer.py:164`) runs on the invoice. It returns early if `doc.update_stock` is set or perpetual inventory is off. - `_get_sdbnb_booking_for_item` finds the Delivery Note row through `item.delivery_note` and `item.dn_detail` and reads that row's `expense_account`. It also looks up the matching Stock Ledger Entry, whose valuation is the amount to reverse. - The Delivery Note's cost therefore sits in the SDBNB liability account until the invoice clears it into COGS. The cost is still booked once, just recognised at invoice time. - **Invoice only, no Delivery Note:** the user sets `update_stock=1`. The invoice then writes the stock ledger entries and the COGS entries itself. - **Reconciling the two documents:** - Delivery Note rows carry `billed_amt`, and `update_billing_status` / `update_billed_amount_based_on_so` in `delivery_note/services/billing_status.py` maintain it. - `SalesInvoice.update_billing_status_in_dn` (line 1084) runs from the invoice and updates `per_billed`. - These keep the Delivery Note's billed status in step with the invoice, so the same delivery cannot be billed twice. - `SalesInvoice.validate_delivery_note` (line 904) is the check that ties the invoice to its Delivery Note.

final answer
I've read the key files, so I'm writing the answer now. I haven't opened `set_rate_for_supplied_items` or `make_rm_stock_entry`, so what I say about them comes from their names and call sites. ## Subcontracting flow: raw materials out, Subcontracting Order tracking, Subcontracting Receipt cost roll-up ### 1. Subcontracting Order (SCO) defines what must be supplied - **File:** `erpnext/subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_order/subcontracting_order.py`, class `SubcontractingOrder(SubcontractingController)`. - `populate_items_table` builds the service-item to finished-item rows, typically from a Purchase Order. - `validate_purchase_order_for_subcontracting`, `validate_service_items` and `validate_supplied_items` check the order. - On validate, `set_missing_values` calls `calculate_service_costs`, `calculate_supplied_items_qty_and_amount` and `calculate_items_qty_and_amount`. - The base class `SubcontractingController.create_raw_materials_supplied_or_received` fills the `supplied_items` child table (`Subcontracting Order Supplied Item`). That table holds the required raw material qty from the BOM, with `reserve_warehouse`, `supplied_qty`, `consumed_qty` and `returned_qty`. - The helpers behind it are `set_materials_for_subcontracted_items` and `__set_supplied_or_received_items`. The BOM explosion is in `_get_materials_from_bom` and `get_available_materials`. - `update_ordered_qty_for_subcontracting` and `update_reserved_qty_for_subcontracting` update the Bin's ordered and reserved quantities. - `reserve_raw_materials` and `cancel_stock_reservation_entries` handle stock reservation. - `update_subcontracted_quantity_in_po` updates the Purchase Order. - `update_status` sets the order status. The module-level `set_subcontracting_order_status` and `update_subcontracting_order_status` are the whitelisted entry points. ### 2. Sending raw materials to the supplier (Stock Entry, "Send to Subcontractor") - **Create the entry:** `make_rm_stock_entry` in `erpnext/controllers/subcontracting_controller.py` builds the Stock Entry from the order. It uses `add_items_in_ste` and sets the `sco_rm_detail` link field. - **Alternative creation:** `get_items_from_subcontract_order` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/services/subcontracting.py` also creates it, using `get_supplied_items`. - **Validation:** `SendToSubcontractorStockEntry` in the same file (subclass of `BaseStockEntry`) checks the transfer. - `validate_subcontract_order` and `validate_subcontracting_order_for_bom` check that the item belongs to the order's BOM. - `_get_required_qty_for_bom`, `_validate_transfer_qty`, `_link_rm_detail_if_missing` and `validate_subcontracting_order_for_transfer` enforce the quantity limits. - `get_total_supplied_qty`, `get_total_returned_qty` and `get_order_rm_detail` supply the quantities already transferred or returned. - **Tracking on submit and cancel:** `on_submit` and `on_cancel` call `update_subcontract_order_supplied_items`. That function uses `_get_order_supplied_items`, `_get_supplied_items_details` and `_update_supplied_items_in_order` to write `supplied_qty` back to the SCO supplied items. It then calls `_update_reserved_qty_for_subcontracting`. - **Related Stock Entry code:** - `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/services/material_transfer.py` has its own `update_subcontract_order_supplied_items`. - `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/stock_entry.py` has `update_subcontracting_order_status`, `reserve_stock_for_subcontracting` and `validate_closed_subcontracting_order`. - **Returns:** - `make_return_stock_entry_for_subcontract` returns unused material from the supplier. - `get_materials_from_supplier` does the same through the "Material Return" stock entry. ### 3. Receiving finished goods: Subcontracting Receipt (SCR) - **Creating the receipt:** `make_subcontracting_receipt` and `get_mapped_subcontracting_receipt` in the SCO file create the receipt. The mapping is also in `subcontracting_receipt/mapper.py`. - **File:** `erpnext/subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_receipt/subcontracting_receipt.py`, class `SubcontractingReceipt(SubcontractingController)`. - **Validate:** - `before_validate` runs `validate_items_qty`, `set_items_bom`, `set_items_cost_center`, `set_service_expense_account` and `set_expense_account_for_subcontracted_items`. - `validate` runs `reset_supplied_items`, then `set_missing_values`, which calls `calculate_items_qty_and_amount`. - `validate` also runs `validate_secondary_items`, `validate_accepted_warehouse`, and the supplied-item helpers `set_supplied_items_expense_account`, `set_supplied_items_cost_center` and `set_supplied_items_inventory_dimensions`. - **Consumption:** the controller calls `__set_supplied_or_received_items`, which uses `__get_transferred_items` and `__get_qty_based_on_material_transfer` to decide how much of each raw material was consumed. The `backflush_based_on` setting (Buying Settings) chooses between BOM-based and transferred-qty-based consumption. - **Supporting helpers:** `set_available_qty_for_consumption`, `validate_bom_required_qty`, `set_batch_for_supplied_items`, `__set_serial_and_batch_bundle` and `__set_batch_nos` handle availability, batches and serial numbers. - **Valuation rate:** `set_valuation_rate_for_rm`, `update_rate_for_supplied_items` and `set_rate_for_supplied_items` set the raw material rate. - **Secondary items:** `get_secondary_items` handles scrap and by-products. ### 4. Cost roll-up (`SubcontractingReceipt.calculate_items_qty_and_amount`) 1. Each supplied item gets `amount = consumed_qty * rate`. These are summed per finished-item row (`reference_name`). 2. That sum becomes `rm_supp_cost` on the finished-good row, and `rm_cost_per_qty = rm_supp_cost / (received_qty or qty)`. 3. Secondary or scrap item values become `secondary_items_cost_per_qty`. 4. `item.rate = rm_cost_per_qty + service_cost_per_qty + additional_cost_per_qty + landed-cost-voucher cost per qty`. 5. If the item has a BOM, `received_qty = qty + rejected_qty + process_loss_qty`. The amount is scaled by the BOM's `cost_allocation_per`, and the rate is recomputed from it. 6. Otherwise `qty = received_qty - process_loss_qty` and `amount = qty * rate`. 7. `total_qty` and `total` are summed over the rows. Related cost helpers in the controller are `calculate_additional_costs` and `get_supplied_items_cost`. ### 5. On submit and cancel: stock ledger, GL and order reconciliation `SubcontractingReceipt.on_submit` and `on_cancel` do the following: - `validate_closed_subcontracting_order` and `validate_bom_required_qty` run first (the second only on submit). - `update_status_updater_args` and `update_prevdoc_status` update the received quantity on the SCO and PO. - `set_consumed_qty_in_subcontract_order` writes `consumed_qty` back to the SCO supplied items. It uses `__update_consumed_materials` and `__update_consumed_qty_in_subcontract_order`. - `update_stock_reservation_entries` updates reservations. - `update_stock_ledger` (controller version) runs `update_ordered_and_reserved_qty`, which calls the SCO's `update_ordered_qty_for_subcontracting` and `update_reserved_qty_for_subcontracting`. - It books the finished good into the warehouse at `item.rate`, with `recalculate_rate`. - It books rejected quantities into the rejected warehouse. - `make_sl_entries_for_supplier_warehouse` issues negative stock for the `consumed_qty` of each raw material from `supplier_warehouse`, with `dependant_sle_voucher_detail_no = reference_name`. - `make_gl_entries`, via `get_gl_entries`, posts the valuation and expense accounts. - `repost_future_sle_and_gle` reposts later entries. - `update_status` sets the receipt status. - `set_subcontracting_order_status` updates the SCO status (Partially Received, Completed, and so on). - `auto_create_purchase_receipt` and `update_job_card` finish the submit. The pending quantity helpers are `__get_pending_qty_to_receive` and `get_pending_subcontracted_quantity` in the controller.

final answer
Raw materials move from the company warehouse to the supplier, and consumption and costing happen when the Subcontracting Receipt (SCR) is submitted. I read the code paths below, but I did not run any of it. ## 1. Order and raw-material requirement (Subcontracting Order) - **Doctype class:** `SubcontractingOrder` in `erpnext/subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_order/subcontracting_order.py`. It extends `SubcontractingController` and holds the `supplied_items` table (Subcontracting Order Supplied Item). - **Raw-material lines:** `SubcontractingController.create_raw_materials_supplied_or_received` and `set_materials_for_subcontracted_items` build them. The helpers are: - `_get_materials_from_bom` explodes the BOM. - `__set_supplied_or_received_items` and `__add_supplied_or_received_item` create each row. - `__get_qty_based_on_material_transfer` is used when Buying Settings backflushes by "Material Transferred for Subcontract". The other mode is "BOM". - `set_rate_for_supplied_items` and `update_rate_for_supplied_items` set the rate. - **Reserved stock:** `update_ordered_and_reserved_qty` and `update_reserved_qty_for_subcontracting` keep the Bin's reserved quantity for subcontracting up to date. `SubcontractingOrder.reserve_raw_materials` creates Stock Reservation Entries when `reserve_stock` is set. - **Transfer buttons:** `make_rm_stock_entry` is whitelisted and builds a Stock Entry in `erpnext/controllers/subcontracting_controller.py`. `add_items_in_ste` adds its rows. `get_pending_subcontracted_quantity` supports it. ## 2. Transfer to the supplier (Stock Entry, purpose "Send to Subcontractor") - **Draft creation:** `make_rm_stock_entry` sets the purpose to "Send to Subcontractor" and links `subcontracting_order` (or `purchase_order`). It copies the order's `supplied_items` rows into the entry. It uses `sco_rm_detail` or `po_detail` to link each row back to its source row, and it honours `over_transfer_allowance`. - **Validation:** `SendToSubcontractorStockEntry` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/services/subcontracting.py` handles this. - `validate_subcontract_order` branches on the backflush setting. - `validate_subcontracting_order_for_bom`, `_get_required_qty_for_bom` and `_validate_transfer_qty` throw if more is sent than the order requires plus the allowance. - `get_total_supplied_qty` and `get_total_returned_qty` sum earlier transfers and returns. - `validate_subcontracting_order_for_transfer` and `get_order_rm_detail` find the order row for each item. - **Tracking on the order:** `update_subcontract_order_supplied_items` runs on submit and cancel. - It calls `_get_order_supplied_items` and `_get_supplied_items_details`. - `_update_supplied_items_in_order` writes `supplied_qty`, `returned_qty` and `total_supplied_qty` to the order's supplied-items rows. - `_update_reserved_qty_for_subcontracting` refreshes the Bin through `get_bin(...).update_reserved_qty_for_sub_contracting()`. - **Hook-up:** `MaterialTransferStockEntry.on_submit` and `on_cancel` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/services/material_transfer.py` call the update through `update_subcontract_order_supplied_items`. Returns come through the same Material Transfer path with `is_return` set. They are created by `make_return_stock_entry_for_subcontract`. - **Stock Entry side effects:** `StockEntry.reserve_stock_for_subcontracting` and `update_wo_reservation_for_subcontracting` are in `stock_entry.py`. The second calls `WorkOrderStockReservation.release_reserved_qty_for_subcontract_transfer`, which uses `_subcontract_transferred_qty_by_item`. All three are in `erpnext/manufacturing/doctype/work_order/services/reservation.py`. - **Supplier warehouse:** the stock ends up in the order's `supplier_warehouse`. ## 3. Subcontracting Receipt (finished goods in) - **Creation:** `make_subcontracting_receipt` in `subcontracting_order.py` creates the SCR from the order. - **Doctype class:** `SubcontractingReceipt` in `erpnext/subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_receipt/subcontracting_receipt.py`. It has `items` (Subcontracting Receipt Item) and `supplied_items` (`SubcontractingReceiptSuppliedItem`), plus the `additional_costs` table. - **Order progress:** its `status_updater` increments `received_qty` on Subcontracting Order Item through `update_status_updater_args` and `update_prevdoc_status`. - **Choosing consumption:** - `SubcontractingController.get_available_materials` uses `__get_transferred_items`, `__get_received_items` and `__get_consumed_items`. - `__update_consumed_materials` works out what the supplier still holds. - In `SubcontractingReceipt`, `reset_supplied_items`, `set_available_qty_for_consumption` and `get_current_stock` prepare the rows. - `validate_bom_required_qty` and `get_rm_wise_consumed_qty` check that consumption is not below the BOM requirement. - **Setup of cost-related fields on validate:** `set_valuation_rate_for_rm` and `set_rate_for_supplied_items` rate each supplied row from the supplier warehouse's valuation. The cost-related setters are `set_supplied_items_expense_account`, `set_supplied_items_cost_center` and `set_supplied_items_inventory_dimensions`. `set_service_expense_account` and `set_expense_account_for_subcontracted_items` fill the item-side expense accounts. - **Reconciling back to the order:** on submit and cancel, `set_consumed_qty_in_subcontract_order` calls `__update_consumed_qty_in_subcontract_order`. That writes `consumed_qty` to the order's supplied-items rows, capped at `supplied_qty`. `set_subcontracting_order_status` then refreshes the order status through `update_status`. ## 4. Cost roll-up - **Per-line roll-up:** `SubcontractingReceipt.calculate_items_qty_and_amount` does this. - Each supplied row gets `amount = consumed_qty × rate`. - These amounts are summed per FG row through `reference_name` into `rm_supp_cost`, then divided by quantity to get `rm_cost_per_qty`. - Secondary or scrap items are rolled in as `secondary_items_cost_per_qty`. They come from `get_secondary_items`, `validate_secondary_items` and `remove_secondary_items`. - The FG `rate` is `rm_cost_per_qty + service_cost_per_qty + additional_cost_per_qty + landed-cost-voucher cost per qty`. `get_supplied_items_cost` in the controller offers an equivalent per-row sum. - If the row has a BOM, `amount` is scaled by the BOM's `cost_allocation_per`. - Quantity and `total_qty` are then recalculated. - **Additional costs:** `SubcontractingController.calculate_additional_costs` totals the `additional_costs` table. They are distributed per item by the "Qty" or "Amount" setting, which becomes `additional_cost_per_qty`. - **Service cost:** `service_cost_per_qty` is copied from the order item's service rate. - **Landed cost:** the Landed Cost Voucher flow can re-enter through `update_stock_ledger(via_landed_cost_voucher=True)`. ## 5. Stock and GL posting on SCR submit - **Order of steps in `on_submit`:** `update_stock_reservation_entries`, `update_stock_ledger`, `make_gl_entries`, `repost_future_sle_and_gle`, `update_status`, `auto_create_purchase_receipt`, then `update_job_card`. - **`SubcontractingController.update_stock_ledger`:** - FG rows get a positive Stock Ledger Entry in the accepted warehouse with `incoming_rate = item.rate` and `recalculate_rate = 1`. - Rejected quantity goes to `rejected_warehouse` at a rate of 0. - `make_sl_entries_for_supplier_warehouse` posts negative `consumed_qty` entries for each raw material out of `supplier_warehouse`. It sets `dependant_sle_voucher_detail_no = reference_name`, so a later valuation repost of the raw materials updates the FG cost. - **GL entries:** `SubcontractingReceiptGLComposer._make_item_gl_entries` in `erpnext/subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_receipt/services/gl_composer.py`, called from `compose` and reached through `SubcontractingReceipt.get_gl_entries`. - It debits the FG inventory account by the SLE `stock_value_difference`. - It credits the item `expense_account` by that difference minus `service_cost_per_qty × qty`. - It credits the service expense account (`service_expense_account`, falling back to `expense_account`) by the service cost. - It also books the supplied-item amounts, additional costs and the rate-difference entries. I only saw the first part of that function, so I haven't confirmed those entries in detail. - **Test:** `test_supplied_items_cost_after_reposting` in `test_subcontracting_receipt.py` checks that a backdated Stock Reconciliation updates `supplied_items.rate` on a submitted SCR. ## 6. Cancel and return - **Cancel:** `SubcontractingReceipt.on_cancel` reverses the same steps: consumed quantity, order status, stock ledger and GL. - **Return:** a return SCR (`is_return`) puts the raw materials back into the supplier warehouse. `incoming_rate` is `item.rate` instead of 0 in `make_sl_entries_for_supplier_warehouse`. - **Unused raw materials:** `get_materials_from_supplier` in `subcontracting_controller.py` creates a return Stock Entry for raw materials left at the supplier. `make_return_stock_entry_for_subcontract` is the helper for returns against the order.

final answer
The raw-material flow runs in four stages: transfer, tracking on the order, receipt, and cost roll-up. I read the key functions in `subcontracting_controller.py`, `subcontracting_receipt.py`, `stock_entry.py` and the stock-entry subcontracting service. I did not read `subcontracting_order.py` in full. The line ranges below for the order's supplied-item validation and status handling come from a function-name search, so treat the order-side details as less verified. **1. Creating the order** - The `Subcontracting Order` doctype is in `erpnext/subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_order/subcontracting_order.py`. - It is built from a Purchase Order with a service item. Its `validate_purchase_order_for_subcontracting`, `validate_service_items` and `validate_supplied_items` methods check the source data. - `SubcontractingController` is in `erpnext/controllers/subcontracting_controller.py` and extends `StockController`. Both the order and the receipt inherit from it. - The controller works out the raw materials needed for each finished item: - `_get_materials_from_bom` reads the BOM. - `__add_supplied_or_received_item` adds a supplied-item row. - `create_raw_materials_supplied_or_received` and `set_materials_for_subcontracted_items` fill the `supplied_items` table. - `set_valuation_rate_for_rm` and `set_rate_for_supplied_items` set the raw-material rates. **2. Transferring raw materials to the supplier** - `make_rm_stock_entry` in the controller (L1344) builds a Stock Entry with purpose "Send to Subcontractor". It uses `add_items_in_ste`, and each row links back to the order's raw-material row through `sco_rm_detail`. - `get_items_from_subcontract_order` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/services/subcontracting.py` (L253) also fetches order items for a Stock Entry. - `StockEntry` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/stock_entry.py` hands the "Send to Subcontractor" purpose to `SendToSubcontractorStockEntry`. That class is in `services/subcontracting.py`: - `validate_subcontract_order` and `validate_subcontracting_order_for_bom` check the transfer against the order and the BOM. - `_validate_transfer_qty` and `get_total_supplied_qty` cap the quantity at what is allowed. - `get_total_returned_qty` nets off returns. - `on_submit` calls `update_subcontract_order_supplied_items`, which calls `_update_supplied_items_in_order`. That writes `supplied_qty` back to the order's "Subcontracting Order Supplied Item" rows. - `_update_reserved_qty_for_subcontracting` updates reserved quantity. - In `StockEntry`: - `update_subcontracting_order_status` refreshes the order status. - `reserve_stock_for_subcontracting` calls `SubcontractingOrder.reserve_raw_materials` when the order has `reserve_stock` set. - `update_wo_reservation_for_subcontracting` releases the Work Order's own reservation. - `validate_closed_subcontracting_order` blocks transfers against a closed order. - Returning unused material uses `make_return_stock_entry_for_subcontract` and `get_materials_from_supplier` in the controller. **3. Tracking against the order** - `SubcontractingOrder.update_ordered_qty_for_subcontracting` and `update_reserved_qty_for_subcontracting` maintain the Bin quantities. - `SubcontractingOrder.update_status` sets the order status from received quantities and transferred materials. `get_pending_subcontracted_quantity` in the controller reports what is still pending. **4. Receiving finished goods and reconciling** - `make_subcontracting_receipt` (L441) and `get_mapped_subcontracting_receipt` (L446) in `subcontracting_order.py` create the `SubcontractingReceipt`. - `SubcontractingReceipt.get_available_materials` in the controller works out what the supplier holds. It uses `__get_transferred_items`, `__get_received_items` and `__get_consumed_items`, and so what was transferred, what came back and what was consumed. - The receipt is in `erpnext/subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_receipt/subcontracting_receipt.py`: - `before_validate` and `validate` call `set_items_bom` and `reset_supplied_items`. - Consumption is set up by `set_available_qty_for_consumption`, `set_batch_for_supplied_items` and `set_rate_for_supplied_items`. - `validate_bom_required_qty` checks that consumed raw materials match the BOM requirement. - `on_submit` runs `update_status_updater_args`, then `update_prevdoc_status`, `set_subcontracting_order_status` and `set_consumed_qty_in_subcontract_order`. It then runs `update_stock_ledger`, `make_gl_entries`, `update_status`, `auto_create_purchase_receipt` and `update_job_card`. - `set_consumed_qty_in_subcontract_order` calls `__update_consumed_qty_in_subcontract_order`, which writes `consumed_qty` back to the order's supplied items. - Stock ledger posting happens in the controller's `update_stock_ledger`: - Finished goods go in with `incoming_rate = item.rate` and `recalculate_rate` set. - `make_sl_entries_for_supplier_warehouse` takes the consumed raw materials out of `supplier_warehouse` with a negative `actual_qty`. - `update_ordered_and_reserved_qty` is called first. **Cost roll-up** - The roll-up is in `SubcontractingReceipt.calculate_items_qty_and_amount`. - Each supplied item's `amount` is `consumed_qty × rate`, summed per finished-item row into `rm_supp_cost`. - From that it computes `rm_cost_per_qty = rm_supp_cost / (received_qty or qty)`. - Secondary or scrap items are costed into `secondary_items_cost_per_qty`. - The finished item's `rate` is `rm_cost_per_qty + service_cost_per_qty + additional_cost_per_qty + landed-cost-voucher per qty`. - When the item has a BOM, the amount is scaled by the BOM's `cost_allocation_per`. Otherwise `qty` is `received_qty` less `process_loss_qty`. - `calculate_additional_costs` in the controller spreads the receipt's additional costs over the finished items. It distributes by Amount or by Qty, depending on `distribute_additional_costs_based_on`. - `get_supplied_items_cost` returns the raw-material cost for a given item row. - `get_gl_entries` and `make_gl_entries` post the accounting. The expense accounts and cost centers on the supplied items come from `set_supplied_items_expense_account` and `set_supplied_items_cost_center`.

final answer
Here is the flow, stage by stage. I read the source for the first stage, the transfer validation, the order-side update, the cost methods and the receipt's `on_submit`. I did not open the bodies of a few methods, which I name below. For those I'm going on their names and call sites, so treat their descriptions as inferred. ## 1. Raw materials leave the company warehouse (Stock Entry, "Send to Subcontractor") - **`make_rm_stock_entry()`** in `erpnext/controllers/subcontracting_controller.py:1344` is a whitelisted function. - It loads the submitted Subcontracting Order (or Purchase Order, via `order_doctype`) and takes its `supplied_items`, or a user-picked subset. - It uses `get_mapped_doc` with a `post_process` that sets `purpose = "Send to Subcontractor"` and the `subcontracting_order` link, then calls `set_stock_entry_type()`. - For each raw material row it works out the qty still to send: `required_qty - total_supplied_qty`. It skips rows already transferred beyond `Buying Settings.over_transfer_allowance`. - Each Stock Entry row gets `s_warehouse` set to the row's `warehouse` or `reserve_warehouse`, and `t_warehouse` set to the order's `supplier_warehouse`. - Each row also carries `sco_rm_detail` (the link back to the order's supplied-item row), `main_item_code`, `subcontracted_item`, and any serial/batch data. - The "Create → Material to Supplier" button in `subcontracting_order.js` calls this function. - **`SendToSubcontractorStockEntry`** (extends `BaseStockEntry`) is in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/services/subcontracting.py`. `_configure_purpose_class` in `stock_entry.py:196` picks it for this purpose. - `validate()` calls `validate_subcontract_order()`. That branches on `Buying Settings.backflush_raw_materials_of_subcontract_based_on`. - In the "BOM" branch, `validate_subcontracting_order_for_bom` runs. It calls `_get_required_qty_for_bom` (including alternative items), `_validate_transfer_qty` (using `get_total_supplied_qty` and `get_total_returned_qty`) and `_link_rm_detail_if_missing`. `_link_rm_detail_if_missing` uses `get_order_rm_detail`. - In the "Material Transferred for Subcontract" branch, `validate_subcontracting_order_for_transfer` requires `subcontracted_item` and links `sco_rm_detail`. - The normal Stock Entry stock-ledger posting moves the stock into the supplier warehouse (`update_stock_ledger` in `stock_entry.py`). ## 2. Tracking against the Subcontracting Order - `on_submit` and `on_cancel` in the same service call `update_subcontract_order_supplied_items()`. That runs three steps: 1. **`_get_order_supplied_items`** reads the order's supplied rows. 2. **`_get_supplied_items_details`** calls the module function `get_supplied_items()`. It aggregates `supplied_qty`, `returned_qty` and `total_supplied_qty`. 3. **`_update_supplied_items_in_order`** writes those totals to the **Subcontracting Order Supplied Item** rows with `frappe.db.set_value`. - It then calls **`_update_reserved_qty_for_subcontracting`**. That calls `Bin.update_reserved_qty_for_sub_contracting()` on each reserve warehouse. - `material_transfer.py` also has `update_subcontract_order_supplied_items` (line 166) for returns of material. - The order itself is `SubcontractingOrder` (`erpnext/subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_order/subcontracting_order.py`, extends `SubcontractingController`). - Its supplied-items table uses the child doctype `SubcontractingOrderSuppliedItem`. - Its `validate` builds that table through `SubcontractingController.validate()`, which calls `create_raw_materials_supplied_or_received("supplied_items")`. I didn't read that method's body. - `calculate_items_qty_and_amount` and `set_missing_values` (which uses `calculate_additional_costs`) are also on the order. `update_status` and `set_subcontracting_order_status` refresh the status and the Bin. - `make_subcontracting_receipt` (whitelisted, called from `subcontracting_order.js`) maps the order to a receipt. ## 3. Receipt, consumption and cost roll-up (Subcontracting Receipt) `SubcontractingReceipt` is in `erpnext/subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_receipt/subcontracting_receipt.py`. It extends `SubcontractingController`, with child doctype `SubcontractingReceiptItem`. **Finding what is available at the supplier** - `SubcontractingController.initialized_fields` and `__get_backflush_based_on` choose between BOM and transferred-material backflushing. - `__get_subcontract_orders` and `__get_pending_qty_to_receive` collect the linked orders and the qty still to receive. - `__get_transferred_items` queries submitted "Send to Subcontractor" Stock Entry rows. `get_available_materials()` builds the pool of material at the supplier, keyed by `(rm_item, fg_item, order)`, including serial and batch data from bundles. - `__get_received_items`, `__get_consumed_items` and `__update_consumed_materials` deduct what earlier receipts already consumed. - `__identify_change_in_item_table` detects changed item rows. **Building the Supplied Items on the receipt** - `validate()` calls `validate_items()` and `create_raw_materials_supplied_or_received("supplied_items")`. It then calls `set_valuation_rate_for_rm()`. - The receipt's `supplied_items` rows (Subcontracting Receipt Supplied Item) get consumed qty, rate and amount. - `set_valuation_rate_for_rm` re-prices each row. It calls `stock.utils.get_incoming_rate()` against the `supplier_warehouse` with the negative consumed qty. If the rate changed, it calls `calculate_items_qty_and_amount()`. - `validate_rejected_warehouse` checks rejected-qty warehouses. **Cost roll-up** - **`get_supplied_items_cost(item_row_id)`** (`subcontracting_controller.py:1223`) sums `consumed_qty × rate` for the supplied rows whose `reference_name` matches the finished-item row. - **`calculate_additional_costs()`** (`subcontracting_controller.py:1243`) totals the `additional_costs` table. It spreads the total over the finished items, by Amount or by Qty depending on `distribute_additional_costs_based_on`, as `additional_cost_per_qty`. It skips secondary and scrap items. - `SubcontractingReceipt.calculate_items_qty_and_amount` (line 496) and `set_missing_values` (line 455) combine these into the finished item's rate: the service cost, plus the consumed raw-material cost, plus the additional cost. I didn't read those bodies, so that is inferred from the names and call sites. `get_secondary_items` (line 377) also calls `calculate_additional_costs`. - Landed Cost Vouchers can push further cost onto the receipt (`via_landed_cost_voucher`, seen near line 1220 of the controller). **`on_submit` order of operations** 1. `validate_closed_subcontracting_order` 2. `validate_bom_required_qty` 3. `update_status_updater_args` 4. `update_prevdoc_status` 5. `set_subcontracting_order_status(update_bin=False)`. This calls `SubcontractingOrder.update_status`, which updates the received qty and status. 6. `set_consumed_qty_in_subcontract_order` 7. `make_bundle_using_old_serial_batch_fields` for `items` and `supplied_items` 8. `update_stock_reservation_entries` 9. `update_stock_ledger`. This is the stock ledger posting. It receives the finished goods into the item warehouse and consumes the raw materials out of the supplier warehouse. 10. `make_gl_entries` 11. `repost_future_sle_and_gle` 12. `update_status` 13. `auto_create_purchase_receipt` 14. `update_job_card` `on_update` calls `set_serial_and_batch_bundle` for `items` and `supplied_items`. **Cancel** `on_cancel` reverses these steps. It also calls `set_subcontracting_order_status`. ## End-to-end summary 1. Subcontracting Order submitted → `supplied_items` computed from the BOM. 2. `make_rm_stock_entry` → Stock Entry "Send to Subcontractor" → `SendToSubcontractorStockEntry` validates it and moves stock to the supplier warehouse. 3. Stock Entry submit → `update_subcontract_order_supplied_items` writes `supplied_qty`, `returned_qty` and `total_supplied_qty` to the order's Supplied Item rows and updates reserved qty in the Bin. 4. Order `make_subcontracting_receipt` → receipt. 5. `SubcontractingController.get_available_materials` plus `create_raw_materials_supplied_or_received` produce the receipt's `supplied_items`. `set_valuation_rate_for_rm`, `get_supplied_items_cost` and `calculate_additional_costs` roll up the cost. 6. Receipt `on_submit` posts the stock ledger and GL entries and updates the order's status and consumed qty.

final answer
A Landed Cost Voucher (LCV) works in two stages. It first splits the charges across its own item rows. Then, on submit, it rewrites the already-submitted receipts' item amounts, valuation rates, stock ledger entries and GL entries. I read the code but did not run it. ## 1. Distributing charges across items This happens in `erpnext/stock/doctype/landed_cost_voucher/landed_cost_voucher.py`. - **Pulling the rows in.** `get_items_from_purchase_receipts` loads the receipt line items into the voucher's `items` table (Landed Cost Item). It uses the helper `get_pr_items` to do this. - **Totalling the charges.** `set_total_taxes_and_charges` sums `base_amount` over the `taxes` table. That table holds the freight, customs and similar rows, each with an expense account. - **Splitting the total.** `set_applicable_charges_on_item` does the proportional split. - It uses the field named by `distribute_charges_based_on`, which can be Qty, Amount or Distribute Manually. The field name comes from `frappe.scrub`. - For each row, `applicable_charges = item.<field> × total_taxes_and_charges / Σ<field>`. - It adds any rounding remainder to the last row. - It throws an error if the total is zero. - **Manual distribution.** If the user chose "Distribute Manually", the user's own `applicable_charges` values are kept as entered. - **Checking on submit.** `validate_applicable_charges_for_item` runs in `on_submit`. It checks that the item charges add up to `total_taxes_and_charges`. A difference within rounding tolerance is added to the last row. A larger difference throws an error. ## 2. Retroactively updating the submitted receipts `on_submit` calls `update_landed_cost`, and `on_cancel` calls it too. `update_landed_cost` has two loops over `purchase_receipts`. **Loop 1 (re-valuation):** 1. It loads each receipt document. The type can be Purchase Receipt, Purchase Invoice, Subcontracting Receipt or Stock Entry. 2. It calls `validate_asset_qty_and_status` for fixed-asset items. 3. It calls `set_landed_cost_voucher_amount(doc)`. For each receipt item, this sums `applicable_charges` from all submitted (`docstatus == 1`) Landed Cost Items. It matches them on `purchase_receipt_item` (or `stock_entry_item` for a Stock Entry). The sum goes into `item.landed_cost_voucher_amount`. Because it sums every submitted LCV, cancelling one removes its share. 4. It calls `doc.update_valuation_rate(reset_outgoing_rate=False)`, defined in `erpnext/controllers/buying_controller.py`. This recomputes the rate as `(base_net_amount + item_tax_amount + landed_cost_voucher_amount + amount_difference_with_purchase_invoice) / (qty × conversion_factor)`. Subcontracting Receipts use `calculate_items_qty_and_amount` instead. 5. It saves the result with `item.db_update()`, without re-submitting the receipt. 6. It calls `update_rate_in_serial_no_for_non_asset_items`, which writes the new rate to `Serial No.purchase_rate`. **Loop 2 (re-posting):** 1. It sets `doc.docstatus = 2` (temporarily), then calls `update_stock_ledger(allow_negative_stock=True, via_landed_cost_voucher=True)` and `make_gl_entries_on_cancel()`. This reverses the old Stock Ledger Entries and GL entries. 2. It sets `docstatus = 1` again and calls `make_bundle_using_old_serial_batch_fields(via_landed_cost_voucher=True)`. 3. It calls `update_stock_ledger(...)` again, so the new SLEs carry the new valuation rate. 4. It calls `make_gl_entries(via_landed_cost_voucher=True)` for a Purchase Receipt, and plain `make_gl_entries()` for the other types. 5. It calls `repost_future_sle_and_gle(via_landed_cost_voucher=True)`. This reposts later stock and GL entries that depend on this receipt's valuation. The mechanism lives in `StockController` (`erpnext/controllers/stock_controller.py`) and its `StockLedgerService`. ## 3. How the GL postings include the charges The Purchase Receipt GL entries are built in `erpnext/stock/doctype/purchase_receipt/services/gl_composer.py`, in `PurchaseReceiptGLComposer.compose`. - The stock-in-hand debit includes `base_net_amount + item_tax_amount + landed_cost_voucher_amount`. - The inner function `make_landed_cost_gl_entries` credits the LCV expense accounts for each item. - It gets the per-account amounts from `get_item_account_wise_lcv_entries` in `landed_cost_voucher.py`. That function reads every submitted LCV for the receipt. It splits each tax account's amount across items in proportion to the same distribution basis. - Fixed-asset items with a landed cost amount get their own handling. The `via_landed_cost_voucher` flag is passed down through `StockController.make_gl_entries`, `make_sl_entries`, `repost_future_sle_and_gle` and `PurchaseReceiptGLComposer._make_tax_gl_entries`. That flag is how the repost logic knows the entries come from an LCV.

final answer
A Landed Cost Voucher (LCV) first splits its charges across the receipt's items. On submit it then writes each item's share back onto the already-submitted receipt, recomputes valuation rates, and cancels and reposts the stock and GL entries in place. I read the code paths below. I didn't run any of it. The core file is `erpnext/stock/doctype/landed_cost_voucher/landed_cost_voucher.py`, class `LandedCostVoucher`. ## 1. Distributing the charges across line items - **Loading items:** `get_items_from_purchase_receipts()` runs when the items table is empty. It calls the module function `get_pr_items()`, which queries each linked receipt's stock or fixed-asset item rows. It copies `qty`, `rate`, `amount` (base currency) and the row link (`purchase_receipt_item`, or `stock_entry_item` for a Stock Entry) into the voucher's `Landed Cost Item` rows. It handles Purchase Receipt, Purchase Invoice with stock update, Subcontracting Receipt and Stock Entry. - **Totalling the charges:** `validate()` calls `init_landed_taxes_and_totals()` and then `set_total_taxes_and_charges()`. The total is the sum of `base_amount` across the `taxes` table (freight, customs and similar). - **Splitting by basis:** `set_applicable_charges_on_item()` does the split. The basis comes from `distribute_charges_based_on`, which is Qty, Amount or Distribute Manually. For Qty or Amount, each item gets `item.applicable_charges = item.<basis> × total_taxes_and_charges / Σ<basis>`, rounded to field precision. Any rounding difference goes onto the last item. Manual mode leaves the user-entered values alone. If the total basis is zero, it throws an error. - **Submit check:** `validate_applicable_charges_for_item()` runs from `on_submit`. It checks that the item charges sum to `total_taxes_and_charges`. A difference under 2 units of the smallest precision step is added to the last row. Anything larger throws an error. ## 2. Retroactively updating already-submitted receipts `on_submit()` and `on_cancel()` both call `update_landed_cost()`. It works in two passes over `purchase_receipts`: **Pass 1: valuation rate** - `validate_asset_qty_and_status()` checks that assets exist for fixed-asset items and that none are submitted yet. - `set_landed_cost_voucher_amount(doc)` re-sums `Landed Cost Item.applicable_charges` from all submitted LCVs for each receipt item. It writes the result to `item.landed_cost_voucher_amount`. Because it sums every submitted LCV, cancelling one lowers the total correctly. - `doc.update_valuation_rate(reset_outgoing_rate=False)` is in `erpnext/controllers/buying_controller.py`. It recomputes `item.valuation_rate = (base_net_amount + item_tax_amount + landed_cost_voucher_amount + amount_difference_with_purchase_invoice) / qty_in_stock_uom`. Subcontracting Receipts use `calculate_items_qty_and_amount()` instead. - `item.db_update()` saves the changed rows directly to the database. The receipt stays submitted and is not amended. - `update_rate_in_serial_no_for_non_asset_items()` sets `Serial No.purchase_rate` to the new valuation rate. Stock Entry and Subcontracting Receipt are skipped. **Pass 2: stock ledger and GL, re-posted in place** - The receipt is temporarily marked `docstatus = 2`. `update_stock_ledger(allow_negative_stock=True, via_landed_cost_voucher=True)` then reverses the old Stock Ledger Entries, and `make_gl_entries_on_cancel()` reverses the old GL entries. - The receipt is then set back to `docstatus = 1`. The next calls are: - `make_bundle_using_old_serial_batch_fields(via_landed_cost_voucher=True)` - `update_stock_ledger(...)`, which creates new SLEs at the updated valuation rate - `make_gl_entries(via_landed_cost_voucher=True)`, or plain `make_gl_entries()` for other receipt types - `repost_future_sle_and_gle(via_landed_cost_voucher=True)`, which re-values later transactions - The `via_landed_cost_voucher` flag is passed down through `StockController.make_sl_entries`, `stock_ledger.make_sl_entries` and `make_entry`. It is stored on the SLE so that negative-stock and serial/batch checks don't block the repost. - `StockLedgerService.repost_future_sle_and_gle` creates a Repost Item Valuation record, which carries the same flag. ## 3. How the GL picks up the charges - `PurchaseReceipt.get_gl_entries()` delegates to `PurchaseReceiptGLComposer.compose()` in `stock/doctype/purchase_receipt/services/gl_composer.py`. - That composer uses `get_item_account_wise_lcv_entries(doc)` from `landed_cost_voucher.py`. For each submitted LCV on the receipt, it splits every taxes-row amount across items by the same basis. It returns a map of `(item_code, item row) → expense_account → amount`. - The composer credits each LCV expense account and debits stock-in-hand (inventory) by the item's share. ## 4. Cancelling a voucher `on_cancel()` runs the same `update_landed_cost()`. Since `set_landed_cost_voucher_amount` only counts LCVs with `docstatus == 1`, the receipt's valuation and postings revert to what they were without that voucher.

final answer
A Landed Cost Voucher (LCV) spreads its charges across items in `LandedCostVoucher.set_applicable_charges_on_item`. It then updates the already-submitted receipts in `LandedCostVoucher.update_landed_cost`, which runs on submit. This is from reading the code; I haven't run it. I also didn't open `make_gl_entries` or `repost_future_sle_and_gle`, so the GL step below is based on the calls and the in-code comments. All of this is in `erpnext/stock/doctype/landed_cost_voucher/landed_cost_voucher.py` unless noted. **1. Distributing the charges across items** - `get_items_from_purchase_receipts` fills the LCV's `items` table (Landed Cost Item rows) from the linked receipts. The receipts are listed in the `purchase_receipts` table (Landed Cost Purchase Receipt). - `set_total_taxes_and_charges` sums the `taxes` rows (Landed Cost Taxes and Charges) into `total_taxes_and_charges`. - `set_applicable_charges_on_item` runs unless "Distribute Charges Based On" is "Distribute Manually". - It totals the chosen field across the items: qty, amount, or similar. - Each item gets `applicable_charges = item_value × total_taxes_and_charges / total_item_cost`. - Any rounding difference goes onto the last item. - It throws an error if the total is zero. - `validate_applicable_charges_for_item` runs on submit. It checks that the item charges add up to `total_taxes_and_charges`, within a rounding tolerance. For manual distribution it uses the entered amounts as the proportions. **2. Updating the receipts that were already submitted** - `on_submit` calls `validate_applicable_charges_for_item`, then `update_landed_cost`, then `update_claimed_landed_cost`. `on_cancel` repeats the last two steps to undo the effect. - `update_landed_cost` has two passes over each receipt in `purchase_receipts`. - **Pass 1 (rate):** - It loads the receipt with `frappe.get_doc`. This can be a Purchase Receipt, Purchase Invoice, Subcontracting Receipt or Stock Entry. - It calls the module function `set_landed_cost_voucher_amount(doc)`. For each receipt item, that sums `applicable_charges` from submitted Landed Cost Item rows. It matches on `receipt_document` and on `purchase_receipt_item` (or `stock_entry_item`), and stores the sum in `item.landed_cost_voucher_amount`. - It then calls `BuyingController.update_valuation_rate(reset_outgoing_rate=False)` in `erpnext/controllers/buying_controller.py`. This recomputes `item.valuation_rate = (base_net_amount + item_tax_amount + landed_cost_voucher_amount + amount_difference_with_purchase_invoice) / (qty × conversion_factor)`. Subcontracting Receipts use `calculate_items_qty_and_amount` instead. - It saves the changes with `item.db_update()`. - For non-asset items with serial numbers, `update_rate_in_serial_no_for_non_asset_items` writes the new valuation rate to each Serial No's `purchase_rate`. - **Pass 2 (ledgers):** - It sets `docstatus = 2` and calls `update_stock_ledger(allow_negative_stock=True, via_landed_cost_voucher=True)`. It then calls `make_gl_entries_on_cancel()`, which reverses the old stock ledger entries and GL entries. - It sets `docstatus = 1` and calls `make_bundle_using_old_serial_batch_fields(via_landed_cost_voucher=True)`. It then calls `update_stock_ledger(...)` again, which re-posts the stock ledger entries at the new rate. - For a Purchase Receipt it calls `make_gl_entries(via_landed_cost_voucher=True)`; other receipt types call `make_gl_entries()`. The `via_landed_cost_voucher` flag is passed on the Purchase Receipt path. - It calls `repost_future_sle_and_gle(via_landed_cost_voucher=True)`. This reposts later stock and GL entries so downstream valuations pick up the change. **3. How the landed cost reaches the GL** - `get_item_account_wise_lcv_entries(doc)` builds an account-wise map of the landed cost for a receipt, using `has_landed_cost_amount`. It reads the submitted "Landed Cost Purchase Receipt" rows that point at the receipt. The GL builders for the receipt use this map, so the posting includes the expense-account credits and the stock-in-hand debit increase. **Related files** - `erpnext/stock/doctype/landed_cost_purchase_receipt/landed_cost_purchase_receipt.py`: the doctype for the `purchase_receipts` table rows. - `erpnext/controllers/taxes_and_totals.py`: `init_landed_taxes_and_totals` sets up the taxes and totals for the LCV.

final answer
**Overview.** A Landed Cost Voucher (LCV) spreads its charges over the receipt's items when it is saved. When it is submitted, it writes the totals back onto the receipt rows and re-posts the receipt's stock ledger and GL entries. All of this is in `erpnext/stock/doctype/landed_cost_voucher/landed_cost_voucher.py`, class `LandedCostVoucher`. ## 1. Distributing charges across items - **`get_items_from_purchase_receipts()`** builds the LCV `items` table. It calls `get_pr_items` for each receipt in the `purchase_receipts` table. Each row stores `purchase_receipt_item`, or `stock_entry_item` for Stock Entries, plus qty, amount and `receipt_document`. - **`validate()`** runs `set_total_taxes_and_charges()`, which sums the base amount of the `taxes` rows (freight, customs and so on). It then calls `set_applicable_charges_on_item()`. - **`set_applicable_charges_on_item()`** does the actual split, unless `distribute_charges_based_on` is "Distribute Manually". - The basis field is Qty, Amount or Distribute Manually, and the code reads the matching item field via `frappe.scrub`. - Each item gets `applicable_charges = item[basis] * total_taxes_and_charges / total_item_cost`. - Any rounding difference is added to the last item. - **`validate_applicable_charges_for_item()`** runs on submit. It checks that the item charges add up to the total taxes and charges, allowing a rounding tolerance that it folds into the last row. This also covers manual distribution. ## 2. Retroactively updating already-submitted receipts `on_submit()` calls `validate_applicable_charges_for_item()`, then `update_landed_cost()`, then `update_claimed_landed_cost()`. `on_cancel()` runs `update_landed_cost()` again. Because cancellation reruns the same recomputation, it reverses the charges. `update_landed_cost()` works in two loops. **Loop 1: recompute the receipt's valuation fields.** For each receipt in `purchase_receipts`: 1. It loads the receipt document with `frappe.get_doc`. 2. It calls `validate_asset_qty_and_status` unless the LCV is cancelled. 3. **`set_landed_cost_voucher_amount(doc)`** (module-level function) sums `applicable_charges` from submitted (`docstatus=1`) `Landed Cost Item` rows. It matches on `receipt_document` and `purchase_receipt_item` (or `stock_entry_item`). The result goes into each receipt item's `landed_cost_voucher_amount`. Cancelled LCVs drop out of the sum automatically. 4. `doc.update_valuation_rate(reset_outgoing_rate=False)` is called. For Subcontracting Receipts it calls `calculate_items_qty_and_amount()` instead. - This method is in `erpnext/controllers/buying_controller.py`, around line 414. - It sets `item.valuation_rate = (net_rate + item_tax_amount + landed_cost_voucher_amount + amount_difference_with_purchase_invoice) / qty_in_stock_uom`. 5. `item.db_update()` persists the new values directly on the submitted document. 6. For receipts other than Stock Entry and Subcontracting Receipt, it calls `update_rate_in_serial_no_for_non_asset_items(doc)` to refresh the serial-number rates. **Loop 2: repost the stock ledger and GL.** For each receipt: 1. It sets `doc.docstatus = 2`, then calls `update_stock_ledger(allow_negative_stock=True, via_landed_cost_voucher=True)` and `make_gl_entries_on_cancel()`. This reverses the old stock ledger entries and GL entries. 2. It sets `docstatus = 1` again and calls `make_bundle_using_old_serial_batch_fields(via_landed_cost_voucher=True)` and `update_stock_ledger(..., via_landed_cost_voucher=True)`. This re-creates the stock ledger entries with the new valuation rate. - Within `StockController` in `erpnext/controllers/stock_controller.py`, the call chain is `make_sl_entries` → `StockLedgerService.make_sl_entries` (`erpnext/stock/services/stock_ledger_service.py`) → `erpnext/stock/stock_ledger.py::make_sl_entries`. - That function calls `make_entry`, which sets the `via_landed_cost_voucher` flag on the Stock Ledger Entry. `repost_current_voucher` also reads the flag. - The flag lets `stock_ledger_entry.py` (around line 184) skip validations that would otherwise reject the change to a submitted voucher. 3. For a Purchase Receipt it calls `doc.make_gl_entries(via_landed_cost_voucher=True)`. - `StockController.make_gl_entries` (`stock_controller.py`, line 178) calls `get_gl_entries(inventory_account_map, via_landed_cost_voucher)`. - `PurchaseReceipt.get_gl_entries` (`purchase_receipt.py`, line 478) delegates to `PurchaseReceiptGLComposer.compose` (under `erpnext/accounts/services`). - That composer posts the landed-cost amounts. I did not open it, so I haven't confirmed how it posts them. - Other receipt types call plain `doc.make_gl_entries()`. 4. `doc.repost_future_sle_and_gle(via_landed_cost_voucher=True)` handles later transactions. It goes through `StockLedgerService.repost_future_sle_and_gle`, which creates a `Repost Item Valuation` document with `via_landed_cost_voucher` set. Downstream vouchers then get the corrected incoming rate and their own stock ledger entries and GL entries reposted. The handling is in `erpnext/stock/doctype/repost_item_valuation/repost_item_valuation.py`, around lines 621 and 637. Fixed-asset items are treated differently. The code comment in `update_landed_cost` says the asset rate is updated when the Purchase Invoice or Payment Entry creates the asset GL entries.

final answer
ERPNext restricts these endpoints with the `methods` argument of Frappe's `@frappe.whitelist` decorator. I read this from the decorator usages. I didn't open Frappe's source, so the enforcement description below comes from my knowledge of Frappe. **Mechanism** A state-changing endpoint is declared as `@frappe.whitelist(methods=["POST"])`. Frappe records the allowed HTTP verbs for that function. When a request reaches `/api/method/<dotted.path>`, Frappe checks the request verb against that list. This check happens in `frappe.is_whitelisted` and is called from `frappe.handler`. If the verb isn't listed, Frappe raises a `PermissionError` (HTTP 403) before the function runs. A plain `@frappe.whitelist()` allows GET, POST, PUT and DELETE by default, so declaring `methods=["POST"]` is what blocks GET. This prevents state changes through GET requests, which are easy to trigger from links or images (CSRF-style). Some read-only endpoints are marked `methods=["GET"]` explicitly. Examples are two functions in `bank_reconciliation_tool.py`, at lines 388 and 900. The restriction also appears on guest-accessible endpoints, as `@frappe.whitelist(allow_guest=True, methods=["POST"])`. **Concrete POST-only endpoints** - `erpnext/selling/page/point_of_sale/point_of_sale.py`: `create_opening_voucher` and `set_customer_info`. - `erpnext/selling/doctype/sales_order/mapper.py`: `make_work_orders`. - `erpnext/controllers/stock_controller.py`: `make_quality_inspections`. - `erpnext/controllers/accounts_controller.py`: `update_company_master_and_address`. - `erpnext/manufacturing/doctype/work_order/work_order.py`: `set_work_order_ops`. - `erpnext/accounts/bulk_payment.py`: `create_payment_entries`. - `erpnext/setup/doctype/company/company.py`: `add_node` and `create_transaction_deletion_request`. - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/bank_reconciliation_tool/bank_reconciliation_tool.py`: many functions, for example at lines 119, 149 and 308. - `erpnext/erpnext_integrations/doctype/plaid_settings/plaid_settings.py`: two endpoints, at lines 53 and 82. - `erpnext/subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_receipt/mapper.py` (line 25) and `erpnext/buying/doctype/request_for_quotation/mapper.py` (line 58). - `erpnext/support/doctype/issue/issue.py` and `erpnext/support/doctype/service_level_agreement/service_level_agreement.py` (line 785). - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/budget/budget.py` (line 881). - Several `bisect_accounting_statements.py` methods, `call_log.py`, and the `Customer` method at `customer.py:206`. - `erpnext/templates/utils.py:11` and `erpnext/www/banking.py:32`, both with `allow_guest=True`. I listed the first 40 grep hits only, so there are probably more endpoints with this restriction than the ones above.

final answer
ERPNext restricts these endpoints with the `methods` argument of Frappe's whitelist decorator. State-changing endpoints are declared as `@frappe.whitelist(methods=["POST"])`. I found this by searching the code. I did not read Frappe's source, so the enforcement description below is from my knowledge of the framework. **How it works** - A plain `@frappe.whitelist()` function can be called through `/api/method/<dotted.path>` with any HTTP verb, including GET. - Passing `methods=[...]` records the allowed verbs for that function. - When a request arrives, Frappe's request handler (`frappe.handler.execute_cmd`) calls `frappe.is_whitelisted(method)`. That check compares `frappe.request.method` against the allowed list. If the verb isn't listed, the request is rejected with a `PermissionError` and an HTTP 403. - A GET to these endpoints is therefore refused before the function body runs. This also blocks state changes through links, images or other cross-site GETs, and cached GETs. - Read-only endpoints are marked the other way, for example `@frappe.whitelist(methods=["GET"])` on two functions in `bank_reconciliation_tool.py`. **Endpoints marked POST-only (file and function)** - `erpnext/controllers/stock_controller.py`: `make_quality_inspections` - `erpnext/controllers/accounts_controller.py`: `update_company_master_and_address` - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/budget/budget.py`: `revise_budget` - `erpnext/projects/doctype/task/task.py`: `set_multiple_status`, `add_node` and `add_multiple_tasks`. `set_multiple_status` calls `task.save()` in a loop. - `erpnext/manufacturing/doctype/work_order/work_order.py`: `set_work_order_ops`, which calls `po.save()`. - `erpnext/stock/doctype/warehouse/warehouse.py`: `add_node` - `erpnext/setup/doctype/company/company.py`: `add_node` and `create_transaction_deletion_request` - `erpnext/setup/doctype/employee/employee.py`: `create_user` - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/bank_reconciliation_tool/bank_reconciliation_tool.py`: many functions, with about ten `methods=["POST"]` decorators - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/bisect_accounting_statements/bisect_accounting_statements.py`: several methods on the `BisectAccountingStatements` class - `erpnext/erpnext_integrations/doctype/plaid_settings/plaid_settings.py`: two endpoints - `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_reposting_settings/stock_reposting_settings.py`: one method on the `StockRepostingSettings` class The restriction isn't applied everywhere. Some functions that create or save documents are still plain `@frappe.whitelist()`. The search also turned up `make_merge_log` in `pos_invoice.py`, which builds an unsaved merge-log document, but I didn't check how it is decorated.
