final answer
ERPNext restricts these endpoints by passing a `methods` argument to Frappe's whitelist decorator. It doesn't use a custom check of its own. I found the pattern by searching the code for the decorator. I didn't open the Frappe framework source, so the enforcement description below comes from how Frappe behaves, not from reading it here. **Mechanism** State-changing endpoints are declared as `@frappe.whitelist(methods=["POST"])`. A plain `@frappe.whitelist()` allows any HTTP verb, including GET. When `methods` is given, Frappe's request handler checks the request verb for `/api/method/<dotted.path>` calls. A GET against a POST-only function is rejected with a 405 "Method Not Allowed" (`frappe.PermissionError`/`NotAllowed`-style error). That check is the CSRF-style protection against the endpoint being triggered by a link, an image tag or a prefetch. Read-only endpoints are sometimes marked `methods=["GET"]` explicitly. Examples are `bank_reconciliation_tool.py` (lines 388 and 900) and `bank_account.py` (lines 140 and 181). **Endpoints with the POST-only restriction** - `erpnext/controllers/accounts_controller.py`: `update_company_master_and_address` - `erpnext/controllers/stock_controller.py`: `make_quality_inspections` - `erpnext/subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_receipt/mapper.py`: `make_purchase_receipt` - `erpnext/manufacturing/doctype/work_order/work_order.py`: `set_work_order_ops` - `erpnext/stock/doctype/material_request/material_request.py`: `raise_work_orders` - `erpnext/stock/doctype/batch/batch.py`: `split_batch` - `erpnext/setup/doctype/company/company.py`: `add_node` and `create_transaction_deletion_request` - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/bank_reconciliation_tool/bank_reconciliation_tool.py`: many functions, including the reconcile and create-voucher ones - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/bisect_accounting_statements/bisect_accounting_statements.py`: four methods on the document class (lines 124, 187, 201 and 215) - `erpnext/support/doctype/issue/issue.py`: a method on the `Issue` class (line 120) and a module-level function (line 276) - `erpnext/stock/doctype/warehouse/warehouse.py` (line 194) - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/budget/budget.py` (line 881) - `erpnext/www/banking.py` (line 32), which also sets `allow_guest=True` My search for the decorator was capped at 40 results, so this list is a sample and the full set is larger. I also only pulled function names for the first seven files above. For the other files I have the decorator line but not the name of the function it decorates. The restriction also applies to decorated methods on a `Document` class. Those are the ones reached through `frappe.call` or `run_doc_method`, such as the `bisect_accounting_statements` and `Issue` methods above.

final answer
ERPNext restricts these endpoints with an argument to Frappe's `@frappe.whitelist` decorator: `@frappe.whitelist(methods=["POST"])`. I found the usages with a grep and didn't read Frappe's own code, so the enforcement details below come from my knowledge of Frappe, not from this repo. **Mechanism** - The `methods` argument gives the HTTP verbs allowed for that function. A plain `@frappe.whitelist()` allows GET, POST, PUT and DELETE. - The decorator records the allowed verbs when the function is registered as an endpoint. When a request reaches `/api/method/<dotted.path>`, Frappe's request handling calls `frappe.is_whitelisted(method)`. That check compares `frappe.request.method` with the allowed list. A GET to a POST-only endpoint is rejected with a `PermissionError` (HTTP 403, "Not permitted") before the function runs. - This blocks state changes triggered by a simple GET, such as an `<img>` tag, a link, or a cross-site GET. POST requests also go through Frappe's CSRF token check. The ERPNext code only sets the `methods` list. - Callers have to use POST. For example, `rfq.js` calls `erpnext.buying.doctype.request_for_quotation.mapper.create_supplier_quotation` with `type: "POST"`. **Endpoints with `methods=["POST"]`** - `erpnext/buying/doctype/request_for_quotation/mapper.py`: `create_supplier_quotation` - `erpnext/selling/doctype/sales_order/mapper.py`: `make_work_orders` - `erpnext/projects/doctype/task/task.py`: `set_multiple_status`, `add_node` and `add_multiple_tasks` - `erpnext/selling/page/point_of_sale/point_of_sale.py`: `create_opening_voucher` and `set_customer_info` - `erpnext/controllers/accounts_controller.py`: `update_company_master_and_address` - `erpnext/crm/doctype/lead/lead.py`: `add_lead_to_prospect` Other files with the same decorator (I didn't check which functions) are: - `customer.py` - `project.py` - `company.py` - `employee.py` - `department.py` - `work_order.py` - `stock_controller.py` - `workstation.py` - `location.py` - `supplier_scorecard.py` - `stock_reposting_settings.py` - `lead/mapper.py` - `www/banking.py` The `www/banking.py` one also sets `allow_guest=True`. My grep output was capped at 25 matches, so this list is probably incomplete.

final answer
I've read the relevant code and have what I need, so here is the answer. ## How the progress is computed The recompute happens in `RequiredItemsService` in `erpnext/manufacturing/doctype/work_order/services/required_items.py`. Whenever a Stock Entry is submitted or cancelled, `update_transferred_qty_for_required_items()` runs. 1. **Per-item totals.** `_material_transfer_qty_by_item(is_return=0)` sums the submitted "Material Transfer for Manufacture" Stock Entry Detail quantities for each item. `update_transferred_qty_for_required_items()` writes each total to `required_items.transferred_qty` with `db_set`. `refresh_material_transferred_for_manufacturing()` does the header recompute without touching the rows or stock reservations. 2. **Header field.** `recompute_material_transferred_for_manufacturing()` sets `material_transferred_for_manufacturing` in one of three ways: - **Job Card transfers.** If `transfer_material_against == "Job Card"` and the work order has operations, it returns early. The Job Card code (`get_transferred_qty_from_stock_entry` in `job_card.py`) manages the field instead. - **Stock entries with `fg_completed_qty`.** If the sum of `fg_completed_qty` is non-zero, that sum is used. `StatusService.get_transferred_or_manufactured_qty()` in `services/status.py` computes it. - **Pick-list partial transfers.** These entries have `fg_completed_qty = 0`, so it uses a min-fraction rule. For each required item with `include_item_in_manufacturing` set, it takes `transferred_qty / required_qty`. It then takes the smallest of those fractions and caps it at 1.0. The result is `min_fraction * work_order.qty`. The min-fraction rule means a work order with several partial batches isn't shown as fully transferred until every raw material is fully transferred. The scarcest component sets the progress. 3. **Status.** `StatusService` in `services/status.py` calls `refresh_material_transferred_for_manufacturing()` before it decides between Not Started and In Process. ## What prevents over-transfer - **Work order level.** `StatusService.update_work_order_qty()` calls `_update_qty_for_purpose()`. If the computed quantity exceeds `qty` plus the allowance, it throws `StockOverProductionError`. The message reads "… cannot be greater than planned quantity …". The allowance comes from `_qty_allowance()`, which reads `overproduction_percentage_for_work_order` in Manufacturing Settings. For the transfer purpose it falls back to `transfer_extra_materials_percentage`. `WorkOrder.validate_additional_transferred_qty()` in `work_order.py` covers the `additional_transferred_qty` case. - **Stock Entry level, per item.** `_validate_no_excess_transfer()` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/services/material_transfer.py` applies when the entry has a work order but no `fg_completed_qty`, which is the pick-list case. For each item it computes the pending quantity as the sum of `required_qty - transferred_qty` over the work order's required items. It sums the entry's rows that have a source warehouse, matching substitutes through `original_item`. If a total exceeds the pending quantity, it throws "Excess Material Transfer: Maximum transferable quantity is …". The check is skipped for returns. It is also skipped when `backflush_raw_materials_based_on` is "Material Transferred for Manufacture". - **When the entry is built.** `get_pending_raw_materials()`, `_is_overproduction_allowed()` and `_calculate_item_transfer_qty()` in the same file cap the quantities for the "Make Stock Entry" flow. They use `required_qty - transferred_qty`, and `_resolve_transfer_qty()` makes the final choice. - **Pick list side.** `pick_list.py` (around line 1350) only picks work orders where `wo.qty > wo.material_transferred_for_manufacturing`. I did not read the body of `_resolve_transfer_qty()` or the rest of `pick_list.py`, so I haven't confirmed exactly how they cap the quantities.

final answer
When a Work Order's raw materials are transferred in partial batches, ERPNext derives the transferred progress from the item quantities actually moved. Two checks stop it going past the work order quantity. I read the code but did not run it. ## How progress is computed 1. A submitted or cancelled "Material Transfer for Manufacture" Stock Entry calls `MaterialTransferForManufactureStockEntry.update_job_card_and_work_order` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/services/material_transfer.py`. - It runs `_validate_work_order`. - It calls `wo_doc.add_additional_items` or `remove_additional_items`. - It calls `update_work_order_qty` and `update_status` on the work order. 2. `RequiredItemsService.update_transferred_qty_for_required_items` in `erpnext/manufacturing/doctype/work_order/services/required_items.py` does the per-item part. It calls `_material_transfer_qty_by_item`, which sums `transfer_qty` from submitted Stock Entry Detail rows, grouped by `item_code` and `original_item` so substitute items are credited to the right required item. It then writes each total to the required item row's `transferred_qty`. `refresh_material_transferred_for_manufacturing` does only the progress recompute, without the per-row updates, for status decisions. 3. Both of those call `recompute_material_transferred_for_manufacturing`, which sets `material_transferred_for_manufacturing` in this order: - If the work order transfers material against Job Card, it returns without changing the field. - If the sum of `fg_completed_qty` on those Stock Entries (`StatusService.get_transferred_or_manufactured_qty`) is above zero, that sum is the value. - Pick-list transfers set `fg_completed_qty` to 0, so they use a fallback. For each required item it takes `transferred / required_qty` and uses the smallest of those ratios, capped at 1.0, times the work order `qty`. For example, if one component is only 50% transferred, progress is 50% of the quantity, however much of the other components has moved. This stops partial pick lists from marking the work order fully transferred. ## What prevents over-transfer - **Work order quantity limit:** `StatusService._update_qty_for_purpose` in `erpnext/manufacturing/doctype/work_order/services/status.py` is called from `update_work_order_qty`. It compares the new total with `qty + qty × allowance%`. If the total is higher, it throws `StockOverProductionError` ("… cannot be greater than planned quantity …"). It also skips the check when `track_semi_finished_goods` is set. - **Extra material limit:** `WorkOrder.validate_additional_transferred_qty` in `erpnext/manufacturing/doctype/work_order/work_order.py` is also called from `update_work_order_qty`. It checks `material_transferred_for_manufacturing + additional_transferred_qty` against `qty × (1 + transfer_extra_materials_percentage/100)`. That percentage is the "Transfer Extra Raw Materials to WIP" setting in Manufacturing Settings. I did not read the other validation code in the Stock Entry itself, so there may be further checks there.

final answer
**How the progress is computed** `material_transferred_for_manufacturing` is recomputed from submitted Stock Entries. It is not incremented. The logic is in `erpnext/manufacturing/doctype/work_order/services/required_items.py`, in `RequiredItemsService`: 1. `update_transferred_qty_for_required_items()` calls `_material_transfer_qty_by_item(is_return=0)`. That query sums `transfer_qty` per item (keyed by `original_item or item_code`) over submitted "Material Transfer for Manufacture" Stock Entries for the work order. The sums are written to each required-items row's `transferred_qty`. The method then calls `recompute_material_transferred_for_manufacturing()`. 2. `refresh_material_transferred_for_manufacturing()` runs only step 3 and leaves the per-row quantities alone. The status logic calls it through `WorkOrder.refresh_material_transferred_for_manufacturing`, so the Not Started / In Process decision uses fresh data. 3. `recompute_material_transferred_for_manufacturing()` handles three cases: - **Job Card transfers:** if the work order has operations and `transfer_material_against == "Job Card"`, it returns without changing the field. - **Direct or excess transfers:** if `StatusService.get_transferred_or_manufactured_qty(...)` returns a non-zero total, it uses `SUM(fg_completed_qty)` over the submitted transfer entries. - **Pick-list or partial transfers:** these entries have `fg_completed_qty = 0`. It builds the required quantity per item from `required_items`, skipping rows with `include_item_in_manufacturing` off or `required_qty <= 0`. It takes `min_fraction = min(transferred/required)` across the items, caps it at 1.0, and stores `min_fraction * work_order.qty`. Because the lowest-fraction item sets the value, a partly fulfilled pick list doesn't mark the work order as fully transferred. **What prevents over-transfer** - **Hard cap:** in `erpnext/manufacturing/doctype/work_order/services/status.py`, `StatusService.update_work_order_qty()` calls `_update_qty_for_purpose()`. For "Material Transfer for Manufacture" it computes the quantity with `get_transferred_or_manufactured_qty()`. It then compares that to `qty + allowance% * qty`. The allowance comes from `_qty_allowance()`, which reads `overproduction_percentage_for_work_order` from Manufacturing Settings. For transfers it falls back to `transfer_extra_materials_percentage` when the first is 0. If the quantity is higher, it throws `StockOverProductionError` ("cannot be greater than planned quantity"). Otherwise it saves the value with `db_set`. Transfers made through Job Cards are skipped here, and so is the whole method when `track_semi_finished_goods` is set. It also calls `validate_additional_transferred_qty()` when `additional_transferred_qty` is set. - **Per-item cap when the entry is built:** in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/services/material_transfer.py`: - `get_pending_raw_materials()` caps each item at what is still needed. - `_calculate_item_transfer_qty()` computes `pending_to_issue = required_qty - transferred_qty` and `desire_to_transfer = fg_completed_qty * required_qty / wo.qty`. It lets the quantity through only if `desire_to_transfer <= pending_to_issue`, or if overproduction is allowed, or if the backflush setting is "Material Transferred for Manufacture". The result goes through `_resolve_transfer_qty`. - `_is_overproduction_allowed()` checks that `material_transferred_for_manufacturing + fg_completed_qty` stays within `qty * (1 + pct/100)`. - If nothing is left, it shows "All items have already been transferred for this Work Order." - **Stock Entry side:** `StockEntry.validate_work_order` in `stock/doctype/stock_entry/services/manufacturing.py` also validates the entry against the work order.

final answer
Two code paths in ERPNext work out the "material transferred for manufacturing" progress. One sums `fg_completed_qty` and one uses item-level transfers. Separate checks stop over-transfer. I didn't open the Stock Entry code that calls the work-order update, so the calling side is inferred from the caller graph. **How the progress is computed** 1. **`StatusService.update_work_order_qty`** is in `erpnext/manufacturing/doctype/work_order/services/status.py`. `WorkOrder.update_work_order_qty` in `work_order.py` delegates to it. - It loops over `_QTY_PURPOSES`. The relevant entries are ("Material Transfer for Manufacture", `material_transferred_for_manufacturing`) and the same purpose with `additional_transferred_qty`. - For each entry it calls `_update_qty_for_purpose`, which sets the field with `db_set`. - `get_transferred_or_manufactured_qty` does the summing. It adds up `fg_completed_qty` over all submitted Stock Entries that have this work order and the purpose, split by the `is_additional_transfer_entry` flag. Each partial transfer therefore adds to the total. - Cancelled or draft entries are excluded, because the filter requires `docstatus == 1`. 2. **`RequiredItemsService.recompute_material_transferred_for_manufacturing`** is in `erpnext/manufacturing/doctype/work_order/services/required_items.py`. It exists for pick-list transfers, which leave `fg_completed_qty` at 0. `refresh_material_transferred_for_manufacturing` calls it with transfers grouped by item from `_material_transfer_qty_by_item`. - If the `fg_completed_qty` sum is non-zero, it uses that sum. - Otherwise it builds the required quantity per item from `required_items`. It then takes the minimum of transferred divided by required across all items, capped at 1.0. - It sets the field to `min_fraction * work_order.qty`. A partly fulfilled pick list therefore never marks the work order as fully transferred. The scarcest raw material sets the progress. - The function returns early when `skip_transfer` is set. It also returns early when transfers are made against Job Cards (`transfer_material_against == "Job Card"`), where Job Card quantities drive the progress instead. - `StatusService.update_status` calls the refresh before deciding the status, so the status reflects pick-list transfers immediately. The status is "In Process" once `material_transferred_for_manufacturing > 0`, or when `_has_transferred_material` finds a pick-list or Material Request-sourced Stock Entry. **What prevents over-transfer** - **Planned-quantity check.** `_update_qty_for_purpose` computes `completed_qty = qty + allowance% * qty`. If the summed quantity is larger, it throws `StockOverProductionError` ("cannot be greater than planned quantity"). - **Allowance percentage.** `_qty_allowance` reads `overproduction_percentage_for_work_order` from Manufacturing Settings. For transfers only, if that is 0 it falls back to `transfer_extra_materials_percentage`. - **Additional transfers.** `WorkOrder.validate_additional_transferred_qty` in `work_order.py` runs when `additional_transferred_qty` is set. It requires `material_transferred_for_manufacturing + additional_transferred_qty` to be at most `qty * (1 + transfer_extra_materials_percentage/100)`. Otherwise it throws. - **Job Card transfers.** `JobCard.validate_transfer_qty` in `job_card.py` is a different check. It blocks submitting a Job Card whose `transferred_qty` is below `for_quantity`.

final answer
`make_gl_entries(gl_map, cancel=False, adv_adj=False, merge_entries=True, update_outstanding="Yes", from_repost=False)` is the central function that posts a voucher's GL map to the GL Entry table. I read `erpnext/accounts/general_ledger.py` and grepped the callers. I did not trace every caller's keyword arguments. ## What it does today Nothing happens if `gl_map` is empty. **Cancel path (`cancel=True`)** - It skips validation and merging and calls `make_reverse_gl_entries(gl_map, adv_adj, update_outstanding)`. - That function reverses the existing entries and calls `set_as_cancel`. **Post path** 1. **Budget validation.** Unless Accounts Settings `use_legacy_budget_controller` is set, or the voucher is a Period Closing Voucher, it runs `BudgetValidation(gl_map).validate()`. 2. **Accounting dimension offsetting.** `make_acc_dimensions_offsetting_entry` adds offsetting entries for accounting dimensions. 3. **Pre-save validations.** - `validate_accounting_period` checks the accounting period. - `validate_disabled_accounts` rejects disabled accounts. 4. **`process_gl_map(gl_map, merge_entries, from_repost)`.** - For non-PCV vouchers, `distribute_gl_based_on_cost_center_allocation` splits entries across cost centers. - If `merge_entries` is true, `merge_similar_entries` runs (see below). - `toggle_debit_credit_if_negative` always runs. 5. **Saving.** - With more than one entry, it first calls `create_payment_ledger_entry`, except for PCV. This writes the Payment Ledger Entry rows and uses `adv_adj`, `update_outstanding` and `from_repost`. - Then `save_entries` runs. - A single remaining entry raises "Incorrect number of General Ledger Entries found". **`save_entries`** - `validate_cwip_accounts` runs unless the call is a repost. - `process_debit_credit_difference` runs (round-off handling below). - `check_freezing_date` runs, using `adv_adj`. - `validate_against_pcv` runs for non-PCV vouchers. - For each entry, `validate_allowed_dimensions` runs, then `make_entry`. - `make_entry` builds a GL Entry, sets the flags `from_repost`, `adv_adj` and `update_outstanding`, and submits it. - After that it calls `validate_expense_against_budget`, unless the call is a repost, the voucher is a PCV, or the entry is a cancellation (Journal Entry is the exception and still runs it). ## Merging and deduplication - `merge_similar_entries` runs only when `merge_entries=True`. - Entries with `_skip_merge` are never merged. - The merge key (`get_merge_properties` and `get_merge_key`) is: - account, cost_center, party, party_type, voucher_detail_no - against_voucher, against_voucher_type, project, finance_book, voucher_no - advance_voucher_type, advance_voucher_no - plus all accounting dimensions - Matching entries are found with `check_if_in_list`. Their debit and credit are summed in company currency, account currency and transaction currency. - Entries where debit and credit are both zero at company-currency precision are then dropped. The exception is a Journal Entry whose voucher_type is "Exchange Gain Or Loss". - `toggle_debit_credit_if_negative` then flips negative debits into credits and negative credits into debits. - If `post_net_value` is set, it nets debit against credit. - This step runs even when `merge_entries=False`. ## Round-off handling `process_debit_credit_difference` works in this order: 1. It rounds each entry to the GL Entry debit precision and computes the debit−credit difference, in both company and transaction currency (`get_debit_credit_difference`). 2. The allowance (`get_debit_credit_allowance`) is `5/10^precision` for Journal Entry and Payment Entry, and 0.5 for every other voucher type. 3. If the difference is above the allowance, it calls `raise_debit_credit_not_equal_error`. The one exception is a Journal Entry of type Exchange Gain Or Loss. 4. If the difference is within the allowance but at least one unit of precision, it calls `make_round_off_gle`. 5. It then re-checks the difference and raises again if it is still too large. `make_round_off_gle` works like this: - `get_round_off_account_and_cost_center` supplies the account and cost center. - The account is the company's `round_off_account`, falling back to `default_expense_account`. - The cost center is the voucher's own cost center if it has one, otherwise the company's `round_off_cost_center`. - It throws if either is missing. - If any entry is an opening entry, it uses `round_off_for_opening` instead, and throws if that is not set. - For non-PCV vouchers, if a round-off entry already exists in the map, it adjusts that entry. If the adjusted difference falls below one unit of precision, it removes the entry. - Otherwise it appends a new entry. - Party and against_voucher are cleared, and `update_accounting_dimensions` fills in the dimensions. ## What a change would affect **Direct callers of `make_gl_entries`** - **Sales Invoice**: `SalesInvoice.make_gl_entries` in `sales_invoice.py`, with a `services/gl_composer.py` that uses `merge_similar_entries` and `get_round_off_account_and_cost_center`. - **Purchase Invoice**: `PurchaseInvoice.make_gl_entries` in `purchase_invoice.py`, with a similar `services/gl_composer.py`. - **Payment Entry**: `make_gl_entries` at `payment_entry.py:1298` and `:1316`. It passes `merge_entries`, `adv_adj` and `update_outstanding`. - **Journal Entry**: `journal_entry.py:878`. - **Invoice Discounting**: `invoice_discounting.py:190`, with `update_outstanding="No"`. - **Period Closing Voucher**: `period_closing_voucher.py:475`, with `merge_entries=False`. - **Process Period Closing Voucher**: `process_period_closing_voucher.py:438`, with `merge_entries=False`. - **Deferred revenue and expense**: - `accounts/deferred_revenue.py` calls it at lines 444 and 580. It also defines its own function named `make_gl_entries` at line 519, which is a different function. - `process_deferred_accounting.py:54` calls it with `cancel=1`. - **Stock vouchers**: `StockController.make_gl_entries` in `controllers/stock_controller.py:205` is the shared call. These doctypes inherit it: - Purchase Receipt, Delivery Note, Stock Entry, Stock Reconciliation - Subcontracting Receipt - Landed Cost Voucher, which calls the vouchers' `make_gl_entries(via_landed_cost_voucher=True)` - **Assets**: - Asset Capitalization, which passes `merge_entries=False` - Asset Repair - Asset (`asset.py:981`) - the Asset mapper, which goes through Journal Entry **Indirect callers, through each doctype's own `make_gl_entries(...)` method** - `repost_accounting_ledger.py` (lines 192–213, including `from_repost=True`) - `accounts/utils.py:1705`, which reposts with `from_repost=True` - `controllers/ledger_preview.py:43` - patches in v12, v13 and v15 **Other code that depends on the same internals** - `process_gl_map` is imported by the stock `base_stock_gl_composer.py` and by the Stock Entry, Purchase Receipt and Subcontracting Receipt `gl_composer.py` files. - `merge_similar_entries` is used by the Sales Invoice and Purchase Invoice composers. So a signature change needs updates in every caller above that passes keyword arguments. A merge-behavior change will mainly hit the invoices, Payment Entry, Journal Entry and stock postings, because they merge by default. The callers that pass `merge_entries=False` (PCV, Process PCV, Asset Capitalization) are affected only through `toggle_debit_credit_if_negative` and the round-off logic.

final answer
`make_gl_entries` is at `erpnext/accounts/general_ledger.py:34-74`. I read the function and the helpers it calls. I built the caller list from the code index plus a grep of `make_gl_entries(` and the `general_ledger` imports. I did not open each caller to check its arguments. ## What it does today The signature is `make_gl_entries(gl_map, cancel=False, adv_adj=False, merge_entries=True, update_outstanding="Yes", from_repost=False)`. It does nothing if `gl_map` is empty. **Posting path (`cancel=False`), in order:** 1. **Budget check.** Unless Accounts Settings has `use_legacy_budget_controller` on, or the voucher is a Period Closing Voucher, it runs `BudgetValidation(gl_map).validate()`. 2. **Dimension offsets.** `make_acc_dimensions_offsetting_entry` mutates `gl_map` in place. For each accounting dimension with automatic balancing enabled for the company, whose values differ across the entries, it adds a mirrored debit/credit row on the dimension's offsetting account. 3. **Period and account checks.** `validate_accounting_period` and `validate_disabled_accounts` (both in `services/gl_validator.py`). 4. **Merge and normalise.** `process_gl_map(gl_map, merge_entries, from_repost)`: - **Cost center allocation:** `distribute_gl_based_on_cost_center_allocation` splits rows across sub cost centers by percentage. This is skipped for Period Closing Vouchers. It validates the budget against the main cost center unless `from_repost` is set, and it assigns the round-off account to the main cost center without splitting it. - **Merge:** if `merge_entries` is true, `merge_similar_entries` runs (details below). - **Sign normalisation:** `toggle_debit_credit_if_negative` flips negative debits and credits to the opposite side. It also nets debit against credit when `post_net_value` is set. 5. **Guard on entry count.** If more than one entry remains: - It calls `create_payment_ledger_entry` (in `accounts/utils.py`), except for Period Closing Vouchers. - Then it calls `save_entries`. - If exactly one entry remains, it throws "Incorrect number of General Ledger Entries found…". 6. **`save_entries`:** - `validate_cwip_accounts`, skipped when `from_repost` is set. - `process_debit_credit_difference`, which handles round-off (see below). - `check_freezing_date`. - `validate_against_pcv`, skipped for Period Closing Vouchers. - `validate_allowed_dimensions` for each entry. - `make_entry` for each entry, which creates and submits a GL Entry. Each entry gets flags `from_repost`, `adv_adj` and `update_outstanding`, and the call then runs `validate_expense_against_budget` unless `from_repost` is set. **Cancel path (`cancel=True`):** it calls `make_reverse_gl_entries(gl_map, adv_adj, update_outstanding)` and skips all the validation and merging above. ## Merge and deduplication (`merge_similar_entries`) - Rows flagged `_skip_merge` are kept as they are. - All other rows get a `merge_key` made of these fields plus every accounting dimension. The `merge_key` is attached to the row: - account - cost_center - party - party_type - voucher_detail_no - against_voucher - against_voucher_type - project - finance_book - voucher_no - advance_voucher_type - advance_voucher_no - Rows with the same key are summed into the first one. The sums cover debit, credit, and their account-currency and transaction-currency variants. - Rows are then dropped if debit and credit both round to zero at the company currency precision. The exception is a Journal Entry whose type is "Exchange Gain Or Loss". ## Round-off handling (`process_debit_credit_difference`) - Every debit and credit is rounded to GL Entry precision, and the total difference is computed in both company and transaction currency. - The allowance is `5 / 10^precision` for Journal Entry and Payment Entry, and 0.5 for every other voucher type. - If the difference exceeds the allowance, it throws "Debit and Credit not equal". Journal Entries of type Exchange Gain Or Loss are exempt. - If the difference is below the allowance but at least one unit of precision, `make_round_off_gle` runs: - It uses the company round-off account and cost center from `get_round_off_account_and_cost_center`. For opening entries it uses "Round Off for Opening", and throws if that is not set. - If an entry on the round-off account already exists, it is adjusted. If the remaining difference is then below one unit of precision, that entry is removed. - Otherwise a new round-off entry is appended. It has no party and no against-voucher, and it gets accounting dimensions from the voucher or the mandatory defaults. - The difference is checked again after the round-off entry is added. ## What a change would affect **Direct callers the index found:** - `repost_accounting_ledger.start_repost` - `controllers/ledger_preview.get_accounting_ledger_preview` - `accounts/utils.repost_gle_for_stock_vouchers` - `process_period_closing_voucher.summarize_and_post_ledger_entries` The index only resolves some call edges, because many doctype methods share the name `make_gl_entries`. The grep of `make_gl_entries(` found these files: - **Accounts:** - `sales_invoice` - `purchase_invoice` - `payment_entry` - `journal_entry` - `period_closing_voucher` - `process_deferred_accounting` - `invoice_discounting` - `deferred_revenue.py` - **Stock:** - `stock_entry` - `delivery_note` - `purchase_receipt` - `stock_reconciliation` - `landed_cost_voucher` - `controllers/stock_controller.py`, the base class that the stock documents use - **Subcontracting:** `subcontracting_receipt` - **Assets:** - `asset` - `asset_capitalization` - `asset_repair` - `asset/mapper.py` - **Patches:** `v12_0/repost_stock_ledger_entries_for_target_warehouse`, `v13_0/modify_invalid_gain_loss_gl_entries`, `v15_0/repost_gl_entries_with_no_account_subcontracting` Several of these probably wrap the function in their own `make_gl_entries` methods. The first four callers above are the ones that carry `from_repost` or preview behaviour. Check each caller's keyword arguments before you change the signature. **Other things that depend on the same code:** - These import `process_gl_map`, `merge_similar_entries` or `get_round_off_account_and_cost_center`. ## What `make_gl_entries` does today The function is `make_gl_entries(gl_map, cancel=False, adv_adj=False, merge_entries=True, update_outstanding="Yes", from_repost=False)` in `erpnext/accounts/general_ledger.py`, lines 34–74. If `gl_map` is empty it does nothing. **Cancel path (`cancel=True`)** - It skips everything below and calls `make_reverse_gl_entries(gl_map, adv_adj, update_outstanding)`. **Post path** 1. **Budget check.** This runs only if the legacy budget controller is off and the voucher type is not Period Closing Voucher. It calls `BudgetValidation(gl_map).validate()`. 2. **Dimension offsetting.** `make_acc_dimensions_offsetting_entry` adds offsetting entries for accounting dimensions. 3. **Validations.** `validate_accounting_period` and `validate_disabled_accounts` run next. 4. **Map processing.** `process_gl_map(gl_map, merge_entries, from_repost)` runs, as described under merging below. 5. **Payment ledger.** If more than one entry remains, it calls `create_payment_ledger_entry`. This is skipped for Period Closing Voucher. 6. **Save.** It then calls `save_entries`. 7. **Entry-count error.** If exactly one entry is left, it throws "Incorrect number of General Ledger Entries found". **Merging and deduplication (`process_gl_map`)** - For non-Period-Closing vouchers it first runs `distribute_gl_based_on_cost_center_allocation`. - If `merge_entries` is true, it runs `merge_similar_entries`: - Entries flagged `_skip_merge` are kept as they are. - The merge key comes from `get_merge_properties` and `get_merge_key`. It covers account, cost_center, party, party_type, voucher_detail_no, against_voucher, against_voucher_type, project, finance_book, voucher_no, advance_voucher_type and advance_voucher_no, plus all accounting dimensions. - Entries with the same key are combined by summing debit, credit and their account-currency and transaction-currency variants (`check_if_in_list`). - Entries where debit and credit are both zero at company precision are dropped. The exception is a Journal Entry of type "Exchange Gain Or Loss". - `toggle_debit_credit_if_negative` always runs, even with `merge_entries=False`. It flips negative debits into credits and vice versa. It also nets debit against credit when `post_net_value` is set. **Save and round-off (`save_entries`)** - Validations: - `validate_cwip_accounts` is skipped on repost. - `check_freezing_date` and `validate_against_pcv` (the latter skipped for Period Closing Voucher) check the posting date. - `validate_allowed_dimensions` checks each entry against the dimension filters. - `process_debit_credit_difference` rounds debit and credit to GL Entry precision and computes the difference, including in transaction currency. - The allowance is `5/10^precision` for Journal Entry and Payment Entry, and 0.5 for everything else. - If the difference is above the allowance, it raises "Debit and Credit not equal for …". This is waived for Journal Entries of type Exchange Gain Or Loss. - If the difference is at least one unit of precision, `make_round_off_gle` fixes it: - It uses the company round-off account, or the "Round Off for Opening" account when the voucher has opening entries. It throws if that account is not set. - It reuses an existing round-off entry if there is one, and removes it if the net difference becomes negligible. - Otherwise it creates a new entry, with party and against-voucher fields cleared and accounting dimensions applied. - The difference is then re-checked and raises again if it is still above the allowance. - `make_entry` builds a `GL Entry` doc, sets flags (`from_repost`, `adv_adj`, `update_outstanding`), and submits it. It then runs `validate_expense_against_budget`, unless the entry is a repost, a Period Closing Voucher, or a cancelled entry that is not a Journal Entry. ## What a change would affect **Direct callers of the module-level function** - **Payment Entry** (`payment_entry.py` ~1298 and ~1316). It passes `cancel`, `adv_adj` and `merge_entries`, and in the second call `update_outstanding`. - **Journal Entry** (`journal_entry.py` ~878, inside `make_gl_entries(cancel, adv_adj)`). - **Sales Invoice** (`sales_invoice.py` ~1045) and **Purchase Invoice** (`purchase_invoice.py` ~678). Both pass `from_repost`, and their gl_composer modules use `merge_similar_entries` directly. - **StockController** (`controllers/stock_controller.py` ~205). It calls `make_gl_entries(gl_entries, from_repost=...)`. Subclasses that inherit this include Purchase Receipt, Delivery Note, Stock Entry, Stock Reconciliation, Subcontracting Receipt and Landed Cost Voucher. - **Invoice Discounting** (~190). It passes `update_outstanding="No"`. - **Asset** (`asset.py` ~981), **Asset Repair** (~315) and **Asset Capitalization** (~390, with `merge_entries=False` and `from_repost`). - **Period Closing Voucher** (~475) and **Process Period Closing Voucher** (~438). Both pass `merge_entries=False`, and this voucher type is special-cased throughout the function. - **Deferred revenue/expense** (`accounts/deferred_revenue.py` ~580). It passes `merge_entries=True`, and its own `make_gl_entries` wrapper is at ~519. - **Process Deferred Accounting** (~54). It calls `make_gl_entries(gl_map=..., cancel=1)`. The `*.make_gl_entries` methods on documents (such as `SalesInvoice.make_gl_entries`) are separate wrappers with their own signatures. They matter only because they delegate to this function.

The caller list below comes from a grep of `make_gl_entries(` plus codegraph's caller summary. I did not open each caller to check how it passes arguments. ## What it does today **Signature:** `make_gl_entries(gl_map, cancel=False, adv_adj=False, merge_entries=True, update_outstanding="Yes", from_repost=False)`. If `gl_map` is empty, nothing happens. Otherwise: **Cancel path (`cancel=True`)** - It skips every validation below and calls `make_reverse_gl_entries(gl_map, adv_adj, update_outstanding)`. **Submit path (`cancel=False`)** 1. **Budget check.** `BudgetValidation(gl_map).validate()` runs unless the "use legacy budget controller" setting is on or the voucher is a Period Closing Voucher. 2. **Dimension offsetting.** `make_acc_dimensions_offsetting_entry` appends mirror entries, in place, for accounting dimensions that have an offsetting account configured. This applies only when the map contains more than one value for that dimension. 3. **Validators.** `validate_accounting_period` and `validate_disabled_accounts` come from `erpnext/accounts/services/gl_validator.py`. 4. **`process_gl_map`:** - It splits entries by cost center allocation (`distribute_gl_based_on_cost_center_allocation`), except for Period Closing Vouchers. - If `merge_entries` is true, it runs `merge_similar_entries`. - It always runs `toggle_debit_credit_if_negative`. 5. **Count check.** If more than one entry remains, it continues. If exactly one remains, it throws "Incorrect number of General Ledger Entries found". 6. **Payment ledger.** `create_payment_ledger_entry` runs, except for Period Closing Vouchers. 7. **`save_entries`:** - `validate_cwip_accounts` runs, unless `from_repost`. It only applies to Journal Entry vouchers. - `process_debit_credit_difference` handles the debit/credit balance and round-off. - `check_freezing_date` and `validate_against_pcv` run. - `validate_allowed_dimensions` runs per entry. - `make_entry` creates and submits a "GL Entry" doc. It sets flags such as `from_repost`, `adv_adj` and `update_outstanding`. After that it calls `validate_expense_against_budget`. **Merging and deduplication (`merge_similar_entries`)** - Entries are merged on a key built from these fields: `account`, `cost_center`, `party`, `party_type`, `voucher_detail_no`, `against_voucher`, `against_voucher_type`, `project`, `finance_book`, `voucher_no`, `advance_voucher_type`, `advance_voucher_no`, plus all accounting dimensions. `get_merge_properties` defines this list. - Matching entries have their debit and credit summed in company currency, account currency and transaction currency. - Entries flagged `_skip_merge` are never merged. - Entries whose debit and credit both round to zero are dropped. The exception is a Journal Entry of type "Exchange Gain Or Loss". - `check_if_in_list` finds the match with a linear scan. - `toggle_debit_credit_if_negative` flips negative debits into credits and negative credits into debits. If an entry has `post_net_value` set, debit and credit are netted into a single side. **Round-off (`process_debit_credit_difference`, `make_round_off_gle`)** - `get_debit_credit_difference` rounds each entry to the GL Entry precision and sums the difference. It does this in both company currency and transaction currency. - The allowance is `5/10^precision` for Journal Entry and Payment Entry, and 0.5 for everything else. - If the difference exceeds the allowance, it throws "Debit and Credit not equal". Exchange Gain Or Loss journals are exempt. - If the difference is below the allowance but at least one unit of precision, `make_round_off_gle` adds a round-off entry: - The account is the company's `round_off_account`, falling back to the default expense account. If the map has opening entries, it uses `round_off_for_opening` instead. - The cost center is the parent document's cost center if it has one. Otherwise it is the company's `round_off_cost_center`. - If a round-off entry already exists in the map, it is adjusted, or removed if the net difference is now below one unit of precision. - `party_type`, `party` and the against-voucher fields are cleared. Accounting dimensions are copied from the voucher, or defaults are applied. - After the round-off entry, the difference is checked again and may throw. ## What a change would affect **Direct call sites** (non-patch): - **Accounts** - Sales Invoice, Purchase Invoice, Payment Entry (also through `make_advance_gl_entries`) and Journal Entry. - Invoice Discounting, Period Closing Voucher, Process Period Closing Voucher and Process Deferred Accounting. - Repost Accounting Ledger (6 occurrences). - `erpnext/accounts/deferred_revenue.py`, which has its own wrapper also called `make_gl_entries`. - `erpnext/accounts/utils.py`, in the repost flow. - **Controllers** - `StockController.make_gl_entries` and its sibling methods. It is a different function from this one, and it calls the module-level one. Those include `PaymentEntry`, `PurchaseInvoice`, `InvoiceDiscounting`, `PeriodClosingVoucher` and `StockController`. Codegraph flagged them as 8 runtime-dispatched implementations on `AccountsController`. If you change it, payment-ledger entries, outstanding updates (`update_outstanding`) and `against_voucher` linkage for invoices and payments all change. - The `merge_entries=False` callers rely on seeing the raw, unmerged lines. final answer
No, the report doesn't break. The shared Trends engine handles that case by skipping the empty detail row. The code is in `erpnext/controllers/trends.py`, in the `get_data` function, in the branch that runs when `filters.get("group_by")` is set. **How the branch works:** 1. The first query, `data1`, aggregates by the based-on key (`conditions["group_by"]`). Each row is appended to `data` with a blank cell inserted at index `ind`. 2. For each `data1[d]`, a second query selects the `DISTINCT` group-by values (`sel_col`, such as `t2.item_code`, `t1.customer` or `t1.supplier`). It filters on `based_on_key = data1[d][0]`. 3. For each distinct value `row[i][0]`, a third query, `row1`, fetches the period-wise sums. It filters on both `sel_col = row[i][0]` and `based_on_key = data1[d][0]`. **What happens when `row1` is empty:** - The code has an explicit guard at lines 210–211: `if not row1: continue`. - That `continue` skips the following lines, so no row is built for that combination: - `des[ind] = row[i][0]` - `des[ind - 1] = row1[0][0]` - the `for j` loop that reads `row1[0][j]` - Without the guard, `row1[0]` would raise an `IndexError`. - The effect is that the based-on row appears with no child row for that group value, and the loop moves on to the next distinct value. - After the loops, `calculate_total_row(data1, ...)` appends the total row as usual. I read the code but didn't run the report.

final answer
No, the report doesn't break. When the detail query finds nothing, `get_data` in `erpnext/controllers/trends.py` skips that group value and carries on. When "Group By" is set, `get_data` works in these steps: 1. It runs a main query grouped by the based-on key. Each result row is appended to `data` with a blank cell inserted at the group-by column (`dt.insert(ind, "")`). 2. For each based-on row, it queries the distinct group-by values (`select DISTINCT(sel_col) ... where based_on_key = data1[d][0]`). 3. For each distinct value, it runs a detail query (`row1`). This query pulls the period-wise sums for that based-on and group-by pair, grouped by currency and `sel_col`. The detail query's result is guarded here (lines 210–211): ```python if not row1: continue ``` An empty `row1` means the loop moves on to the next group value. No detail row is appended, and the code never reaches `row1[0][0]` or `row1[0][j]`, so there is no `IndexError`. The result is that the based-on row is still shown, with its blank group-by cell and its own totals. That group value just has no child row. The grand total row is still added afterwards by `calculate_total_row(data1, ...)`. The guard is the only protection against empty results. Without it, the next lines (`des[ind - 1] = row1[0][0]` and the `row1[0][j]` loop) would raise `IndexError`. The chart builders in the individual reports, such as `get_chart_data` in `sales_order_trends.py`, also skip rows where the group-by column is `""` and skip the `'Total'` row. That avoids counting the based-on summary rows twice in the chart.

The first query (`data1`) groups by the based-on column or columns, using `conditions["group_by"]`. Each row of `data1` is appended to `data` with a blank cell inserted at the group-by column index `ind`. 2. For each `data1` row, a second query selects `DISTINCT(sel_col)`. It filters on both `sel_col` and `based_on_key`. **What happens when the detail query returns nothing** The code has this guard right after the `row1` query: ```python if not row1: continue ``` - That detail row is skipped. No `des` row is built or appended, so there is no `IndexError` on `row1[0][...]`. - The loop moves to the next distinct group value. - The based-on summary row from `data1` is already in `data`, so it stays in the output. - `calculate_total_row(data1, ...)` totals only the `data1` rows. It doesn't depend on the detail rows, so the total row is still appended. **Why a detail query can come back empty** `based_on_key` is the first column of the multi-column `based_on_group_by`, such as `t1.supplier` in `t1.supplier, t3.supplier_group`. The detail query's join and `group by t4.default_currency` can also leave no rows for a given pairing. The output is a report that is missing the detail line for that pairing, not one that fails. When the detail query does return data, it fills `des[ind]` and `des[ind-1]`. The values then shift by `inc` columns: 3 for a Customer or Supplier based-on, 2 for Item, and 1 otherwise. Q08Group B — Caller/callee trace + impact analysisWhen ERPNext checks a Material Request or Purchase Order against a Budget, how does the system compute the "requested amount" acro…
When ERPNext checks a Material Request or Purchase Order against a Budget, how does the system compute the "requested amount" across multiple matching line items, and why does the placement of rate relative to the aggregate Sum() function matter for correctness?

turns: 2

cost: $0.0257
elapsed: 11.7s

49941,91712,01414,929

  1. #1Grep
    {"pattern": "def get_requested_amount|def get_ordered_amount", "path": "/benchmarks/dataset/repos/erpnext/erpnext/accounts/doctype/budget/budget.py", "output_mode": "content", "-A": 40}
    → 689:def get_requested_amount(params): 690- item_code = params.get("item_code") 691- 692- child = frappe.qb.DocType("Material Request Item") 693- parent = frappe.qb.DocType("Material Request") 694- 695- data = ( 696- frappe.qb.from_(child) 697- .join(parent) 698- .on(parent.name == child.parent) 6…
final answer
**How the requested amount is computed** `get_requested_amount(params)` in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/budget/budget.py` runs one SQL query. It joins `Material Request Item` (child) to `Material Request` (parent) and returns a single scalar: `Coalesce(Sum((child.stock_qty - child.ordered_qty) * child.rate), 0)` The query only includes lines that meet all of these conditions: - `child.item_code` matches the item being checked. - The parent is submitted (`docstatus == 1`). - The line still has unordered quantity (`stock_qty > ordered_qty`). - The parent is a Purchase-type request (`material_request_type == "Purchase"`) and is not `Stopped`. - The conditions from `get_other_condition` hold. These are the same `expense_account`, the budget-against dimension (such as cost center or project) if one is set, and a `schedule_date` within the budget's fiscal year range. Each matching line contributes its own pending quantity times its own rate. The database adds these up, and `Coalesce` turns a NULL (no matching rows) into 0. `get_ordered_amount` does the same for Purchase Orders. It sums `amount - billed_amt` over lines that are not yet fully billed. Those amounts are already monetary, so no rate is involved. **Why rate placement relative to `Sum()` matters** The rate has to be inside the aggregate, so the query multiplies per row and then sums. The code comment says this is "correct requested amount and is PG-valid". - **Correctness:** Different lines can have different rates. `Sum(qty) * rate` would need one rate for the whole group, and there is no single correct rate. `Sum(qty) * child.rate` would apply one arbitrary row's rate to the total quantity. That gives a wrong budget figure, so the budget could be wrongly blocked or wrongly allowed. - **PostgreSQL validity:** The `rate` column is not aggregated and not in a `GROUP BY`. Postgres rejects that query with an error ("column must appear in the GROUP BY clause or be used in an aggregate function"). MariaDB/MySQL would silently pick an arbitrary row's value when `ONLY_FULL_GROUP_BY` is off. Putting the rate inside `Sum()` makes the query valid on both databases and gives the correct total.

final answer
When ERPNext checks a Material Request or Purchase Order against a Budget, it adds up the open, unordered value of every matching Material Request line. That sum is computed per line inside the SQL aggregate, not as a sum of quantities multiplied by one rate. I read this from the code and did not run it. **How the requested amount is computed** There are two code paths, and both use the same formula. 1. `get_requested_amount(params)` in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/budget/budget.py` is called from `compare_expense_with_budget`. - It joins `Material Request Item` (child) to `Material Request` (parent). - It selects `Coalesce(Sum((child.stock_qty - child.ordered_qty) * child.rate), 0)`. - A line counts only if all of these hold: - The item code matches. - The parent is submitted (`docstatus == 1`). - The type is `Purchase`. - The status is not `Stopped`. - `stock_qty > ordered_qty`, so only lines with quantity still to be ordered are included. - `get_other_condition` adds three more filters: the line's expense account, the budget dimension (cost center or project), and a date range. The date is `schedule_date` for a Material Request and `transaction_date` otherwise. The range runs from the fiscal year start to the fiscal year end. 2. `BudgetValidation.get_requested_amount` in `erpnext/controllers/budget_controller.py` does the same thing. - Its formula is `Sum((IfNull(stock_qty,0) - IfNull(ordered_qty,0)) * IfNull(rate,0))`. - It filters by company, the dimension key, the expense account and the items on the current document. - The result is stored in `to_validate[key].requested_amount`. `compare_expense_with_budget` then uses this amount differently depending on the document being checked: - For a Material Request, it uses requested amount plus ordered amount. - For a Purchase Order, it uses the ordered amount only. - It adds the result to the actual expense and compares the total with the budget amount. - If the total is over, it stops or warns. **Why the placement of rate matters** The code comment in `budget.py` says it directly: "rate inside the aggregate: Sum(qty * rate) is the correct requested amount and is PG-valid". - **Correct arithmetic.** Each line has its own rate, so the value has to be computed per line and then summed. That gives Σ(qty_i × rate_i). - A form like `Sum(qty) * rate` puts the rate outside the aggregate. It multiplies the total quantity by a single rate, and the rate is not a grouped or aggregated column. - With several matching lines at different rates, that form gives a wrong amount. Quantities are valued at one arbitrary row's rate, or at an average. The budget check is then misstated: it can let an overspend through or block a valid request. - **PostgreSQL validity.** A bare `child.rate` outside `Sum()` is a non-aggregated column in an aggregate query with no `GROUP BY`. MariaDB tolerates that, but PostgreSQL rejects it. Putting rate inside `Sum()` makes the query valid on both databases.

final answer
When ERPNext checks a Material Request or Purchase Order against a Budget, it computes the requested amount in SQL. It sums the open amount of every matching Material Request line, and the rate is multiplied in inside each row's term, before the sum. **Where it happens** There are two implementations, both querying Material Request Item joined to Material Request. 1. `BudgetController.get_requested_amount(key)` in `erpnext/controllers/budget_controller.py`, around line 239. 2. The module-level `get_requested_amount(params)` in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/budget/budget.py`, around line 689. **Which lines match** Both functions keep only rows that meet these conditions: - The Material Request is submitted (`docstatus = 1`). - Its type is "Purchase". - Its status is not "Stopped". - The item or dimension matches the budget key (the controller version also matches the expense account and the fiscal-year dates). - In `budget.py`, the line must still be open: `stock_qty > ordered_qty`. The controller version has no such filter. When the document being checked is a Purchase Order or Material Request, the controller version also limits matches to the item codes on that document. **How the amount is computed** The controller version, for example, selects: `Sum((IfNull(stock_qty,0) - IfNull(ordered_qty,0)) * IfNull(rate,0)).as_("amount")` Each line contributes its own `(stock_qty − ordered_qty) × rate`, which is the quantity not yet ordered, valued at that line's own rate. The database adds these up across all matching lines. The result is stored in `self.to_validate[key].requested_amount` (`or 0` covers a NULL result). The `budget.py` version wraps the sum in `Coalesce(..., 0)` and returns `data[0][0]`. **Why the rate placement matters** - **Correct total:** `Sum(qty × rate)` multiplies per row and then adds. Lines for the same item or account can have different rates. Writing `Sum(qty) * rate` would multiply the total quantity by a single rate. That rate is either an arbitrary one picked from some row or an invalid expression, so the amount would be wrong. For example, 10 units at 5 plus 10 units at 7 is 120, not 20 × 5 or 20 × 7. - **Valid SQL on PostgreSQL:** a bare `rate` outside the aggregate is neither aggregated nor in a GROUP BY. PostgreSQL rejects that, and MariaDB would silently return an arbitrary row's value. The comment in `budget.py` says this directly: "rate inside the aggregate: Sum(qty * rate) is the correct requested amount and is PG-valid". I read the two functions above directly. I did not trace how `to_validate` feeds the final budget check.

The system computes the requested amount in SQL. It sums the open quantity times the rate for each matching row, so the rate has to sit inside `Sum()`. I read the code but did not run it or look at the git history. **Where the computation lives** There are two implementations. 1. **`get_requested_amount(params)` in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/budget/budget.py` (line 689).** This is the older path, reached from `compare_expense_with_budget`. It joins `Material Request Item` to `Material Request` and selects: ```python Coalesce(Sum((child.stock_qty - child.ordered_qty) * child.rate), 0).as_("amount") ``` - It filters on the item code and `docstatus == 1`. - It keeps only rows where `stock_qty > ordered_qty`, so only the open quantity counts. - It requires `material_request_type == "Purchase"` and `status != "Stopped"`. - It adds the extra conditions from `get_other_condition(...)`. - The comment in the code says the rate is inside the aggregate so the result is correct and valid on PostgreSQL. 2. **`BudgetValidation.get_requested_amount(key)` in `erpnext/controllers/budget_controller.py` (line 239).** This is the newer path and is called from `validate_for_overbooking`. Its filters are: - the company, `docstatus = 1`, "Purchase" type, and status not "Stopped"; - the transaction date within the fiscal year; - the expense account and the dimension from the key `(dimension, value, account)`; - the item codes on the current document. It selects: ```python Sum((IfNull(mri.stock_qty, 0) - IfNull(mri.ordered_qty, 0)) * IfNull(mri.rate, 0)) ``` The result is stored in `to_validate[key].requested_amount` (or 0 if the sum is null). `get_ordered_amount` works the same way for Purchase Orders, using `Sum(amount - billed_amt)`. **How multiple line items are combined** Each matching Material Request Item row contributes its own `(stock_qty - ordered_qty) * rate`. The database adds those products together. The result is the total open requested value for that budget key, covering every submitted Material Request in the fiscal year. The code compares that total plus the current document's amount with the budget. The comparison is in `handle_individual_doctype_action`, which checks the annual budget and the accumulated monthly budget. The action is Stop or Warn, unless the user has the exception approver role. **Why the rate must be inside `Sum()`** - **Correct maths.** Rates differ from row to row. The right total is the sum of each row's quantity times its own rate: Σ(qty_i · rate_i). - **Wrong if the rate is outside.** Writing `Sum(qty) * rate` or `Sum(qty - ordered) * child.rate` is wrong for two reasons: - It multiplies the total quantity by one rate, which gives a wrong amount when rows have different rates. - It leaves `rate` as a non-aggregated column in an aggregate query. MySQL's lax grouping lets that through by picking an arbitrary row's rate, so the answer is silently wrong. PostgreSQL rejects it with a "column must appear in GROUP BY or be used in an aggregate" error. - **Safe with nulls.** In the newer path, `IfNull` on each term keeps a null `ordered_qty` or `rate` from turning a row's contribution into NULL.

final answer
`POS Invoice.reset_mode_of_payments` hands the work to a shared helper, `update_multi_mode_option`. That helper clears the payments table and rebuilds it from the POS Profile. It uses `doc.get("is_created_using_pos")` so it doesn't fail on a doctype without that field. I read the code but didn't run it. **Entry point.** `reset_mode_of_payments` is a whitelisted method on the POS Invoice class in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/pos_invoice/pos_invoice.py` (around line 796). The Sales Invoice has equivalent versions: one in `sales_invoice.py` and one in `services/pos.py` (`reset_mode_of_payments`, line 173). It works like this: 1. If `self.pos_profile` is set, it loads the profile with `frappe.get_cached_doc("POS Profile", self.pos_profile)`. 2. It calls `update_multi_mode_option(self, pos_profile)`. 3. It sets `self.paid_amount = 0`. **Repopulating the table.** `update_multi_mode_option` is in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/sales_invoice/services/pos.py` (line 339). It does the following: 1. It wipes the existing rows with `doc.set("payments", [])`. 2. It collects the mode of payment from each row in `pos_profile.payments`. 3. It calls `get_mode_of_payments_info(mode_of_payments, doc.company)`. This returns, for each enabled mode of payment, its default account for that company and its type. 4. For each profile row, it copies the profile row's `default` flag onto the matching info entry and calls the inner `append_payment`. That adds a payments child row with `default`, `mode_of_payment`, `account` (the mode's default account) and `type`. 5. If a profile mode has no usable info, it collects a link to that Mode of Payment and skips it. The helper raises a "Missing Account" `frappe.throw` at the end, listing the modes that have no default Cash or Bank account. 6. If it replaced existing rows, it shows a toast: "Payment methods refreshed. Please review before proceeding." **Why it doesn't break on POS Invoice.** The helper is shared by both doctypes. The check for whether this is a refresh uses `mop_refetched = bool(doc.payments) and not doc.get("is_created_using_pos")`. `is_created_using_pos` exists only on Sales Invoice. Reading it as an attribute would raise `AttributeError` on a POS Invoice. `doc.get("is_created_using_pos")` returns `None` when the field is absent. The code comment at that line says the same thing. There is also a bug in the error-message branch. It compares `invalid_modes == 1`, which compares a list to an integer. That is never true, so the plural message is always used.

final answer
When a cashier reopens a saved draft POS Invoice, `POSInvoice.reset_mode_of_payments` (`erpnext/accounts/doctype/pos_invoice/pos_invoice.py`, around line 796) rebuilds the payments table from the POS Profile. I read the code but did not run it. **How the payments table is repopulated** 1. `reset_mode_of_payments` does nothing unless the invoice has a `pos_profile`. If it has one, it loads the profile with `frappe.get_cached_doc("POS Profile", self.pos_profile)`. 2. It passes the invoice and the profile to the shared helper `update_multi_mode_option(doc, pos_profile)`. This helper is in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/sales_invoice/services/pos.py`, around lines 339-376. 3. After the helper returns, `reset_mode_of_payments` sets `self.paid_amount = 0`. 4. The helper clears the table with `doc.set("payments", [])`. 5. It collects the `mode_of_payment` from each row of the profile's `payments` table. It then calls `get_mode_of_payments_info(modes, doc.company)` to get each mode's default account and type. 6. For each profile row, it looks up that mode's info and copies `row.default` onto it. 7. A local `append_payment` closure then adds a row to `doc.payments`. It sets `default`, `mode_of_payment` (from `payment_mode.mop`), `account` (from `default_account`) and `type`. 8. If a mode has no info, which means no default Cash or Bank account, its link is collected. After the loop the helper calls `frappe.throw` with the title "Missing Account" and a message listing those modes. 9. If the table already had rows and the doc is not marked as created using POS, the helper shows a toast. The toast says "Payment methods refreshed. Please review before proceeding." **How the helper copes with fields POS Invoice lacks** - The helper is shared with Sales Invoice. `POSService.reset_mode_of_payments`, `set_pos_fields` and `_apply_editable_pos_defaults` also call it. - `is_created_using_pos` exists only on Sales Invoice. - The helper therefore reads it with `doc.get("is_created_using_pos")` instead of `doc.is_created_using_pos`. On a POS Invoice, `get` returns `None` rather than raising `AttributeError`. - The line is `mop_refetched = bool(doc.payments) and not doc.get("is_created_using_pos")`. On a POS Invoice the flag is always falsy, so any non-empty payments table counts as refetched and triggers the toast.

final answer
`reset_mode_of_payments` clears the payments table and rebuilds it from the POS Profile's payment rows. The shared helper stays safe on POS Invoice by reading the Sales-Invoice-only field with `doc.get()` instead of attribute access. I read the code but didn't run it. **Entry points** - On POS Invoice, `reset_mode_of_payments` is a whitelisted method (`erpnext/accounts/doctype/pos_invoice/pos_invoice.py`, around line 796). - Sales Invoice has its own copy, `POSService.reset_mode_of_payments` (`erpnext/accounts/doctype/sales_invoice/services/pos.py`, around line 173), plus a wrapper in `sales_invoice.py`. - Both do the same three things: 1. If `pos_profile` is set, load it with `frappe.get_cached_doc("POS Profile", pos_profile)`. 2. Call `update_multi_mode_option(doc, pos_profile)`. 3. Set `paid_amount = 0`. **How `update_multi_mode_option` rebuilds the table** (`sales_invoice/services/pos.py`, around line 339) 1. It checks whether payments already exist and whether `is_created_using_pos` is unset. It stores that as `mop_refetched`. 2. It empties the table with `doc.set("payments", [])`. 3. It collects the `mode_of_payment` names from the profile's `payments` rows. It then calls `get_mode_of_payments_info(names, doc.company)`, which returns a dict keyed by mode of payment name. Each entry holds the company's default account (`default_account`) and the mode's `type`. The query only includes enabled modes and groups by all selected columns so Postgres accepts it. 4. For each profile row, it looks up that mode's info. - If there is no info, which means no default account for the company, it records a link to the Mode of Payment form and skips the row. - Otherwise it copies the row's `default` flag onto the info and calls `append_payment`. That adds a payments row with `default`, `mode_of_payment`, `account` and `type`. 5. If any modes were invalid, it throws a "Please set default Cash or Bank account in Mode of Payment(s) …" error titled "Missing Account". Its singular/plural check is `invalid_modes == 1`, which compares a list to an int and is never true. The plural message is therefore always used. 6. If `mop_refetched` is true, it shows an orange toast saying "Payment methods refreshed. Please review before proceeding." **Avoiding breakage on doctypes without Sales-Invoice-only fields** - `is_created_using_pos` exists on Sales Invoice but not on POS Invoice. The helper therefore uses `doc.get("is_created_using_pos")`, which returns `None` when the field is missing, instead of `doc.is_created_using_pos`, which would raise `AttributeError`. - The code comment at that line says this explicitly. - For a POS Invoice the field is always missing, so the check reduces to "payments already existed". That is why the refresh toast appears when a saved draft's payments are re-fetched.

Reading it as `doc.is_created_using_pos` would raise `AttributeError` on a POS Invoice. `doc.get("is_created_using_pos")` returns `None` instead, so `not None` is true. The refresh toast therefore fires for a POS Invoice that had existing payments.

How does ERPNext compute a transaction item's final rate from its price list rate, margin, discount and applied pricing rules — and what happens when the user manually overrides the rate? final answer
Per-item rate calculation lives in `erpnext/controllers/taxes_and_totals.py`, in the `calculate_taxes_and_totals` class. The main method is `calculate_item_rate(item)`. **Call chain** - `calculate_taxes_and_totals.calculate()` calls `calculate_item_values()`. After that it calls `calculate_net_total()`, the tax calculations, and `set_discount_amount()` / `apply_discount_amount()` for the document-level discount. - `calculate_item_values()` returns early if the document `is_consolidated` or `discount_amount_applied` is set. - Otherwise, for each item it does the following: - It calls `round_floats_in`, skipping valuation_rate, incoming_rate and sales_incoming_rate. - It calls `calculate_item_rate(item)`. - It sets `net_rate = rate`. - It sets `amount = rate * qty`. For a zero-qty return, `amount = -rate`. For a zero-qty debit note, `amount = rate`. - It sets `net_amount = amount`. - It calls `_set_in_company_currency` for price_list_rate, rate_with_margin, rate, net_rate, amount and net_amount. This produces the `base_*` fields. **`calculate_item_rate(item)`** 1. **No price list rate.** If `price_list_rate` is falsy, it calls `remove_margin` and `remove_discount`, sets `rate_with_margin = 0`, and returns. The rate is left as the user entered it. 2. **Pricing rules.** `has_pricing_rules = item.pricing_rules and not doc.ignore_pricing_rule`. - If true, it calls `remove_margin(item)`. - It then loops over `get_applied_pricing_rules(item.pricing_rules)` and loads each rule with `frappe.get_cached_doc("Pricing Rule", ...)`. - A rule that has a `margin_type` and `margin_rate_or_amount` sets `item.margin_type` and `item.margin_rate_or_amount`. This applies if the type is Percentage, or if the rule's currency matches the document's currency. - Rule discounts have already been written into `discount_percentage` and `discount_amount` on the item by the pricing-rule application code, outside this function. 3. **Margin.** `item.rate_with_margin = get_rate_with_margin(item)`. - With no `margin_type`, it returns `price_list_rate`. - With Percentage, it returns `price_list_rate * (1 + margin/100)`. - Otherwise it returns `price_list_rate + margin_rate_or_amount`. 4. **Discount.** If `discount_percentage > 0`, then `discount_amount = rate_with_margin * discount_percentage / 100`. 5. **Calculated rate.** `calculated_rate = rate_with_margin - discount_amount`, rounded to the rate precision. **Manual rate override** - If pricing rules apply, or `item.rate` is empty, then `item.rate = calculated_rate`. The calculated rate wins. - If `item.rate == calculated_rate`, nothing changes. - Otherwise the user's rate is kept and margin and discount are rewritten to explain it: - **Rate above `price_list_rate`:** `margin_type` becomes "Amount" and `margin_rate_or_amount = rate - price_list_rate`. `rate_with_margin = rate`, and `remove_discount(item)` is called. - **Rate at or below `price_list_rate`:** `rate_with_margin = price_list_rate`, `discount_amount = price_list_rate - rate`, and `discount_percentage = 0`. `remove_margin(item)` is called. **Client side** `erpnext/public/js/controllers/taxes_and_totals.js` has a JS mirror of this logic. It computes `rate_with_margin` from `price_list_rate` and `margin_rate_or_amount`, applies `discount_percentage`, and then subtracts `discount_amount`.

final answer
The per-item rate is computed in `calculate_taxes_and_totals.calculate_item_rate(item)` in `erpnext/controllers/taxes_and_totals.py`. A client-side function does a similar calculation as you edit fields. I read the code but did not run it. ## Server side (`erpnext/controllers/taxes_and_totals.py`) `calculate_item_values()` loops over `doc.items`. It returns early for consolidated documents or when `discount_amount_applied` is set. For each item it does the following: 1. It rounds the item's float fields with `doc.round_floats_in`. 2. It calls `calculate_item_rate(item)`. 3. It sets `net_rate = rate` and `amount = rate * qty`. Returns and debit notes with zero qty get special handling. 4. It fills the base-currency fields with `_set_in_company_currency`. These cover `price_list_rate`, `rate_with_margin`, `rate`, `net_rate`, `amount` and `net_amount`. `calculate_item_rate(item)` works through these steps in order: 1. **No price list rate.** If `item.price_list_rate` is falsy, it calls `remove_margin` and `remove_discount`, sets `rate_with_margin = 0`, and returns. The rate is left as the user entered it. 2. **Pricing rules.** `has_pricing_rules` is true when `item.pricing_rules` is set and `doc.ignore_pricing_rule` is not. - It first calls `remove_margin(item)`. - For each rule from `get_applied_pricing_rules`, it loads the cached Pricing Rule doc. - A rule's `margin_type` and `margin_rate_or_amount` are copied onto the item only if the rule has both. The margin type must be Percentage, or the rule's currency must match the document currency. 3. **Margin.** `item.rate_with_margin = get_rate_with_margin(item)`. - With no `margin_type`, it returns `price_list_rate`. - Percentage gives `price_list_rate * (1 + margin/100)`. - Amount gives `price_list_rate + margin`. 4. **Discount.** If `discount_percentage > 0`, then `discount_amount = rate_with_margin * discount_percentage / 100`. The discount is applied to the rate with margin, not to the bare price list rate. 5. **Calculated rate.** `calculated_rate = rate_with_margin - discount_amount`, rounded to the rate's precision. ## Manual rate override After step 5 the function compares the calculated rate with `item.rate`: - **Pricing rules apply, or `item.rate` is empty or 0.** The calculated rate wins and `item.rate = calculated_rate`. A manual override is therefore discarded when pricing rules are active. - **`item.rate == calculated_rate`.** Nothing changes. - **Manual rate differs and is above `price_list_rate`.** The user's rate is kept and the difference is stored as a margin. - `margin_type` becomes "Amount". - `margin_rate_or_amount` becomes `rate - price_list_rate`. - `rate_with_margin` becomes `rate`. - The discount is cleared with `remove_discount`. - **Manual rate differs and is at or below `price_list_rate`.** The user's rate is kept and the difference is stored as a discount. - `rate_with_margin` becomes `price_list_rate`. - `discount_amount` becomes `price_list_rate - rate`. - `discount_percentage` becomes 0. - The margin is cleared with `remove_margin`. `remove_margin` and `remove_discount` are small helpers that reset `margin_type` and `margin_rate_or_amount`, or `discount_percentage` and `discount_amount`. ## Where pricing rules come from `get_item_details` calls `get_pricing_rule_for_item` in `pricing_rule.py`, and that calls `apply_price_discount_rule`. This function writes `margin_type`, `margin_rate_or_amount`, `discount_percentage`, `discount_amount` and `price_list_rate` into the item details. A rule of type "Rate" overrides `price_list_rate` and zeroes the discount. `calculate_item_rate` then reads those fields. ## Client side `erpnext/public/js/controllers/taxes_and_totals.js` has `apply_pricing_rule_on_item(item)`, which does the same margin, then discount, then rate calculation in the browser. - It uses `blanket_order_rate` instead of `price_list_rate` for Sales Order and Quotation items when that field is set. - It then calls `frappe.model.set_value` to write `rate`. - It is triggered from the handlers in `transaction.js` for `price_list_rate`, `margin_type` and `margin_rate_or_amount`. - Those handlers then call `calculate_taxes_and_totals()`.

final answer
The per-item rate is computed in `erpnext/controllers/taxes_and_totals.py`, in `calculate_taxes_and_totals.calculate_item_rate(item)` (about L166–222). I read the code but didn't run it. **Call chain** - `calculate()` calls `_calculate()`. - `_calculate()` calls `validate_conversion_rate()`, then `calculate_item_values()`, then the tax and total steps. - `calculate_item_values()` returns early if the document is consolidated or `discount_amount_applied` is set. - For each item, `calculate_item_values()` does the following: 1. It runs `round_floats_in`. 2. It calls `calculate_item_rate(item)`. 3. It sets `net_rate = rate`. 4. It sets `amount = rate * qty`. For a return or debit note with no qty, the amount is `±rate` instead. 5. It sets `net_amount = amount`. 6. It calls `_set_in_company_currency` to fill the `base_*` fields for price_list_rate, rate_with_margin, rate, net_rate, amount and net_amount. **Inside `calculate_item_rate`** 1. **No price list rate.** If `price_list_rate` is empty, the function calls `remove_margin` and `remove_discount`, sets `rate_with_margin = 0` and returns. Any rate the user typed is kept. 2. **Pricing rules.** `has_pricing_rules` is true when `item.pricing_rules` is set and `doc.ignore_pricing_rule` is not. - In that case it calls `remove_margin(item)`. - It then loops over `get_applied_pricing_rules(item.pricing_rules)` and loads each rule with `frappe.get_cached_doc("Pricing Rule", ...)`. - A rule's margin is copied onto `item.margin_type` and `item.margin_rate_or_amount` only if the rule has both a margin type and an amount. For an amount-type margin, the rule's currency must also match the document currency. 3. **Margin.** `item.rate_with_margin = get_rate_with_margin(item)` (about L1182): - With no margin type, it returns `price_list_rate`. - For "Percentage", it returns `price_list_rate * (1 + margin_rate_or_amount / 100)`. - Otherwise it returns `price_list_rate + margin_rate_or_amount`. 4. **Discount.** If `discount_percentage > 0`, `discount_amount = rate_with_margin * discount_percentage / 100`. The percentage is applied to the rate with margin, not to the price list rate. 5. **Calculated rate.** `calculated_rate = rate_with_margin - discount_amount`. **Manual rate override** These checks run in order after the calculated rate is known: - **Use the calculated rate.** If pricing rules apply, or `item.rate` is empty or zero, then `item.rate = calculated_rate` and the function returns. Pricing rules therefore always overwrite a manual rate. - **No change.** If `item.rate == calculated_rate`, the margin and discount are consistent and the function returns. - **Rate above the price list rate.** The user's rate is kept and the difference is treated as a margin: - `margin_type` is set to "Amount". - `margin_rate_or_amount` is set to `rate - price_list_rate`. - `rate_with_margin` is set to `rate`. - `remove_discount(item)` clears the discount. - **Rate at or below the price list rate.** The user's rate is kept and the difference is treated as a discount: - `rate_with_margin` is set to `price_list_rate`. - `discount_amount` is set to `price_list_rate - rate`. - `discount_percentage` is set to 0. - `remove_margin(item)` clears the margin. The helpers `remove_margin` (sets margin type to `None` and the amount to 0) and `remove_discount` (zeroes the discount percentage and amount) are module-level functions near L1172–1180. The client-side equivalent is in `erpnext/public/js/controllers/taxes_and_totals.js`. I didn't trace it.
