final answer
The current per-item rate calculation runs in two places. The server-side `TaxesAndTotals` class in `erpnext/controllers/taxes_and_totals.py` is authoritative. The client-side `erpnext.taxes_and_totals` class in `erpnext/public/js/controllers/taxes_and_totals.js` and `transaction.js` mirrors it for live editing. I read the source below but did not run anything. ## Server side: `erpnext/controllers/taxes_and_totals.py` The call chain is `calculate_taxes_and_totals.__init__` → `calculate()` → `_calculate()` → `calculate_item_values()` → `calculate_item_rate(item)`. `calculate_item_values()` returns early if the document is consolidated or `discount_amount_applied` is set. Otherwise, for each item it does the following: - It rounds the item's float fields. - It calls `calculate_item_rate(item)`. - It sets `net_rate = rate`. - It sets `amount = rate * qty`, with special cases for returns and debit notes that have qty 0. - It sets `net_amount = amount`. - It fills the `base_*` fields through `_set_in_company_currency`, covering `price_list_rate`, `rate_with_margin`, `rate`, `net_rate`, `amount` and `net_amount`. `calculate_item_rate(item)` works through these steps: 1. **No price list rate.** `remove_margin` and `remove_discount` clear the margin and discount, and `rate_with_margin` is set to 0. The function then returns. 2. **Pricing rules applied.** `has_pricing_rules = item.pricing_rules and not doc.ignore_pricing_rule`. - If true, it calls `remove_margin(item)`. - It then loops over `get_applied_pricing_rules(item.pricing_rules)` and loads each Pricing Rule. - A rule's `margin_type` and `margin_rate_or_amount` are copied onto the item. This happens only if the rule has both, and the type is Percentage or the rule's currency matches the document currency. 3. **Margin.** `item.rate_with_margin = get_rate_with_margin(item)`. - With no margin type, it returns `price_list_rate`. - Percentage gives `price_list_rate * (1 + margin / 100)`. - Amount gives `price_list_rate + margin`. 4. **Discount.** If `discount_percentage > 0`, then `discount_amount = rate_with_margin * discount_percentage / 100`. 5. **Calculated rate.** `calculated_rate = rate_with_margin - discount_amount`. ## Manual rate override (server) The rest of `calculate_item_rate` compares the stored `item.rate` with `calculated_rate`: - **Pricing rules apply, or `rate` is 0.** The calculated rate always wins: `item.rate = calculated_rate`. A manual rate does not survive a pricing rule. - **`rate == calculated_rate`.** Nothing changes. - **`rate` differs and is above `price_list_rate`.** The user's rate is kept and treated as a margin: - `margin_type` becomes "Amount". - `margin_rate_or_amount` becomes `rate - price_list_rate`. - `rate_with_margin` becomes `rate`. - The discount is cleared with `remove_discount`. - **`rate` differs and is at or below `price_list_rate`.** The user's rate is kept and treated as a discount: - `rate_with_margin` becomes `price_list_rate`. - `discount_amount` becomes `price_list_rate - rate`. - `discount_percentage` becomes 0. - The margin is cleared with `remove_margin`. So the manual rate stays, and the margin and discount fields are rewritten to explain it. ## Client side: `taxes_and_totals.js` and `transaction.js` - **Totals entry point.** `calculate_taxes_and_totals()` calls `_calculate_taxes_and_totals()`, which calls `calculate_item_values()`. The JS version only does the following: - It rounds the item. - It sets `net_rate = rate`. - It computes `amount` and `net_amount`. - It sets `total_weight`. - It fills the base-currency fields. It does not recompute the rate, because the rate is set earlier by the field handlers. - **`apply_pricing_rule_on_item(item)`** (`taxes_and_totals.js`, line 11) does the rate derivation: - The base rate is `price_list_rate`, or `blanket_order_rate` on a Sales Order or Quotation. - `rate_with_margin` is the base rate plus the margin, as a percentage or an amount. - If `discount_percentage` is set, `discount_amount = rate_with_margin * pct / 100`. - The rate is `rate_with_margin - discount_amount`, and it is written with `frappe.model.set_value(..., "rate", ...)`. - **Handlers that call it** (`transaction.js`): - `price_list_rate()` calls `apply_pricing_rule_on_item` and then `calculate_taxes_and_totals`. - `margin_rate_or_amount()` and `margin_type()` do the same. - `apply_discount_on_item()` calls `price_list_rate()`, or zeroes the discount if there is no price list rate. - **Manual override** is the child-table `rate` handler in `TransactionController.setup`, around lines 51–98 of `transaction.js`: - If the item has a `price_list_rate` and no `blanket_order_rate`, it computes `calculated_rate` from `get_rate_with_margin` and the discount. - If that differs from the entered `item.rate`, it applies the same split as the server: - A rate above `price_list_rate` becomes an "Amount" margin, with the discount cleared. - Otherwise the margin is cleared and the difference becomes `discount_amount`, with `discount_percentage` set to 0. - It then calls `set_in_company_currency`, `set_gross_profit` and `calculate_taxes_and_totals`. The JS handler and the server's `calculate_item_rate` therefore produce the same margin and discount adjustments for an overridden rate.

final answer
When you create a Dunning from an overdue Sales Invoice, each Overdue Payment row's `outstanding` comes from the invoice's Payment Schedule row. The code overrides that value in one case, and that override is what keeps foreign-currency invoices correct. All of this is in `create_dunning` in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/sales_invoice/mapper.py` (line 570). I read the code but didn't run it or check the tests. **Row mapping** - `create_dunning` calls `get_mapped_doc` with a table map from `Payment Schedule` to `Overdue Payment`. - The map copies `name` into `payment_schedule` and `parent` into `sales_invoice`. - Only schedule rows with `outstanding > 0` and `getdate(due_date) < getdate()` are mapped. - Because the rows are mapped field by field, each Overdue Payment's `outstanding` starts as the schedule row's own `outstanding`. That value is in the invoice's transaction currency and is updated as payments are allocated. **Override in `postprocess_dunning`** - This only runs when the invoice has exactly one payment schedule row (`len(source.payment_schedule) == 1`). - The code then finds the Overdue Payment row whose `payment_schedule` matches that row's name and sets its `outstanding` as follows: - If `source.party_account_currency` is set and differs from `source.currency`, it uses `source.payment_schedule[0].outstanding`. - Otherwise it uses `source.outstanding_amount`. - Afterwards it calls `target.validate()`. **Why the currency check matters** - The invoice's `outstanding_amount` is in the party (receivable) account currency. The Overdue Payment row is in the invoice's transaction currency. - If the invoice is in USD and posts to an INR receivable account, copying `outstanding_amount` would put an INR figure in a USD row. - The payment schedule's outstanding avoids that mismatch. The code comment says it stays correct even when the invoice and its payments post at different exchange rates (#56006). **Downstream in the Dunning** - In `dunning.py`, `validate_overdue_payments` computes `row.interest = row.outstanding * daily_interest * row.overdue_days`. - `validate_totals` sets `total_outstanding` to the sum of the row outstandings. - `grand_total` is then `total_outstanding + dunning_amount`. - `validate_same_currency` is also in that file, but I didn't read it.

final answer
When you create a Dunning from a Sales Invoice, each Overdue Payment row's outstanding amount comes from the Payment Schedule row it was mapped from. The one exception is a single-row schedule on an invoice whose receivable account is in a different currency. For that case the code reads the schedule row's outstanding instead of the invoice's outstanding amount. I read this from the code in `mapper.py` only and didn't run it. **Where it happens:** `create_dunning` in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/sales_invoice/mapper.py` (lines 570–618), which calls `get_mapped_doc` and then a `postprocess_dunning` step. **Which rows are created:** each Payment Schedule row becomes an Overdue Payment row. The row's `name` maps to `payment_schedule` and its `parent` maps to `sales_invoice`. A schedule row is only included if it is overdue and unpaid: `doc.outstanding > 0 and getdate(doc.due_date) < getdate()`. **How `outstanding` is set:** 1. The mapper copies the Payment Schedule row's `outstanding` into the matching Overdue Payment field. This value is in the invoice's transaction currency. 2. `postprocess_dunning` then overrides it only when the invoice has exactly one payment schedule row. 3. If `source.party_account_currency` is set and differs from `source.currency`, `row.outstanding = source.payment_schedule[0].outstanding`. 4. Otherwise `row.outstanding = source.get("outstanding_amount")`. 5. After that it calls `target.validate()`. **Why the foreign-currency case works:** - On a Sales Invoice, `outstanding_amount` is held in the party (receivable) account currency. - The Overdue Payment row is in the invoice's transaction currency. - When the two currencies differ, copying `outstanding_amount` would put a value in the wrong currency on the row. - The Payment Schedule's `outstanding` is kept in transaction currency and updated as payments are allocated. It stays correct even when the invoice and its payments post at different exchange rates (the code comment cites #56006). **Related code:** `is_overdue` in `sales_invoice/services/status.py` applies the same currency logic. It sums `base_payment_amount` when `party_account_currency != currency`, and `payment_amount` otherwise. `update_linked_dunnings` in `dunning.py` later re-resolves each Dunning's status from the live Payment Schedule `outstanding` and the invoice's `outstanding_amount`.

final answer
When a Dunning is created from an overdue Sales Invoice, each Overdue Payment row's `outstanding` comes from one of two sources. Which one depends on whether the invoice currency matches its receivable account currency. I read the code but did not run it or the tests. **Where it happens:** `create_dunning()` in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/sales_invoice/mapper.py` (around lines 570–618). The "Fetch Overdue Payments" button in `dunning.js` calls the same method through `map_current_doc`. **Step 1: `get_mapped_doc` builds the rows.** - Each `Payment Schedule` row of the invoice becomes an `Overdue Payment` row. It is mapped only if `doc.outstanding > 0` and `due_date < today`. - The field map copies `name` into `payment_schedule` and `parent` into `sales_invoice`. - Matching fields, including the schedule's `outstanding`, are copied by default. So each row starts with that schedule row's own outstanding, which is in the invoice's transaction currency. **Step 2: `postprocess_dunning` overrides the value in one case.** - The override applies only when the invoice has exactly one payment schedule row (`len(source.payment_schedule) == 1`). - It finds the Overdue Payment row whose `payment_schedule` matches that schedule row, then picks the value: - **Currencies differ** (`source.party_account_currency` is set and not equal to `source.currency`): it uses `source.payment_schedule[0].outstanding`. - **Otherwise:** it uses `source.outstanding_amount`. - With several schedule rows, no override happens. Each row keeps its own schedule outstanding. - Afterwards it calls `target.validate()`. **Why the foreign-currency branch exists:** - `Sales Invoice.outstanding_amount` is kept in the party (receivable) account currency. - The Overdue Payment row, like the Dunning, is in the invoice's transaction currency. Copying `outstanding_amount` straight across would put the wrong currency's number in the row. - The comment in the code (issue #56006) says the payment schedule's `outstanding` stays in transaction currency and is updated as payments are allocated. So it stays correct even when the invoice and its payments post at different exchange rates. **Related checks in `dunning.py`:** - `validate()` runs `validate_same_currency`, `validate_overdue_payments`, `validate_totals`, `set_party_details` and `set_dunning_level`. - `validate_totals` sums `row.outstanding` into `total_outstanding`, and sums `row.interest` into `total_interest`. - `dunning.js` computes interest per row as `interest_per_day * overdue_days * outstanding`. - `update_linked_dunnings` (around line 207) refreshes the rows when the invoice's outstanding changes.

No indexed file uniquely matches `mapper.py`. **Blast radius — what depends on these (update/verify before editing)** - `create_return_sales_invoice` (erpnext/accounts/doctype/pos_invoice/pos_invoice.py:319) …
final answer
The outstanding amount on each Overdue Payment row is set in `create_dunning` and its inner `postprocess_dunning`, in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/sales_invoice/mapper.py` (lines 569–618). I read that code but did not run it or the tests. **Row creation.** `create_dunning` calls `get_mapped_doc` to turn a Sales Invoice into a Dunning. Each Payment Schedule row becomes an Overdue Payment row, but only if its `outstanding > 0` and its `due_date` is before today. The mapping copies `payment_schedule` (the schedule row's name) and `sales_invoice` (its parent) onto the row. The Payment Schedule's `outstanding` field is presumably mapped onto the row's `outstanding` by matching field names, though I didn't check the Overdue Payment doctype to confirm. That is the default value when the invoice has several schedule rows. **Override in `postprocess_dunning`.** This applies only when the invoice has exactly one payment schedule row. For the Overdue Payment row whose `payment_schedule` matches that schedule row, `postprocess_dunning` sets `row.outstanding` in one of two ways: - **Normal case.** If the party account currency is empty or equals the invoice currency, it uses the invoice's `outstanding_amount`. - **Currency mismatch.** If `source.party_account_currency` is set and differs from `source.currency`, it uses `source.payment_schedule[0].outstanding`. After that, `target.validate()` runs. **Why the mismatch case uses the schedule row.** The code comment cites issue #56006. On the invoice, `outstanding_amount` is held in the party (receivable) account currency. An Overdue Payment row, however, is in the invoice's transaction currency. For example, an invoice in USD posted against a EUR receivable account has an `outstanding_amount` in EUR. Copying that straight into the USD row would give the wrong figure. The comment says the payment schedule's `outstanding` is kept in transaction currency and is updated as payments are allocated. That keeps it correct even when the invoice and its payments post at different exchange rates. **Downstream checks in `dunning.py`.** - `validate_same_currency` requires every linked invoice's `currency` to equal the Dunning's `currency`. So the Dunning is in the invoice's transaction currency, which is why the row amount must be in that currency too. - `validate_overdue_payments` computes each row's `interest = outstanding * daily_interest * overdue_days`. - `validate_totals` sets `total_outstanding` to the sum of the row `outstanding` values and `grand_total` to that total plus `dunning_amount`. **Multi-row invoices.** When the invoice has more than one payment schedule row, the single-row override in `postprocess_dunning` doesn't run. Each row then keeps its own schedule `outstanding`, which is already in transaction currency.

final answer
All three are in `erpnext/stock/doctype/item/item.py`. Each is decorated with `@frappe.whitelist()` and does an explicit `frappe.has_permission` check as its first step. - **`get_item_attribute(parent, attribute_value="")`** (attribute autocomplete) - It calls `frappe.has_permission("Item")`. With no ptype given, this checks the default `read` permission on the Item doctype. - If the check fails, it calls `frappe.throw(_("No Permission"))`. - It then runs `frappe.get_all("Item Attribute Value", ...)`. `get_all` skips permission checks, so the guard above is the only protection. - It checks doctype-level access only. It doesn't check access to a specific Item or to the `parent` Item Attribute. - **`get_item_prices(item_code)`** (price preview) - It calls `frappe.has_permission("Item Price", "read")`. - On failure it calls `frappe.throw(_("Not permitted"), frappe.PermissionError)`. - It checks read permission on Item Price, not on Item. This makes sense because it returns Item Price data. - The query uses `frappe.qb`, which also skips permissions. So the explicit check is the only guard. - The query is limited to 11 rows, 10 of which are returned, and it filters out cancelled and expired prices. - **`make_opening_stock_entry(item_code, company, qty, valuation_rate, warehouse=None)`** (opening stock creation) - It calls `frappe.has_permission("Item", "write", item_code)`. This is a write check on that specific Item document, so document-level rules such as user permissions apply. - On failure it calls `frappe.throw(_("Not permitted"), frappe.PermissionError)`. - After the permission check, it validates the request in this order: - The item must be a stock item. - The item must have neither serial nor batch numbers. - No stock ledger entries may already exist for it. - `qty` must be greater than 0. - `valuation_rate` must not be negative. - If a warehouse is given, it must belong to `company`. - A Temporary account must exist for the company. - Only then does it call `create_opening_stock_reconciliation`. - This function doesn't check the user's permission on Stock Reconciliation. Whether the reconciliation creation itself enforces that permission depends on `create_opening_stock_reconciliation`, which I didn't read.

final answer
All three whitelisted functions are in `erpnext/stock/doctype/item/item.py`. Each starts with an explicit `frappe.has_permission` check and throws if it fails. I read the code but didn't run it. - **Attribute autocomplete, `get_item_attribute(parent, attribute_value="")`:** - It calls `frappe.has_permission("Item")`. With no ptype given, that checks the default read permission on the Item doctype. - If the check fails, it throws `_("No Permission")`. - Only then does it run `frappe.get_all("Item Attribute Value", …)` with a `like` filter on `attribute_value`. - The guard is on the Item doctype only. It doesn't check Item Attribute and isn't tied to a specific document. - **Item price preview, `get_item_prices(item_code)`:** - It checks `frappe.has_permission("Item Price", "read")`. - If the check fails, it throws `_("Not permitted")` with `frappe.PermissionError`. - The guard is on the Item Price doctype, not on Item. The query is a `frappe.qb` query, which doesn't apply permission filtering itself, so this up-front check is the only protection. - The query excludes cancelled records (`docstatus != 2`) and expired prices. It returns at most 10 rows plus a `has_more` flag. - **Opening stock creation, `make_opening_stock_entry(item_code, company, qty, valuation_rate, warehouse=None)`:** - It checks `frappe.has_permission("Item", "write", item_code)`. This is a write check on the specific Item document, and it throws `_("Not permitted")` with `frappe.PermissionError` if it fails. - After that it runs business validations. The item must be a stock item and can't have serial or batch numbers. No stock ledger entries may exist yet. Quantity must be positive, valuation rate can't be negative, and the warehouse must belong to the company. A Temporary account must also exist for the company. - It then calls `create_opening_stock_reconciliation`, which is not whitelisted. That function inserts and submits a Stock Reconciliation with the "Opening Stock" purpose. - The write check is on Item. I didn't see a separate permission check on Stock Reconciliation in this path. - A second path, `Item.set_opening_stock()`, calls the same helper when an Item is saved. It isn't directly callable from the client, so it relies on the normal document save permissions.

All three whitelisted functions are in `erpnext/stock/doctype/item/item.py`. Each one checks permission itself with `frappe.has_permission` and throws if the check fails. - **Attribute autocomplete, `get_item_attribute(parent, attribute_value="")`:** - It checks `frappe.has_permission("Item")`. With no ptype given, this defaults to read permission at the doctype level. - If the check fails, it calls `frappe.throw(_("No Permission"))`. - After that it queries `Item Attribute Value` with `frappe.get_all`, filtering on `parent` and a `like` match on `attribute_value`. - The guard is the permission check on Item. `get_all` itself does not apply permissions. - **Item price preview, `get_item_prices(item_code)`:** - It checks `frappe.has_permission("Item Price", "read")`. - If the check fails, it throws `frappe.PermissionError` with the message "Not permitted". - The guard is on the Item Price doctype, not Item, because the data comes from there. - The query uses `frappe.qb`, which bypasses permissions, so the check at the top is the only guard. - The query is restricted to the given `item_code`, excludes cancelled records (`docstatus != 2`) and expired prices, and returns at most 10 rows plus a `has_more` flag. - **Opening stock creation, `make_opening_stock_entry(item_code, company, qty, valuation_rate, warehouse=None)`:** - It checks `frappe.has_permission("Item", "write", item_code)`. The warehouse must belong to the company. I only read the code down to the account lookup at line 1668 and did not read the rest of the function. I did not check whether it checks permission on the documents it creates after that point, such as the Stock Reconciliation. The guards differ in two ways: - Autocomplete and price preview are read-only and use the read check (`"read"` by default for autocomplete, explicit `"read"` for prices). - Opening stock creates records, so it needs write permission on the specific item. Two of them also raise `frappe.PermissionError`, which returns an HTTP 403. The autocomplete guard raises a generic `frappe.throw`.

final answer
A reservation is a submitted **Stock Reservation Entry** (SRE). Available-to-promise is actual stock minus the open SRE quantity. The stock ledger refuses any outflow that would eat into that reserved quantity. ## Where the promise is recorded - **The doctype** is Stock Reservation Entry. The class `StockReservationEntry` is in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_reservation_entry/stock_reservation_entry.py`. - **What each entry holds:** `item_code` and `warehouse`, the source `voucher_type`, `voucher_no` and `voucher_detail_no` (usually a Sales Order row), and `reserved_qty`. It also tracks `delivered_qty`, `transferred_qty`, `consumed_qty` and `status`. Serial and batch reservations are held in child entries. - **Creation:** - `create_stock_reservation_entries_for_so_items` creates entries for a Sales Order's items. The Sales Order side lives in `erpnext/selling/doctype/sales_order/services/reservation.py`. - A helper class in the same file, using `sre.reserved_qty = self.qty_to_be_reserved` (around line 1220), builds the entries. - `validate_stock_reservation_settings` checks that reservation is enabled in Stock Settings. - **Entry checks:** - `validate` runs `validate_reservation_based_on_qty` and `validate_with_allowed_qty`. - `validate_with_allowed_qty` caps a new reservation at `min(available_qty, voucher_qty - delivered - already_reserved)`. - **On submit, cancel and delivery:** - `on_submit` calls `update_reserved_qty_in_voucher` and `update_reserved_qty_in_pick_list`. Both write `stock_reserved_qty` on the source document. - `update_status` sets the status. - `cancel_stock_reservation_entries` releases the reservation. - `update_delivered_qty` advances `delivered_qty` as goods ship, so the reservation is consumed. ## How "still available to promise" is calculated - **Open reserved quantity** is `reserved_qty - delivered_qty - transferred_qty - consumed_qty` over submitted entries (`docstatus == 1`) where `delivered_qty < reserved_qty`. - **`get_available_qty_to_reserve(item_code, warehouse, batch_no, ignore_sre)`** returns `get_stock_balance(...) - open reserved quantity`. - For batch items it uses `get_batch_qty` instead. - It locks the SRE rows with `for_update` (on the Bin row for Postgres), so two concurrent reservations can't both take the same stock. - **Serial and batch items:** `get_available_serial_nos_to_reserve`, `get_sre_reserved_serial_nos_details` and `get_sre_reserved_batch_nos_details` handle these. - **Aggregates:** - `get_sre_reserved_qty_for_item_and_warehouse` totals open reservations for one item and warehouse. - `get_sre_reserved_qty_for_items_and_warehouses` does the same in bulk. - `get_sre_reserved_qty_details_for_voucher` and `get_sre_reserved_qty_for_voucher_detail_no` give per-voucher figures. - **Bin cache:** `Bin.update_reserved_stock` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/bin/bin.py` stores the total in `Bin.reserved_stock`. The stock balance report, the item dashboard and stock reconciliation read the same SRE helpers. ## How an unrelated shipment is blocked All of this is in `erpnext/stock/stock_ledger.py`: - **Reading the reserved quantity:** `update_entries_after.get_reserved_stock` sums open SRE quantity for the item and warehouse as of the posting time. It is stored as `self.reserved_stock`. `get_sle_by_args` reads it from `Bin.reserved_stock`. - **Blocking the outflow:** - When `reserved_stock` is non-zero, `validate_negative_qty_in_future_sle` calls `validate_reserved_stock(args)`. - `validate_reserved_stock` computes `get_stock_balance(...) - reserved_stock`. If the result is negative, it throws "units needed…" with the title "Reserved Stock". - The Reserved Stock validation in the stock ledger handles plain quantities. The batch and serial checks are separate. - **Negative-stock messages:** around line 1860 the error is extended with "As N units are reserved for other sales orders, you are allowed to consume only X units." - **Serial and batch:** - `validate_reserved_serial_nos` throws "Serial Nos are reserved in Stock Reservation Entries…" with the title "Reserved Serial No.". - `validate_reserved_batch_nos` compares each batch's available quantity with its reserved quantity and throws with the title "Reserved Stock for Batch". I did not trace how the customer's own Delivery Note is exempted from this block. I'd expect that to happen through the SRE's `delivered_qty` and `status` updates, but I haven't confirmed it.

final answer
Promised stock is recorded in **Stock Reservation Entry** (SRE) documents. Availability is actual stock minus open reservations. The stock ledger refuses any outflow that would eat into the reserved quantity. I read the code paths below but did not run any of them. ## Where the promise is recorded - **Doctype:** the `StockReservationEntry` class is in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_reservation_entry/stock_reservation_entry.py`. - Each submitted SRE holds the item, warehouse, optional batch or serial numbers, and the `voucher_type`, `voucher_no` and `voucher_detail_no` of the document that made the promise. For a sales order, that is the Sales Order and its item row. - It also holds `reserved_qty` and the running counters `delivered_qty`, `transferred_qty` and `consumed_qty`. It has a `reservation_based_on` setting of Qty or Serial and Batch. - **Creating SREs:** - `create_stock_reservation_entries_for_so_items` creates them for Sales Order items. - `StockReservation.make_stock_reservation_entries` and `make_stock_reservation_entry` create them for other source documents. - `StockReservation.transfer_reservation_entries_to` moves a reservation to a work order, subcontracting order or production plan. - **Validation before submit:** `before_submit` and `on_update_after_submit` run `validate_reservation_based_on_serial_and_batch`, `validate_reservation_based_on_qty` and `validate_with_allowed_qty`. These make sure you cannot reserve more than is free. - **Bin total:** `update_reserved_stock_in_bin` calls `bin_doc.update_reserved_stock()`, so the Bin keeps a `reserved_stock` figure for the item and warehouse. ## How "available to promise" is calculated - `get_available_qty_to_reserve(item_code, warehouse, batch_no, ignore_sre)` in `stock_reservation_entry.py` returns actual qty minus reserved qty. - Actual qty comes from `get_stock_balance` in `erpnext/stock/utils.py`. For a batch it comes from `get_batch_qty` instead. - Reserved qty is `SUM(reserved_qty − delivered_qty − transferred_qty − consumed_qty)` over submitted SREs for that item and warehouse that are not fully delivered. - The query takes a `for_update` lock, with a postgres variant that locks the Bin row first. This stops two concurrent reservations from over-promising. - `StockReservation.get_available_qty_to_reserve` is a similar method with no locking and no batch handling. - `get_sre_reserved_qty_for_item_and_warehouse`, `get_sre_reserved_qty_for_items_and_warehouses` and `get_sre_reserved_qty_details_for_voucher` are helpers that return the reserved totals. - `update_status` and `update_reserved_qty_in_voucher` keep the status and quantities in step as deliveries happen. ## How an unrelated shipment is blocked All of this is in `erpnext/stock/stock_ledger.py`. - **Qty-based items:** - `update_entries_after.__init__` calls `get_reserved_stock()`. That sums the open SRE quantity for the item and warehouse, only for SREs created at or before the posting time. - `validate_negative_stock` then computes `qty_after_transaction + sle.actual_qty − reserved_stock`. If the result is negative, the entry is rejected. - The error message is extended with "units are reserved for other sales orders, you are allowed to consume only N units", or "the full stock is reserved…". This is the check that blocks an unrelated Delivery Note or Stock Entry. - **Direct check:** in the same file, `validate_negative_qty_in_future_sle` calls `validate_reserved_stock(args)` when `args.reserved_stock` is set. That function raises "units needed … to complete this transaction" if the balance minus the reserved qty is below zero. - **Serial and batch items:** `validate_reserved_serial_nos` and `validate_reserved_batch_nos` use `get_sre_reserved_serial_nos_details` and `get_sre_reserved_batch_nos_details`. They block use of specific reserved serial numbers, or of the reserved share of a batch. - **Stock Reconciliation:** `StockReconciliation.validate_reserved_stock` (called from `validate` and `on_cancel`) refuses to change quantities for item and warehouse pairs that have reservations. - **Consuming your own reservation:** - The document that owns the reservation can still use it. `get_reserved_materials` and `StockEntrySABB.get_available_reserved_materials` (in `stock_entry/services/serial_batch.py`) supply the reserved serial and batch numbers to a Work Order or Subcontracting stock entry. - Delivery then raises `delivered_qty` on the SRE, which shrinks the reservation. ## Related but separate `get_pos_reserved_qty` in `pos_invoice.py` handles stock held by unconsolidated POS invoices. It is a different mechanism from SREs.

final answer
The promise is recorded as a submitted **Stock Reservation Entry** (SRE). The available-to-promise figure is actual stock minus the open reserved quantity. The stock ledger check then blocks any other outgoing transaction that would eat into that reserved quantity. **1. Where the promise is recorded** - The SRE doctype is in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_reservation_entry/stock_reservation_entry.py`. - Each SRE holds the item, warehouse, `reserved_qty`, and the counters `delivered_qty`, `transferred_qty` and `consumed_qty`. It also links back to the voucher (for example the Sales Order) and carries `reservation_based_on` (Qty or Serial and Batch). - A serial/batch reservation stores its serial and batch numbers in `Serial and Batch Entry` rows under the SRE. - The `StockReservation` helper class in the same file creates and cancels these entries through `make_stock_reservation_entries`, `cancel_stock_reservation_entries`, `get_items_to_reserve` and `update_delivered_qty`. The SRE's own `validate_reserved_entries` and its `on_submit`/`on_cancel` hooks run around that. - Every SRE submit, cancel or update calls `update_reserved_stock_in_bin` (around line 523), which calls `Bin.update_reserved_stock` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/bin/bin.py`. That function recomputes `Bin.reserved_stock` using `get_sre_reserved_qty_for_item_and_warehouse`. The Bin therefore holds a running total of the promised quantity. **2. How "available to promise" is calculated** - `get_available_qty_to_reserve(item_code, warehouse, batch_no, ignore_sre)` in the SRE file returns actual qty minus reserved qty. - Actual qty comes from `get_stock_balance`, or from `get_batch_qty` when a batch is given. - Reserved qty is the sum of `reserved_qty - delivered_qty - transferred_qty - consumed_qty` over submitted SREs for that item and warehouse that are not fully delivered. - The query uses `FOR UPDATE` row locks so two concurrent reservations can't both claim the same stock. - The serial-number equivalent is `get_available_serial_nos_to_reserve`. The SRE class also has a method version, `get_available_qty_to_reserve`. - The helpers `get_sre_reserved_qty_for_item_and_warehouse`, `get_sre_reserved_serial_nos_details` and `get_sre_reserved_batch_nos_details` give the reserved totals. The Stock Balance report uses `get_sre_reserved_qty_details`, and the Reserved Stock report is `erpnext/stock/report/reserved_stock`. **3. How an unrelated shipment is stopped** - The stock ledger code is in `erpnext/stock/stock_ledger.py`. In `update_entries_after`, `get_reserved_stock` (around line 631) sums the open SRE qty for the item and warehouse into `self.reserved_stock`. - The negative-stock check (around line 1357) compares `qty_after_transaction + actual_qty - reserved_stock`. A delivery that would dip into the reserved quantity counts as a shortfall. The error message then says the units are "reserved for other sales orders, you are allowed to consume only N units", or that the full stock is reserved. - When posting a stock ledger entry, `validate_negative_qty_in_future_sle` (around line 2399) calls `validate_reserved_stock(args)` if `reserved_stock` is set. It throws "Reserved Stock" when `get_stock_balance` minus reserved is below zero. - Serial and batch items get stricter checks. `validate_reserved_serial_nos` throws "Serial Nos are reserved in Stock Reservation Entries, you need to unreserve them before proceeding." `validate_reserved_batch_nos` checks batch available qty minus reserved qty. `validate_reserved_batches` in `serial_batch_bundle_service.py` adds the same kind of check. - `StockReconciliation.validate_reserved_stock` in `stock_reconciliation.py` stops a reconciliation from reducing stock below the reserved quantity. - The voucher that the stock was promised to is not blocked. Its own delivery raises `delivered_qty` on the SRE, which frees the reservation as the goods leave.

final answer
ERPNext records the promise as a **Stock Reservation Entry** (SRE). Available-to-promise is actual stock minus open reservations. The Stock Ledger check stops any other shipment that would dip into reserved stock. I read most of this in source. I did not open `Bin.update_reserved_stock` or the place where `args.reserved_stock` is filled in, so those two points are inferred. ## Where the promise is recorded **`erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_reservation_entry/stock_reservation_entry.py`**, class `StockReservationEntry`: - Each submitted SRE ties an `item_code` and `warehouse` to a `voucher_type` / `voucher_no` / `voucher_detail_no`, for example a Sales Order and its item row. It can also be tied to a Work Order, Production Plan, or Subcontracting order. - It stores `reserved_qty`, `delivered_qty`, `transferred_qty`, `consumed_qty`, a `status`, and optionally batch or serial entries in `sb_entries`. These are used when `reservation_based_on` is "Serial and Batch". - `on_submit` and `on_cancel` call: - `update_reserved_qty_in_voucher` - `update_reserved_qty_in_pick_list` - `update_status` - `update_reserved_stock_in_bin` - `update_reserved_stock_in_bin` (line 523) gets the Bin with `get_or_make_bin` and calls `Bin.update_reserved_stock()` in `bin.py`. That keeps a Bin-level reserved-stock figure in sync. - The SREs are created from a Sales Order by: - `SalesOrderStockReservation.create_stock_reservation_entries` in `erpnext/selling/doctype/sales_order/services/reservation.py` - `create_stock_reservation_entries_for_so_items` - the `StockReservation` class, which also handles Pick List, Purchase Receipt, Work Order, and Production Plan. - `SalesOrderStockReservation.has_unreserved_stock` and `get_unreserved_qty` work out what is still unreserved on a Sales Order row. The formula is `stock_qty - delivered_qty*conversion_factor - already reserved`. - Reservation only happens if "Enable Stock Reservation" is on. `SalesOrderStockReservation.validate_reserved_stock` clears the `reserve_stock` flag on rows when it is off or the item is not a stock item. - `StockSettings.validate_stock_reservation` stops you enabling "Allow Negative Stock" while reservation is enabled. ## How "available to promise" is calculated `get_available_qty_to_reserve(item_code, warehouse, batch_no=None, ignore_sre=None)` in the same file (line 693) returns Actual Qty − Reserved Qty: - **Batch case:** it returns `get_batch_qty(...)`, with the SRE named in `ignore_sre` excluded. - **Non-batch case:** it starts from `get_stock_balance(item_code, warehouse)`. - **Reserved total:** it then subtracts `Sum(reserved_qty - delivered_qty - transferred_qty - consumed_qty)`. That sum covers submitted SREs for the item and warehouse where `delivered_qty < reserved_qty`. - **Locking:** the query runs `FOR UPDATE`, so two concurrent reservations can't both claim the same stock. On Postgres it locks the Bin row first. The Bin keeps its own `reserved_stock` figure via `Bin.update_reserved_stock`. Separately, `get_reserved_qty` and `update_bin_qty` in `erpnext/stock/stock_balance.py` handle the older Sales Order `reserved_qty` figure. ## How it blocks an unrelated shipment The block is in `erpnext/stock/stock_ledger.py`: - When a Stock Ledger Entry posts, `validate_negative_qty_in_future_sle(args, ...)` runs the normal negative-stock checks. - If `args.reserved_stock` is set, it then calls `validate_reserved_stock(args)` (line 2470). - `validate_reserved_stock` computes `balance_qty = get_stock_balance(item, warehouse)` and `diff = balance_qty - reserved_stock`. If `diff` is negative, it throws "{n} units of {item} needed in {warehouse} … to complete this transaction" with the title "Reserved Stock". So an outbound movement can only use the unreserved part of the balance. The same logic exists in `StockReconciliation.validate_reserved_stock`, so a reconciliation can't cut stock below what is reserved. Reservations for the right customer are consumed as that customer's delivery posts. The `delivered_qty` and `status` update on the SRE, and `update_unreserved_qty_in_sre` handles the reverse when a delivery is cancelled. The Bin figure is refreshed again in both cases.
Q14Group C — Semantic gap (no keyword overlap with the code)When item prices in ERPNext already include tax, how does the system work backwards to determine the pre-tax amount of each line? …
When item prices in ERPNext already include tax, how does the system work backwards to determine the pre-tax amount of each line? Explain where this happens and how it handles multiple inclusive taxes, compounding tax rows, per-quantity taxes and deduction-type taxes.

final answer
When item prices already include tax, ERPNext backs the tax out of each line in `TaxesAndTotals.determine_exclusive_rate()`. This is in `erpnext/controllers/taxes_and_totals.py`. `_calculate()` calls it (line 84) before the net totals and the taxes are computed. **Entry check.** The function returns immediately unless at least one row in `taxes` has `included_in_print_rate` set. **Per-item loop.** - It runs once for every item. - It loads the item's `item_tax_rate` JSON through `_load_item_tax_rate`. - It walks every tax row in order and calls `get_current_tax_fraction(tax, item_tax_map)` for each. That call returns two values: - a rate fraction (`tax_fraction_for_current_item`), and - a fixed amount per unit of quantity. **Rate used.** `_get_tax_rate` supplies the rate. - If the item has an override for that row's `account_head` in `item_tax_rate`, that rate is used. - Otherwise the row's own `tax.rate` is used. - If the override is `NOT_APPLICABLE_TAX`, the row contributes nothing for that item. **How each row type contributes** (only rows with `included_in_print_rate` count): - **On Net Total:** the fraction is `rate/100`. - **On Previous Row Amount (compounding):** the fraction is `rate/100` times the fraction of the row at `row_id`. That row's fraction is `tax_fraction_for_current_item`. - **On Previous Row Total (compounding):** the fraction is `rate/100` times `grand_total_fraction_for_current_item` of the referenced row. - **On Item Quantity (per-quantity):** there is no fraction. The fixed per-unit amount is the rate itself, `inclusive_tax_amount_per_qty = tax_rate`. - **Deduct:** if `add_deduct_tax == "Deduct"`, both the fraction and the per-quantity amount are multiplied by -1. **Running totals across rows.** - `grand_total_fraction_for_current_item` starts at `1 + fraction` for the first row. - For later rows it is the previous row's value plus the current row's fraction. This is what "On Previous Row Total" refers to. - `cumulated_tax_fraction` is the sum of all the fractions. - `total_inclusive_tax_amount_per_qty` is the sum of each row's per-quantity amount times `item.qty`. **Resulting formula.** This only runs if `discount_amount_applied` is false, `item.qty` is non-zero, and there is some inclusive tax (a non-zero fraction or per-quantity amount). Then: `net_amount = (item.amount − total_inclusive_tax_amount_per_qty) / (1 + cumulated_tax_fraction)` - The unrounded value is kept in `item._unrounded_net_amount`. - `item.net_amount` is that value rounded to the field's precision. - `item.net_rate = net_amount / qty`, also rounded. - The base-currency versions are set with `_set_in_company_currency`. **Why this handles each case.** - **Multiple inclusive taxes:** their fractions add up, so one division strips all of them at once. - **Compounding rows:** the fractions are multiplied through the previous-row references, so a tax on a tax is accounted for. - **Per-quantity taxes:** they are fixed amounts, so they are subtracted before the division. Dividing them by `1 + fraction` would be wrong. - **Deductions:** the sign flip makes them reduce the cumulated fraction and the fixed amount. I did not read `calculate_taxes()` (line 418). It is where the actual tax amounts are computed afterwards, using the resulting `net_amount`.

final answer
The back-calculation is in `erpnext/controllers/taxes_and_totals.py`, in the class `calculate_taxes_and_totals`. `determine_exclusive_rate` does the per-item work and `get_current_tax_fraction` supplies the per-tax numbers. I read both in the source. I did not run any examples. **Where it sits in the flow** - `determine_exclusive_rate` returns immediately if no tax row has `included_in_print_rate` set. - `_calculate` runs it before the normal forward tax calculation. - A client-side copy of the same logic is in `erpnext/public/js/controllers/taxes_and_totals.js`. It has the same `determine_exclusive_rate` and `get_current_tax_fraction`. **Per item, in `determine_exclusive_rate`** 1. It loads the item's `item_tax_rate` JSON as `item_tax_map`, so item-specific rates override the row's rate. 2. It loops over every tax row in order. For each row it calls `get_current_tax_fraction(tax, item_tax_map)`. That returns two values: - `tax_fraction_for_current_item`: the tax as a fraction of the net amount. - `inclusive_tax_amount_per_qty`: a fixed amount per unit. 3. It keeps a running `grand_total_fraction_for_current_item` on each row. Row 0 is `1 + its fraction`. Each later row is `previous row's grand_total_fraction + its own fraction`. 4. It sums the fractions into `cumulated_tax_fraction`. It sums `inclusive_tax_amount_per_qty × item.qty` into `total_inclusive_tax_amount_per_qty`. 5. It then solves for the net amount: - `amount = item.amount − total_inclusive_tax_amount_per_qty` - `_unrounded_net_amount = amount / (1 + cumulated_tax_fraction)` 6. It rounds that into `item.net_amount`, sets `item.net_rate = net_amount / qty`, and converts both to company currency with `_set_in_company_currency`. 7. It skips this step when `discount_amount_applied` is true, or when `qty` is 0, or when both accumulators are 0. **How each case is handled (`get_current_tax_fraction`)** - **Multiple inclusive taxes:** Only rows with `included_in_print_rate` contribute. Their fractions add up, so the net amount is `amount / (1 + r1 + r2 + …)`. A row that is not inclusive contributes 0. A row whose rate comes back as `NOT_APPLICABLE_TAX` for that item (from `_get_tax_rate`) is skipped for that item. - **On Net Total:** The fraction is `rate/100`. - **Compounding rows:** - "On Previous Row Amount" uses `rate/100 × (the referenced row's tax_fraction_for_current_item)`. - "On Previous Row Total" uses `rate/100 × (the referenced row's grand_total_fraction_for_current_item)`. This is why the running grand-total fraction is kept per row. - The referenced row is `taxes[row_id − 1]`. - `validate_inclusive_tax` in `erpnext/accounts/services/taxes.py` enforces the dependencies. A "Previous Row Amount" row needs the referenced row to be inclusive too. A "Previous Row Total" row needs every earlier row to be inclusive. "Actual" charges and Valuation-category rows cannot be inclusive. - **Per-quantity taxes ("On Item Quantity"):** The rate is a fixed amount per unit, not a percentage. The fraction stays 0 and `inclusive_tax_amount_per_qty = rate`. It is multiplied by `qty` and subtracted from the line amount before dividing by `1 + fractions`. - **Deduction-type taxes:** If `add_deduct_tax == "Deduct"`, both the fraction and the per-qty amount are multiplied by −1. A deduction therefore lowers the cumulative fraction, so the net amount comes out higher than the inclusive price. **After the net amounts are found** - `get_current_tax_and_net_amount` runs during the normal tax pass. For inclusive "On Net Total" rows it uses `item._unrounded_net_amount` to avoid rounding twice. - `adjust_grand_total_for_inclusive_tax` then computes `grand_total_diff`. This is a small rounding difference, kept only if it is at most 5 / 10^(tax_amount precision). It is applied to the last tax row so the grand total matches the inclusive price.

The back-calculation is done in `determine_exclusive_rate()` in `erpnext/controllers/taxes_and_totals.py` (line 303). I read that function and its helpers. I did not read the later steps in `calculate_taxes`, `adjust_grand_total_for_inclusive_tax` or `apply_discount_amount`, so what I say about them comes from their names and the call graph. I also did not check the `calculate()` order or the Payment Entry copy of the function. **Where it sits** - `calculate_taxes_and_totals.calculate()` runs `determine_exclusive_rate()` early in the pipeline, before `calculate_net_total` and `calculate_taxes`. - The function does nothing unless at least one tax row has `included_in_print_rate` set. - `payment_entry.py:1486` has its own `determine_exclusive_rate`, which I did not read. **What it does for each item** 1. It loads the item's per-item tax overrides with `_load_item_tax_rate(item.item_tax_rate)`. 2. It loops over every tax row and calls `get_current_tax_fraction(tax, item_tax_map)`. That returns two values: - a percentage-based `tax_fraction_for_current_item` - a fixed `inclusive_tax_amount_per_qty` 3. It then solves for the net amount: - `amount = item.amount − Σ(inclusive_tax_amount_per_qty × item.qty)` - `_unrounded_net_amount = amount / (1 + Σ tax_fraction)` - `net_amount` is that value rounded to the field's precision. - `net_rate = net_amount / qty`, rounded. - Both are also converted to company currency with `_set_in_company_currency`. 4. This step is skipped when `discount_amount_applied` is set, when `qty` is 0, or when both sums are 0. The discount-amount case is handled later by `apply_discount_amount` and `adjust_grand_total_for_inclusive_tax`. **How each case is handled** (in `get_current_tax_fraction`) - **Several inclusive taxes:** each inclusive row adds its fraction to `cumulated_tax_fraction`. The divisor is therefore `1 + sum of all fractions`. A row that is not inclusive contributes 0. - **Item-specific rates:** `_get_tax_rate` uses the rate from `item_tax_rate` for that account head, or `tax.rate` if there is none. If the item's rate is `NOT_APPLICABLE_TAX`, the row contributes nothing. - **On Net Total:** the fraction is `rate/100`. - **Compounding rows:** - On Previous Row Amount: the fraction is `rate/100 × (tax_fraction_for_current_item of the row at row_id)`. - On Previous Row Total: the fraction is `rate/100 × (grand_total_fraction_for_current_item of the row at row_id)`. - `grand_total_fraction_for_current_item` is a running total. Row 0 is `1 + its fraction`. Each later row is the previous row's value plus its own fraction. - **Per-quantity taxes (On Item Quantity):** these have no percentage. The fixed `tax_rate` per unit goes into `inclusive_tax_amount_per_qty`. It is multiplied by `qty` and subtracted from the line amount before dividing. - **Deduct taxes:** if `add_deduct_tax == "Deduct"`, both the fraction and the per-qty amount are multiplied by −1. A deduct row therefore reduces the divisor (or adds back to the numerator), so the net amount comes out higher. **What happens afterwards** `calculate_taxes` then computes the actual tax amounts from the `net_amount` values that `determine_exclusive_rate` set. `adjust_grand_total_for_inclusive_tax` looks like it corrects rounding differences so the grand total matches the original inclusive amounts.

final answer
The back-calculation happens in `determine_exclusive_rate()` in `erpnext/controllers/taxes_and_totals.py`. It's a method of the `calculate_taxes_and_totals` class and runs inside `_calculate()`. I read the Python server-side code in full. I only read the JS mirror's `determine_exclusive_rate` body, not its `get_current_tax_fraction`. **Where it sits in the flow** `_calculate()` runs in this order: 1. `calculate_item_values` 2. `update_item_tax_map` 3. `initialize_taxes` 4. `determine_exclusive_rate` 5. `calculate_net_total` 6. `calculate_taxes` 7. `adjust_grand_total_for_inclusive_tax` 8. `calculate_totals` The same logic is mirrored client-side in `erpnext/public/js/controllers/taxes_and_totals.js`. Payment Entry has a simplified version in `payment_entry.py`. That version uses the `included_in_paid_amount` flag and has no per-item or per-quantity handling. **How `determine_exclusive_rate` works** 1. It returns immediately unless at least one tax row has `included_in_print_rate` set. 2. For each item, it loads the item's tax map from `item.item_tax_rate` using `_load_item_tax_rate`. This is parsed JSON of `{account_head: rate}`, so a per-item rate can override the rate on the tax row. `_get_tax_rate` does the lookup and can return `NOT_APPLICABLE_TAX`. 3. For each tax row, it calls `get_current_tax_fraction(tax, item_tax_map)`. That returns two values: - `tax_fraction_for_current_item`: the tax as a fraction of the net amount. - `inclusive_tax_amount_per_qty`: a fixed amount per unit. 4. It also builds `grand_total_fraction_for_current_item` for each row. For row 0 this is `1 + fraction`. For later rows it is the previous row's value plus this row's fraction. This running total is what "On Previous Row Total" rows use. 5. It sums the fractions into `cumulated_tax_fraction` and the fixed amounts into `total_inclusive_tax_amount_per_qty += per_qty * item.qty`. 6. It then calculates the net amount, provided no discount has been applied yet and the item has a quantity: - `amount = item.amount - total_inclusive_tax_amount_per_qty` - `item._unrounded_net_amount = amount / (1 + cumulated_tax_fraction)` - `net_amount` is that value rounded to the field precision. - `net_rate = net_amount / qty`. - Both are converted to company currency with `_set_in_company_currency`. The unrounded net amount is kept so that `calculate_taxes` can later derive each row's tax amount without compounding rounding error. **How each case is handled (`get_current_tax_fraction`)** - **Multiple inclusive taxes:** Each row with `included_in_print_rate` contributes its fraction, and the fractions are added up. Net = (amount − fixed) / (1 + Σ fractions). Rows that are not inclusive contribute 0, so they are ignored here and added on top later. - **Compounding rows:** - "On Net Total" gives `rate/100`. - "On Previous Row Amount" gives `rate/100 × (the referenced row's tax_fraction_for_current_item)`. The referenced row is found with `row_id − 1`. - "On Previous Row Total" gives `rate/100 × (the referenced row's grand_total_fraction_for_current_item)`, which is the running `1 + …` total. - Because rows are processed in order, the referenced row's fractions already exist when it is needed. - **Per-quantity taxes:** "On Item Quantity" has no fraction. It returns `inclusive_tax_amount_per_qty = rate`. The code multiplies that by `item.qty` and subtracts it from the line amount before dividing by `(1 + fractions)`. - **Deduction taxes:** If `add_deduct_tax == "Deduct"`, both the fraction and the per-quantity amount are multiplied by −1. That reduces `cumulated_tax_fraction`, so the net amount comes out higher than the inclusive amount. The grand-total fraction chain also carries the negative value into later rows. - **Not-applicable items:** If the item tax map marks the tax `NOT_APPLICABLE_TAX`, the function returns (0, 0) early. The row is skipped for that item. **Afterwards** `calculate_taxes` computes the per-row tax amounts for inclusive rows from the net amounts. `adjust_grand_total_for_inclusive_tax` then handles rounding. It compares `doc.total + non-inclusive taxes` with the last tax row's total. If the difference is within half a unit of the tax-amount precision, it stores the difference as `grand_total_diff`. `calculate_totals` adds that difference to `grand_total`. If a document-level discount is applied, `discount_amount_applied` is set. The initial inclusive back-calculation is skipped on that pass, and the amounts are recomputed through the discount flow.

final answer
ERPNext parks the value in the **Stock Received But Not Billed (SRBNB)** account. It's a liability account, set per company as `stock_received_but_not_billed`. For fixed assets the equivalent is **Asset Received But Not Billed**. The Purchase Invoice later debits the same account to clear it. I read the code for both sides but didn't run any of it. ## Receipt side (Purchase Receipt) - **Entry point:** `PurchaseReceipt.get_gl_entries` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/purchase_receipt/purchase_receipt.py`. It delegates to `compose()` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/purchase_receipt/services/gl_composer.py`. - **Order of work in `compose()`:** it calls `_make_item_gl_entries`, then `_make_tax_gl_entries`, then `set_gl_entry_for_purchase_expense`. The result goes through `process_gl_map`. - **Per stock item:** `_make_item_gl_entries` runs only when perpetual inventory is enabled and the item is a stock item. It picks `stock_asset_rbnb` from the company default `stock_received_but_not_billed`, or `asset_received_but_not_billed` for fixed assets. It then books two sides: - `make_item_asset_inward_gl_entry` debits the warehouse's inventory account. The amount is `get_stock_value_difference(...)`, which comes from the Stock Ledger Entry. - `make_stock_received_but_not_billed_entry` credits SRBNB with `base_net_amount`. The code writes this as a negative debit. - **Related entries:** the same loop also posts landed cost, expenses-included-in-valuation, amount-difference, subcontracting and divisional-loss entries through helpers such as `make_landed_cost_gl_entries` and `make_divisional_loss_gl_entry`. - **Non-stock items:** if provisional accounting is on, `ProvisionalAccountingService.add_provisional_gl_entry` (in `services/provisional_accounting.py`) parks the value in a provisional expense account instead. So after receipt: Dr Stock In Hand (inventory), Cr Stock Received But Not Billed. ## Billing side (Purchase Invoice) - **Choosing the account:** `ExpenseAccountService.set_expense_account` in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/purchase_invoice/services/expense_account.py` sets each stock item's `expense_account`. The result depends on the case: - If `update_stock` is off, it checks for a GL Entry from the linked Purchase Receipt on the SRBNB account. If one exists, the item's `expense_account` becomes SRBNB. - If there is no linked receipt, it also uses SRBNB, so an invoice entered before the receipt still works. - If `update_stock` is on, it uses the warehouse inventory account instead. - Fixed assets use Asset Received But Not Billed if the receipt booked it. Otherwise they use the CWIP or fixed-asset account from the Asset Category. - **Posting the entry:** the item-entry code in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/purchase_invoice/services/gl_composer.py` debits `item.expense_account`, which is SRBNB. It credits the supplier payable account through the normal payable entry. This is what clears the parked liability. - There is a comment at lines 331–336 saying the invoice only clears SRBNB at the full billed amount when Update Stock is off. - **Exchange-rate difference:** if the invoice's exchange rate differs from the receipt's, the difference is debited to the same account and credited to `exchange_gain_loss_account`. - **Item taxes in valuation:** if `item_tax_amount` was not booked in the receipt, the invoice posts an extra SRBNB debit. It adds that amount to `negative_expense_to_be_booked`. - **Provisional accounting:** `get_provisional_accounts` and `make_provisional_gl_entry` in the same file reverse the provisional expense entries from the receipt. - **Receipt status:** `update_billing_status` on the receipt (`purchase_receipt.py`) updates `per_billed`. `set_gl_entry_for_purchase_expense` and `remove_amount_difference_with_purchase_invoice` handle receipt-versus-invoice rate differences. After billing: Dr Stock Received But Not Billed, Cr Creditors (supplier). SRBNB nets to zero, and the inventory debit from the receipt stays in the stock account.

final answer
The holding spot is the **"Stock Received But Not Billed" (SRBNB)** account. Fixed assets use **"Asset Received But Not Billed"** instead. Both are company defaults. I read the code but did not run it. ## Receipt side (Purchase Receipt) The GL entries come from `PurchaseReceipt.get_gl_entries` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/purchase_receipt/purchase_receipt.py`. That method delegates to `PurchaseReceiptGLComposer._make_item_gl_entries` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/purchase_receipt/services/gl_composer.py`. - **Account choice:** for each item, the composer picks `stock_asset_rbnb`. It is `asset_received_but_not_billed` for fixed-asset items and `stock_received_but_not_billed` otherwise, both read with `doc.get_company_default(...)`. - **Which items qualify:** the item must have a valuation rate (or be a return). It must also be a stock item under perpetual inventory, or a fixed asset. - **Debit side:** `make_item_asset_inward_gl_entry` debits the warehouse's inventory account. For a stock item the amount is `get_stock_value_difference(...)`, the Stock Ledger Entry value. For a fixed asset it is the net amount plus item taxes plus landed cost. - **Credit side:** `make_stock_received_but_not_billed_entry` credits SRBNB. It does this by calling `add_gl_entry` with a negative debit, which is a credit in effect. The amount is `base_net_amount`. It is the stock value difference for internal transfers and for rejected warehouses when valuation of rejected materials is on. - **Adjustments:** the same composer also posts these: - `make_landed_cost_gl_entries` credits the landed-cost accounts. - `make_amount_difference_entry` adjusts SRBNB when the purchase invoice rate differs from the receipt rate. It uses `amount_difference_with_purchase_invoice`. - `make_divisional_loss_gl_entry` handles the valuation-versus-billed difference. - **Net effect of the receipt:** Dr Stock-in-Hand (or fixed-asset account) and Cr SRBNB. The goods are on the balance sheet, but no payable exists yet. - **Reporting:** the "Received Items To Be Billed" report (`accounts/report/received_items_to_be_billed`) lists receipt items where `billed_amt < amount`. ## Billing side (Purchase Invoice) - **Setting the expense account:** `ExpenseAccountService.set_expense_account` in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/purchase_invoice/services/expense_account.py` runs on validate. - With perpetual inventory, every stock item that isn't a fixed asset, opening entry or drop-ship gets `item.expense_account` forced to SRBNB. This applies when the invoice has no stock update, or when the row has a `from_warehouse`. - It keeps the expense account as SRBNB if the linked receipt already booked SRBNB. If no receipt exists, it also forces SRBNB, so the invoice-before-receipt order works. - If `update_stock` is on and the row has a warehouse without a `from_warehouse`, the account becomes the warehouse inventory account instead. - **Posting the GL entries:** `PurchaseInvoice.make_gl_entries` and `get_gl_entries` in `purchase_invoice.py` call the composer in `purchase_invoice/services/gl_composer.py`. - `make_supplier_gl_entry` credits the Payable account for the supplier. - `make_item_gl_entries` debits `item.expense_account`, which is SRBNB for these items. This is the `else` branch near lines 319–350. - **Net effect of the bill:** Dr SRBNB and Cr Creditors. This offsets the receipt's SRBNB credit, so the account nets to zero, and the liability is recognised without touching inventory again. - **Exchange rate differences:** if the exchange rate changed between receipt and bill, the same method adds a debit to the expense account and a credit to the exchange gain/loss account. - **Item taxes:** for valuation taxes with no negative expense booked in the receipt, it debits SRBNB for `item_tax_amount`. - **Bills that update stock:** with `update_stock` on, `make_stock_adjustment_entry` debits the warehouse directly and there is no SRBNB clearing. - **Billing status:** the invoice links to the receipt through `purchase_receipt` and `pr_detail`. - `BillingStatusService` in `purchase_invoice/services/billing_status.py` has `update_billing_status_in_pr`. - It calls `update_billed_amount_based_on_po` and `update_billing_percentage` in `stock/doctype/purchase_receipt/services/billing_status.py`. - These helpers sum submitted Purchase Invoice Item amounts (`get_billed_amount_against_pr`) into `Purchase Receipt Item.billed_amt` and `per_billed`. `adjust_incoming_rate_for_pr` is also called there. - `validate_multiple_billing` stops a receipt from being over-billed.

final answer
**Short answer:** ERPNext parks the value in the **Stock Received But Not Billed (SRBNB)** account. This is a liability-type account, set per company as `stock_received_but_not_billed`. Fixed-asset items use **Asset Received But Not Billed** (`asset_received_but_not_billed`) instead. When the Purchase Invoice is booked, it debits that same account and so clears the balance. I read the code below. I did not run it. **Receipt side: Purchase Receipt** - File: `erpnext/stock/doctype/purchase_receipt/services/gl_composer.py`. `PurchaseReceipt.get_gl_entries` in `purchase_receipt.py` (around line 478) leads to this composer. - For each item, the composer picks the holding account. This is around lines 297–301. Fixed-asset items get `asset_received_but_not_billed`. Everything else gets `stock_received_but_not_billed`. Both come from `doc.get_company_default(...)`. The composer only does this when perpetual inventory is enabled and the item is a stock item. - `make_item_asset_inward_gl_entry` debits the warehouse's stock account, or the fixed-asset account. The amount is `stock_value_diff`, taken from `get_stock_value_difference`. - `make_stock_received_but_not_billed_entry` (around line 74) posts the matching entry on the holding account (SRBNB). The code writes it as `debit=-1 * outgoing_amount`, which is a credit of the item's `base_net_amount`. It skips the entry if `credit_amount` is zero. - Other helpers post landed costs, expenses added to stock, amount differences and subcontracting entries. - Net effect: Dr Stock (inventory) and Cr SRBNB. The value stays in SRBNB until a bill arrives. **Billing side: Purchase Invoice** - `ExpenseAccountService.set_expense_account` (`erpnext/accounts/doctype/purchase_invoice/services/expense_account.py`) runs during validation. For a stock item, when the invoice does not update stock itself, it sets `item.expense_account` to the company's SRBNB account. It does this when `item.purchase_receipt` is linked and that receipt already has a GL entry on SRBNB. It also does it when there is no linked receipt, and its comment says this covers an invoice created before the receipt. - For fixed assets, the same method checks whether the receipt booked ARBNB. If it did, it uses `asset_received_but_not_billed`. Otherwise it falls back to the asset category's CWIP or fixed-asset account. - When the invoice has `update_stock` set and a warehouse, the expense account becomes the inventory account instead, so SRBNB is not used. - `PurchaseInvoice.make_gl_entries` (`purchase_invoice.py`, around line 671) calls the composer in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/purchase_invoice/services/gl_composer.py`. - `make_supplier_gl_entry` credits the supplier's payable account. - `make_item_gl_entries` debits `item.expense_account`, which is SRBNB for receipt-backed stock items. This debit offsets the credit made at receipt, so the holding balance clears. The posting references the receipt through the item's `purchase_receipt` and `pr_detail` links. - `make_stock_adjustment_entry` handles any difference between the invoiced amount and the stock value. - `make_tax_gl_entries` posts taxes. - There is also a branch around lines 395–440 for items with `item_tax_amount`. If the receipt did not already book the tax into valuation, it debits `stock_received_but_not_billed` for that tax amount. That branch tracks the amount in `negative_expense_to_be_booked`. - If the receipt booked a provisional expense, `get_provisional_accounts` and `make_provisional_gl_entry` reverse that provisional posting. - Net effect: Dr SRBNB and Cr Supplier payable. The inventory side was already booked at receipt, so only the liability swaps from "received but not billed" to a real payable. **Monitoring reports:** `erpnext/accounts/report/received_items_to_be_billed` and `billed_items_to_be_received` show the items still sitting in this holding state.

final answer
**Where the value is parked** ERPNext holds the value in the **"Stock Received But Not Billed" (SRBNB)** account. It is a company default, `stock_received_but_not_billed`. Fixed-asset items use the parallel "Asset Received But Not Billed" account, `asset_received_but_not_billed`. This applies when perpetual inventory (auto accounting for stock) is on. I read the code but did not run a receipt and invoice through it. **Receipt side** The file is `erpnext/stock/doctype/purchase_receipt/services/gl_composer.py`. The Purchase Receipt's GL composer builds the entries, and `StockController.make_gl_entries` in `erpnext/controllers/stock_controller.py` posts them. For each stock item (around lines 290–335): - **Account choice:** it picks `stock_asset_rbnb` from `doc.get_company_default("stock_received_but_not_billed")`. For a fixed asset it uses `asset_received_but_not_billed`. - **Debit:** `make_item_asset_inward_gl_entry` debits the warehouse's inventory account (or the asset account). The amount comes from `get_stock_value_difference`, which reads the Stock Ledger Entry. - **Credit:** `make_stock_received_but_not_billed_entry` credits SRBNB with the item's `base_net_amount`. In the code the credit is written as a negative debit. The supplier is not credited at this point. - **Other lines:** the same pass adds the landed-cost, expenses-included-in-valuation, amount-difference and divisional-loss lines. - **Provisional accounting:** this is an alternative, used when `enable_provisional_accounting_for_non_stock_items` is on. Non-stock items go through `add_provisional_gl_entry`, which parks their value in a provisional expense account instead. The result is Dr Stock-in-Hand and Cr SRBNB. SRBNB now carries a credit balance, which is the liability for goods not yet billed. **Billing side** **Expense account:** `ExpenseAccountService.set_expense_account` in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/purchase_invoice/services/expense_account.py` sets each stock item's `expense_account` to SRBNB. It does this when the invoice does not update stock and the item is not a fixed asset. It also checks whether a GL Entry already exists for the linked Purchase Receipt on that account. If the invoice has no Purchase Receipt, it also forces SRBNB. This covers an invoice that comes before the receipt. **Invoice GL entries:** the file is `erpnext/accounts/doctype/purchase_invoice/services/gl_composer.py`, with `PurchaseInvoice.make_gl_entries` in `purchase_invoice.py` as the entry point. For a stock item that is not an internal transfer (around lines 330–350): - **Debit:** it debits `item.expense_account`, which is SRBNB. This cancels the receipt's credit. - **Credit:** the supplier (payable) account is credited for the full bill. - **Net effect:** SRBNB goes back to zero and the liability moves to Accounts Payable. - **Exchange-rate gap:** if the exchange rate changed between receipt and bill, the difference goes to the exchange gain/loss account. - **Item taxes:** if the receipt did not already book the item tax, the invoice debits SRBNB for it. - **Price differences:** `make_stock_adjustment_entry` handles other price differences. - **Fixed assets:** they are cleared against ARBNB the same way. **Billing status and invoice-first case** - **Billing status:** `update_billing_status_in_pr` (`erpnext/accounts/doctype/purchase_invoice/services/billing_status.py`) updates the receipt's billed amount and percentage. `update_billing_status` in `purchase_receipt/services/billing_status.py` does the counterpart on the receipt side. - **Invoice first:** if the invoice is posted before the receipt, the invoice debits SRBNB with no matching credit yet. The later receipt's credit then clears it.

Yes, there is a save-time check. It lives in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/pricing_rule/pricing_rule.py`. **Call path** - `PricingRule.validate()` calls `validate_duplicate_apply_on()`. - That method only runs when `apply_on != "Transaction"`. - It looks up the child table for the chosen "Apply On" in `apply_on_dict`. For "Item Code" the field is `item_code`. - It collects every non-empty `item_code` value from the table into `values`. - If `len(values) != len(set(values))`, it throws "Duplicate Item Code found in the table". This is the plain duplicate check. - When `apply_on == "Item Code"`, it then calls `validate_template_with_variant(values)`. **The template/variant check** `validate_template_with_variant(item_codes)` runs one database query: ```python frappe.get_all("Item", filters={"name": ("in", item_codes), "variant_of": ("in", item_codes)}, fields=["name", "variant_of"]) ``` - It returns Item records whose own `name` is in the rule's item list and whose `variant_of` (the template) is also in that list. That is exactly a variant listed alongside its template. - If any rows come back, it takes the first one and calls `frappe.throw`. - The message is "Variant {0} and its template {1} cannot both be added to the same Pricing Rule". - The save is rejected. **Limits** - It only reports the first conflicting pair found. - It only applies to "Item Code". The "Item Group" and "Brand" options skip it.

final answer
When the Pricing Rule is saved, `PricingRule.validate()` calls `validate_duplicate_apply_on()`. That method is in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/pricing_rule/pricing_rule.py`, and it rejects the template-plus-variant combination. I read this from the search results and did not open the full file. So I did not confirm that `validate()` calls it unconditionally. **What `validate_duplicate_apply_on()` does** 1. It only runs when `apply_on` is not "Transaction". 2. It looks up the child table for the `apply_on` value in `apply_on_dict`. For "Item Code" the field is `item_code`. 3. It collects the non-empty `item_code` values from that table. 4. If `len(values) != len(set(values))`, it throws "Duplicate Item Code found in the table". This catches exact duplicates only. 5. If `apply_on == "Item Code"`, it then calls `validate_template_with_variant(values)`. **What `validate_template_with_variant(item_codes)` checks** - It runs one query: `frappe.get_all("Item", filters={"name": ("in", item_codes), "variant_of": ("in", item_codes)}, fields=["name", "variant_of"])`. - That query returns any Item that is itself in the rule's list and whose `variant_of` template is also in the list. In other words, a variant and its own template both appear in the rule. - If the query returns anything, it takes the first row and calls `frappe.throw`. The message is "Variant {0} and its template {1} cannot both be added to the same Pricing Rule", with the variant name and the template name in bold. The save is blocked. A variant alongside an unrelated template does not trigger this check. The check only fires when the variant's own `variant_of` template is also in the list. **Why the check exists** When a pricing rule is looked up at transaction time, `_get_pricing_rules` in `utils.py` matches a variant item against rules that list the item itself or its `variant_of` template. A rule listing both would therefore match the same item twice.

Yes, a validation runs at save time and rejects the configuration. It lives in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/pricing_rule/pricing_rule.py`. **Call path** 1. `PricingRule.validate()` calls `validate_duplicate_apply_on()` as its second step, right after `validate_mandatory()`. 2. For "Item Code" this is the `items` table (`Pricing Rule Item Code`). 3. It collects the `item_code` values from every row. It uses `frappe.scrub(self.apply_on)` to get the field name. 4. If `len(values) != len(set(values))`, it throws "Duplicate Item Code found in the table". This is a separate check for exact duplicates. 5. If `apply_on == "Item Code"`, it then calls `validate_template_with_variant(values)`. **What `validate_template_with_variant(item_codes)` checks** - It runs one `frappe.get_all("Item", ...)` query with `filters={"name": ("in", item_codes), "variant_of": ("in", item_codes)}` and `fields=["name", "variant_of"]`. - The query returns an Item only if two conditions both hold: - The item's own code is in the rule's list. - The item's `variant_of` (its template) is also in the rule's list. - That means a variant and its own template are both present in the same rule. - If the result is non-empty, it takes the first match and calls `frappe.throw`. The message is "Variant {0} and its template {1} cannot both be added to the same Pricing Rule", with the variant's name and its template's name in bold. - Saving is blocked. The check is a single database lookup on the Item's `variant_of` link. It does not iterate over variants or check `has_variants`. It applies only when "Apply On" is "Item Code", so Item Group and Brand rules are not checked this way. Because it reports only `variants[0]`, only the first conflicting pair is shown.

Q17Group D — Numeric/logic edge casesWhen two stock transactions share the exact same posting date and time (e.g. a Purchase Receipt and a Material Transfer both poste…
When two stock transactions share the exact same posting date and time (e.g. a Purchase Receipt and a Material Transfer both posted at the same second), how does ERPNext's stock ledger reposting logic determine the correct ordering/selection of "current" vs "previous" Stock Ledger Entries when one of those transactions is later cancelled, so that negative-stock validation isn't fooled by ambiguous same-timestamp ordering?
