final answer
When a Stock Entry is made from a Job Card, the system pre-fills serial numbers or batches only for items that an earlier operation of the same Work Order produced. If fewer units are available than the row needs, it fills what exists and leaves the rest blank for the user. I read the code but did not run it. **Where it is called** - `erpnext/manufacturing/doctype/job_card/mapper.py` calls `set_previous_operation_serial_batch(target, item)` for each mapped row (around line 132). - `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry_type/stock_entry_type.py` calls it too (around line 144). - The function is `set_previous_operation_serial_batch(parent_doc, row)` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/services/manufacturing.py` (around line 1132). **When it does nothing** - The Stock Entry has no `work_order`, the row has no `s_warehouse`, or the row quantity is zero or less. - The row already has a `serial_and_batch_bundle`, `serial_no` or `batch_no`. - The item is an ordinary raw material. - The item has neither a serial number nor a batch number. **How it picks the serials and batches** 1. The row quantity is converted to stock UOM: `qty * conversion_factor`. 2. `get_previous_operation_output_sn_batch(work_order, item_code, warehouse)` builds the pool of candidates. It returns an empty pool unless a `Work Order Operation` row on that Work Order lists the item as its `finished_good`, so only semi-finished goods qualify. The item must also have `has_serial_no` or `has_batch_no`. 3. The pool is "produced" minus "consumed", via `_get_operation_sn_batch`, `_get_operation_bundles` and the Serial and Batch Entry table: - **Produced:** bundles on submitted Stock Entries for the same Work Order with purpose "Manufacture", `is_finished_item=1`, and the row's `t_warehouse` equal to the source warehouse. - **Consumed:** bundles on submitted Stock Entries for the same Work Order with `s_warehouse` equal to that warehouse. - **Serials:** every produced serial not already consumed. - **Batches:** produced quantity minus consumed quantity, kept only where the result is greater than 0. 4. `_cap_pool_to_qty(pool, qty)` trims the pool to the row quantity: - **Serials:** it takes the first `cint(qty)` serials, in Serial and Batch Entry order (by bundle, then `idx`). - **Batches:** it walks the batches in order, taking `min(batch_qty, remaining)` from each until the quantity is met. 5. If anything was selected, `SerialBatchCreation(...).make_serial_and_batch_bundle()` creates an Outward bundle with `do_not_submit=True`. The row gets `serial_and_batch_bundle` set and `use_serial_batch_fields = 0`. **If fewer units are available than the row's quantity** - The code does not raise an error. It prefills only what exists, and the docstring says it leaves "any shortfall blank for the user". - One detail from my reading of the code: the bundle is created with `qty` set to the full row quantity, not the capped amount. I did not check how that interacts with the shortfall, but the user would have to complete or correct the bundle before submitting. - If nothing is available, no bundle is created and the row stays untouched.

final answer
When a Stock Entry is made from a Job Card, ERPNext pre-fills serial numbers or batches only for items that an earlier operation of the same Work Order produced. It takes whatever is still available, up to the row's quantity, and leaves any shortfall blank for the user. I read the code but didn't run it. **Where it starts** - `make_stock_entry` in `erpnext/manufacturing/doctype/job_card/mapper.py` maps the Job Card to a Stock Entry with purpose "Material Transfer for Manufacture". - Its `set_missing_values` step loops over the Stock Entry rows and calls `set_previous_operation_serial_batch(target, item)` on each one. - The same function is called from `ManufactureEntry.add_raw_materials` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry_type/stock_entry_type.py`. That path runs when the Job Card has `skip_material_transfer` set. **Which rows are filled** (`set_previous_operation_serial_batch` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/services/manufacturing.py`) - It does nothing if the Stock Entry has no `work_order`, the row has no source warehouse, or the quantity is zero or less. The quantity used is `qty × conversion_factor`. - It also skips rows that already have a `serial_and_batch_bundle`, `serial_no` or `batch_no`, so user-entered values are never overwritten. **Which serials or batches are chosen** (`get_previous_operation_output_sn_batch`) - The item must be the `finished_good` of some Work Order Operation in that Work Order, meaning it is a semi-finished good. Ordinary raw materials get an empty result. - The item must also have `has_serial_no` or `has_batch_no`. - The pool is what earlier operations produced in the row's source warehouse, minus what later entries have already taken out of it: - *Produced:* submitted Manufacture entries for the Work Order where the row is a finished item (`is_finished_item = 1`) with `t_warehouse` equal to that warehouse. - *Consumed:* submitted entries for the Work Order whose `s_warehouse` is that warehouse. - For both, `_get_operation_sn_batch` reads the Serial and Batch Entry rows from the bundles. - Serial numbers that were produced and not consumed stay in the pool. - Batches stay in the pool only when produced quantity minus consumed quantity is above zero. **Capping and shortfall** - `_cap_pool_to_qty` trims the pool to the row quantity. For serials it takes the first `cint(qty)`. For batches it goes through them in order, taking `min(batch_qty, remaining)` from each until the quantity is covered. - If fewer units are available than the row quantity, it takes what exists. The bundle is built with the full row `qty`, but the serial or batch entries cover only the available units, so the rest is left for the user to complete. - It builds a `SerialBatchCreation` bundle with `type_of_transaction="Outward"` and `do_not_submit=True`. Then it sets `row.serial_and_batch_bundle` and `use_serial_batch_fields = 0`. - If nothing is available, it returns without setting anything and the row stays blank. I did not see where the unfilled part is validated. It is presumably caught when the bundle is validated at save or submit.
Q24Group E — Concurrency & database semanticsSome ERPNext flows perform a best-effort or optional database write inside a larger transaction — for example auto-creating a link…
Some ERPNext flows perform a best-effort or optional database write inside a larger transaction — for example auto-creating a linked document while submitting another. On PostgreSQL, if such a write fails, every later statement in the transaction fails too. What mechanism does the codebase use to survive these failures, how does it differ from a full rollback, and where is it applied? Name concrete functions and files.

final answer
ERPNext uses **database savepoints**, through Frappe's `frappe.db.savepoint(name)` and `frappe.db.rollback(save_point=name)`. I found them by grepping for those calls. I didn't open the Frappe framework source, so the mechanics below are standard SQL and Frappe behaviour rather than something I checked in this repo. **The pattern** ```python frappe.db.savepoint("some_name") try: ...optional or best-effort write... except Exception: frappe.db.rollback(save_point="some_name") # a comment in the code reads "preserve transaction in postgres" ``` **How it differs from a full rollback** - `frappe.db.rollback()` with no argument rolls back the whole transaction. That discards all earlier work, such as the document being submitted. - `rollback(save_point=...)` runs `ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT`. It undoes only the writes made since the savepoint. This also clears PostgreSQL's "current transaction is aborted" state, so later statements work again. Everything before the savepoint stays pending and can still be committed. - MariaDB doesn't abort the transaction on a failed statement, so this matters mainly on PostgreSQL. The code uses it on both databases. **Where it's applied** Auto-creating or linking documents inside a larger flow: - `erpnext/stock/utils.py` (`bin_creation_savepoint`) wraps Bin creation. - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/fiscal_year/fiscal_year.py` wraps `auto_create_fiscal_year`. - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/payment_entry/payment_entry.py` wraps `subscription_update`. - `erpnext/buying/doctype/purchase_order/mapper.py` wraps `submit_subcontracting_order`. - `erpnext/subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_receipt/mapper.py` wraps `submit_subcontracting_receipt`. - `erpnext/telephony/doctype/call_log/call_log.py` wraps `link_call_logs`. - `erpnext/crm/frappe_crm_api.py` wraps `crm_create_address` and `crm_customer_links`. - `erpnext/crm/doctype/email_campaign/email_campaign.py` wraps `email_campaign_send`. Setup and installation: - `erpnext/setup/setup_wizard/operations/install_fixtures.py` wraps `create_bank_account`. - `erpnext/setup/setup_wizard/operations/taxes_setup.py` wraps `regional_tax_settings`. - `erpnext/setup/demo.py` wraps `demo_data`. Per-row isolation in bulk and background jobs: - `erpnext/utilities/bulk_transaction.py` wraps `before_creation_state`. - `erpnext/accounts/bulk_payment.py` wraps `bulk_pe`. - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/ledger_merge/ledger_merge.py` wraps `ledger_merge_row`. - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/opening_invoice_creation_tool/opening_invoice_creation_tool.py` uses a named `savepoint` variable. - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/bank_transaction/bank_transaction_upload.py` wraps `bank_entry`. - `erpnext/stock/reorder_item.py` wraps `reorder_mr`. - `erpnext/regional/doctype/import_supplier_invoice/import_supplier_invoice.py` wraps `import_invoice`. - `erpnext/assets/doctype/asset/depreciation.py` wraps `depr_entry`. - `erpnext/stock/doctype/repost_item_valuation/repost_item_valuation.py` wraps `repost_accounting_ledger`. - `erpnext/erpnext_integrations/doctype/plaid_settings/plaid_settings.py` wraps `plaid_bank_account`, `plaid_update_account` and `plaid_sync_txn`. - The stock reports `stock_ledger_invariant_check` and `stock_and_account_value_comparison` also use savepoints around reposts. - The patch `erpnext/patches/v13_0/trim_whitespace_from_serial_nos.py` uses one too. Previews and tests: - `erpnext/controllers/ledger_preview.py` uses a `ledger_preview` savepoint. It writes preview GL entries and then rolls them back to the savepoint, not to a failure. - Tests use it to survive expected errors: `stock/doctype/bin/test_bin.py` (`dup_bin`), `stock/doctype/item/test_item.py` (`dup_barcode`), `manufacturing/doctype/routing/test_routing.py` and `buying/doctype/supplier_quotation/test_supplier_quotation.py`. `.github/helper/postgres_compat.py` mentions "savepoint discipline" as something a human reviewer has to check, because the script can't detect it.

final answer
The codebase uses database savepoints: `frappe.db.savepoint(name)` before the risky write, and `frappe.db.rollback(save_point=name)` if it fails. I read the main call sites in the search output. I did not open every file in the grep list, so those entries are listed from their savepoint calls only. **How it differs from a full rollback** - A plain `frappe.db.rollback()` ends the whole transaction. It discards everything done earlier in the request, including the document being submitted, and it fires rollback hooks. - `rollback(save_point=...)` runs `ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT`. It undoes only the writes made after the savepoint was set, and the outer transaction stays open and usable. - On PostgreSQL a failed statement, such as a duplicate-key insert, puts the transaction into an aborted state. Every later statement then errors until you roll back. Rolling back to the savepoint clears that state. - The code comments say this directly: - `erpnext/stock/utils.py` has `# preserve transaction in postgres`. - `erpnext/stock/report/stock_and_account_value_comparison/stock_and_account_value_comparison.py` explains that a failed insert aborts the whole transaction, which would kill the rest of the loop. - `erpnext/erpnext_integrations/doctype/plaid_settings/plaid_settings.py` has the comment "so a failed insert doesn't poison the transaction on postgres". **Where it is applied** - **Concurrent or duplicate inserts** - `_create_bin` in `erpnext/stock/utils.py` uses the `create_bin` savepoint. On `UniqueValidationError` it rolls back and fetches the existing Bin. - `repost_based_on_transaction` in `stock_and_account_value_comparison.py` rolls back on `DuplicateEntryError`. - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/fiscal_year/fiscal_year.py` uses `auto_create_fiscal_year`, so a duplicate-year insert doesn't abort the transaction. - **Auto-created linked documents during submit** - `erpnext/buying/doctype/purchase_order/mapper.py` uses `submit_subcontracting_order`. - `erpnext/subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_receipt/mapper.py` uses `submit_subcontracting_receipt`. - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/payment_entry/payment_entry.py` uses `subscription_update`. - `erpnext/crm/frappe_crm_api.py` uses `crm_create_address` and `crm_customer_links`. A failure while linking contacts or addresses doesn't discard the Customer. - `erpnext/telephony/doctype/call_log/call_log.py` uses `link_call_logs`. - `erpnext/crm/doctype/email_campaign/email_campaign.py` uses `email_campaign_send`. - `erpnext/stock/reorder_item.py` uses `reorder_mr`. - **Per-item isolation in loops and bulk jobs** - `job` and `retry_failed_transactions` in `erpnext/utilities/bulk_transaction.py` use `before_creation_state`. final answer
**Mechanism: database savepoints.** The pattern is `frappe.db.savepoint(name)` before the risky write. If the write fails, the code calls `frappe.db.rollback(save_point=name)`. **Why it's needed.** On PostgreSQL, a failed statement aborts the whole transaction. Every later statement then fails with "current transaction is aborted". Rolling back to a savepoint clears that aborted state and keeps the transaction usable. **How it differs from a full rollback.** - A plain `frappe.db.rollback()` discards the entire transaction. That includes the parent document being submitted, earlier rows in a loop, and any queued or enqueued work. - Rolling back to a savepoint undoes only the work done since the savepoint was set. Everything before it stays pending and can still be committed. - `opening_invoice_creation_tool.py` states this in a comment: rolling back to a savepoint keeps both the prior work and the later work. **Where it is applied.** I found these by grepping for `savepoint`. I read only two of the call sites in detail, `_create_bin` and `make_subcontracting_receipt`'s mapper. For the rest I relied on the call and the nearby comments. - **Concurrent-insert race:** `_create_bin` in `erpnext/stock/utils.py` uses savepoint `create_bin`. On `frappe.UniqueValidationError` it rolls back to the savepoint and fetches the existing Bin with `get_last_doc`. A comment there reads "preserve transaction in postgres". - **Auto-create and submit a linked document:** - `erpnext/subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_receipt/mapper.py` sets savepoint `submit_subcontracting_receipt` around `target_doc.submit()`. On failure it rolls back to it and adds a "Submit Action Failed" comment to the document. - `erpnext/buying/doctype/purchase_order/mapper.py` sets `submit_subcontracting_order`. - `erpnext/crm/frappe_crm_api.py` uses `crm_create_address` and `crm_customer_links`. The comment there says a link failure must not discard the Customer. - **Optional side effects:** - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/fiscal_year/fiscal_year.py` uses `auto_create_fiscal_year`, so a duplicate-year INSERT doesn't abort the transaction. - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/payment_entry/payment_entry.py` uses `subscription_update`. - `erpnext/setup/setup_wizard/operations/install_fixtures.py` uses `create_bank_account`. - `erpnext/setup/setup_wizard/operations/taxes_setup.py` uses `regional_tax_settings`. - `erpnext/telephony/doctype/call_log/call_log.py` uses `link_call_logs`. - `erpnext/stock/reorder_item.py` uses `reorder_mr`. - `erpnext/crm/doctype/email_campaign/email_campaign.py` uses `email_campaign_send`. - `erpnext/assets/doctype/asset/depreciation.py` uses `depr_entry`. - **Per-item isolation in loops and bulk jobs:** - `erpnext/utilities/bulk_transaction.py` uses `before_creation_state`. - `erpnext/accounts/bulk_payment.py` uses `bulk_pe`. - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/opening_invoice_creation_tool/opening_invoice_creation_tool.py` uses a per-invoice savepoint named `opening_invoice_<hash>`. - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/ledger_merge/ledger_merge.py` uses `ledger_merge_row`. - `erpnext/erpnext_integrations/doctype/plaid_settings/plaid_settings.py` uses `plaid_bank_account`, `plaid_update_account` and `plaid_sync_txn`. - `erpnext/regional/doctype/import_supplier_invoice/import_supplier_invoice.py` uses `import_invoice`. - `erpnext/stock/doctype/repost_item_valuation/repost_item_valuation.py` uses `repost_accounting_ledger`. - `erpnext/stock/report/stock_ledger_invariant_check/stock_ledger_invariant_check.py` uses `repost_invariant_check`. - `erpnext/patches/v13_0/trim_whitespace_from_serial_nos.py` uses `serial_no_patch`. - **Never-persist previews:** `erpnext/controllers/ledger_preview.py` sets a `ledger_preview` savepoint and rolls back to it so nothing is saved. - **Demo data and tests:** `erpnext/setup/demo.py` uses `demo_data`. Tests use savepoints too, for example `test_bin.py`, `test_item.py` and `test_routing.py`. `.github/helper/postgres_compat.py` mentions "savepoint discipline" as something a Postgres compatibility check can't verify mechanically.

final answer
The codebase uses database savepoints. It calls `frappe.db.savepoint(name)` before the risky write. If the write raises, it calls `frappe.db.rollback(save_point=name)`. **How it differs from a full rollback** - A plain `frappe.db.rollback()` discards the whole transaction. That includes the parent document, the earlier rows, and any `frappe.db.after_commit` or `on_rollback` bookkeeping. - `rollback(save_point=...)` undoes only the statements made since that savepoint. On PostgreSQL this clears the "current transaction is aborted" state, so later statements in the same transaction work again. - The work done before the savepoint stays pending and is committed normally. The parent flow carries on. **Where it is applied** - **`erpnext/buying/doctype/purchase_order/mapper.py`** - Savepoint `submit_subcontracting_order`: the subcontracting order is saved, and the failing `submit()` is rolled back to the savepoint. A comment is then added to the document instead of raising. - **`erpnext/subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_receipt/mapper.py`** - Savepoint `submit_subcontracting_receipt`, used the same way. - **`erpnext/stock/utils.py`** - `_create_bin` uses savepoint `create_bin`. On `UniqueValidationError` it rolls back to the savepoint and fetches the existing Bin, with the comment "preserve transaction in postgres". - **`erpnext/accounts/doctype/fiscal_year/fiscal_year.py`** - Savepoint `auto_create_fiscal_year`: a duplicate-year INSERT would otherwise abort the transaction. - **`erpnext/crm/frappe_crm_api.py`** - Savepoints `crm_create_address` and `crm_customer_links`. A failure while linking contacts or addresses must not discard the Customer. - **`erpnext/erpnext_integrations/doctype/plaid_settings/plaid_settings.py`** - Savepoints `plaid_bank_account`, `plaid_update_account` and `plaid_sync_txn`. A failed insert or submit must not discard the Bank Account or the other transactions. - **`erpnext/accounts/doctype/opening_invoice_creation_tool/opening_invoice_creation_tool.py`** - A per-invoice savepoint with a random name. A failure undoes only that invoice, and earlier invoices and error logs survive. - **`erpnext/accounts/doctype/payment_entry/payment_entry.py`** - Savepoint `subscription_update`. - **`erpnext/accounts/bulk_payment.py`** - Savepoint `bulk_pe`. - **`erpnext/utilities/bulk_transaction.py`** - Savepoint `before_creation_state`. - **`erpnext/accounts/doctype/ledger_merge/ledger_merge.py`** - A per-row savepoint, `ledger_merge_row`. - **`erpnext/assets/doctype/asset/depreciation.py`** - Savepoint `depr_entry`. - **`erpnext/stock/reorder_item.py`** - Savepoint `reorder_mr`. - **`erpnext/telephony/doctype/call_log/call_log.py`** - Savepoint `link_call_logs`. - **`erpnext/stock/doctype/repost_item_valuation/repost_item_valuation.py`** - Savepoint `repost_accounting_ledger`. - **`erpnext/stock/report/stock_and_account_value_comparison/stock_and_account_value_comparison.py`** - A per-submit savepoint, so an already-queued repost raising `DuplicateEntryError` doesn't abort the rest. - **`erpnext/accounts/doctype/bank_transaction/bank_transaction_upload.py`** - Savepoint `bank_entry`. - **`erpnext/crm/doctype/email_campaign/email_campaign.py`** - Savepoint `email_campaign_send`. - **`erpnext/regional/doctype/import_supplier_invoice/import_supplier_invoice.py`** - Savepoint `import_invoice`. - **`erpnext/setup/setup_wizard/operations/install_fixtures.py`** - Savepoint `create_bank_account`. - **`erpnext/setup/setup_wizard/operations/taxes_setup.py`** - Savepoint `regional_tax_settings`. - **`erpnext/patches/v13_0/trim_whitespace_from_serial_nos.py`** - A patch-level savepoint, `serial_no_patch`. A related use is in `erpnext/controllers/ledger_preview.py`. It sets a `ledger_preview` savepoint so that a preview never persists anything. MariaDB and PostgreSQL sort NULL values differently. In queries where that difference would change which row ERPNext picks (not just display order), how does the codebase explicitly control NULL ordering? Describe the mechanisms used and name concrete query sites.

final answer
ERPNext doesn't use `NULLS FIRST` or `NULLS LAST` anywhere. I searched the Python code for those keywords and found none. Instead it makes NULL ordering the same on both databases by changing the sort key itself. Three techniques do this. I found the sites below with regex searches for `IfNull`, `Coalesce` and `isnull` in `orderby`/`order by` clauses, so the list may not be exhaustive. **1. Sorting on an `IS NULL` flag first (query builder)** - In `get_item_price()` in `erpnext/stock/get_item_details.py` (~line 1233), the first sort key is `.orderby(ip.valid_from.isnull(), order=frappe.qb.asc)`. - `IS NULL` evaluates to 0 or 1 on both databases, so rows with a `valid_from` come first and undated rows come last. - The next key is `.orderby(ip.valid_from, order=desc)`. Together they act as "dated prices newest first, then undated". The query ends in `.limit(1)`, so this decides which Item Price is chosen. **2. Replacing NULL with a sentinel value in the sort key (`IfNull` / `Coalesce`)** - **`get_item_price()`** in `get_item_details.py` also uses `.orderby(IfNull(ip.batch_no, ""), desc)`. It uses `IfNull(ip.customer, "")` desc when a customer is passed, and `IfNull(ip.supplier, "")` desc when a supplier is passed. A row that names the party or batch therefore beats a generic row with NULL, whichever database is used. The `.limit(1)` makes this a row-selection decision. - **Pricing rule lookup**, in the raw SQL in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/pricing_rule/utils.py` (~line 155): `order by coalesce(`tabPricing Rule`.priority, '') desc, `tabPricing Rule`.name desc`. This puts rules with no priority last, and `name desc` breaks ties. The results are then used to pick which rule applies. - **`erpnext/www/payment_setup_certification.py`** (~line 24): `.orderby(IfNull(cc.to_date, "0001-01-01"), order=desc)`. NULL is turned into a very early date so it sorts last when descending. - **`erpnext/accounts/doctype/payment_entry/payment_entry.py`** (~line 2798): `.orderby(Coalesce(PR.transaction_date, PR.creation), order=asc)`. A missing transaction date falls back to the creation time. - **`erpnext/controllers/queries.py`** (~line 914): `query.orderby(IfNull(bin_dt.actual_qty, 0), order=Order.desc)`. A missing quantity is treated as 0. **3. Making the filters NULL-safe to match** - The same queries use `IfNull(ip.valid_from, "2000-01-01")` and `IfNull(ip.valid_upto, "2500-12-31")` in their `where` clauses. - They also use `IfNull(ip.uom, "")` and `IfNull(ip.batch_no, "")`. - The pricing rule SQL uses `coalesce(valid_from, '2000-01-01')`, `coalesce(valid_upto, '2500-12-31')`, and `coalesce(field, '') in (..., '')`. - These treat NULL and empty values the same way in the filter, so NULL means "applies to everything" on both databases.

final answer
ERPNext has no `NULLS FIRST` or `NULLS LAST` clauses anywhere in its Python. Instead it makes the ordering engine-independent in the query itself, using three mechanisms. I found the sites with a regex search for NULL-ordering patterns, so the list may not be exhaustive. **1. Order by an `IS NULL` flag first.** The flag sorts as 0 or 1 on both engines, so it fixes where NULLs land before the real column is considered. - `erpnext/stock/get_item_details.py`, in the Item Price lookup (around lines 1224–1257): `.orderby(ip.valid_from.isnull(), order=frappe.qb.asc)` is followed by `.orderby(ip.valid_from, order=frappe.qb.desc)`. Rows with a date come before rows with no `valid_from`, then the latest date wins. The query ends in `limit(1)`, so the NULL placement decides which price is returned. **2. Wrap the column in `IfNull(col, sentinel)` inside the `ORDER BY`.** The sentinel puts NULL at one end of the sort on both engines. - `get_item_details.py`, same query: `IfNull(ip.batch_no, "")`, `IfNull(ip.customer, "")` and `IfNull(ip.supplier, "")` are ordered descending. A NULL is treated as `""` and sorts last, so a specific batch, customer or supplier beats a generic price row. The query is `limit(1)`. - `erpnext/setup/doctype/email_digest/email_digest.py`, `EmailDigest.get_todo_list` (a `limit(20)` query): - `.orderby(IfNull(todo.date, "1000-01-01"))` keeps NULL dates first, matching MariaDB's old `date asc` behaviour. - A `Case().else_(0)` priority expression reproduces MySQL's `FIELD(priority, 'High', 'Medium', 'Low')`, where unknown or empty priority sorts first. - `test_email_digest.py::test_get_todo_list_priority_and_date_ordering` checks both behaviours. - `erpnext/www/payment_setup_certification.py` (line 24): `.orderby(IfNull(cc.to_date, "0001-01-01"), order=frappe.qb.desc)`. A NULL `to_date` is given a very early date, so it sorts last in descending order. - `erpnext/controllers/queries.py` (line 914): `.orderby(IfNull(bin_dt.actual_qty, 0), order=Order.desc)`. A NULL quantity is treated as 0. **3. Filter NULLs out with `IS NOT NULL` before ordering and taking the first row.** - `erpnext/crm/report/lead_conversion_time/lead_conversion_time.py` (around lines 119–126): the `first_contact` query uses `.where(... & comm.communication_date.isnotnull()).orderby(comm.communication_date).limit(1)`. - Without the filter, MariaDB (NULLs first) returned the NULL-dated Communication, while PostgreSQL (NULLs last) returned the earliest real date. - `test_lead_conversion_time.py::test_first_contact_ignores_null_communication_date` covers this. **Related, but not ordering:** `IfNull(..., 0)` is also used in `production_plan/services/planning_queries.py` (`_bin_qty_columns`) and in `budget_controller.py`. That is NULL-safe arithmetic, not sort control.

Graphify 75.0%

final answer
ERPNext doesn't use `NULLS FIRST` or `NULLS LAST` anywhere. A search of the non-test code found no such clause. It controls NULL position in two other ways. Both put a boolean "is this column NULL?" expression in front of the real sort key. The other is `IfNull` with a sentinel value. **1. Sort on `column.isnull()` first (pypika `Term.isnull()`)** `col IS NULL` evaluates to 0 or 1 on both MariaDB and PostgreSQL. Sorting it first, ascending or descending, puts the NULL rows in the same place on both databases. Only then does the real column decide the order. - `get_item_price()` in `erpnext/stock/get_item_details.py` (around L1233) uses `.orderby(ip.valid_from.isnull(), order=asc)`, then `.orderby(ip.valid_from, order=desc)`, and ends with `.limit(1)`. Item Prices with a `valid_from` date are preferred, newest first, and an undated price is used only as a fallback. Without the first clause, MariaDB (NULLs sort first in ascending order) and PostgreSQL (NULLs sort first in descending order) would pick different rows. - The same pattern appears at about L1132 of `get_item_details.py`. - `erpnext/selling/page/point_of_sale/point_of_sale.py` (L234–235) repeats it. It applies to the Item Price lookup in the POS item-price code, which also filters on `valid_from IS NULL OR <= date`. - `get_serial_nos_based_on_filters()` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/serial_and_batch_bundle/serial_and_batch_bundle.py` (L2510–2512) is the same idea, applied only when `order_by == "amc_expiry_date"` (the FIFO/expiry path). It adds `.orderby(col.isnull(), order=Order.desc)` and then `.orderby(col)`, so serial numbers with no AMC expiry date are picked last. Here the NULL flag is sorted descending, so NULL rows go to the end. **2. `IfNull(col, sentinel)` as the sort key** Replacing NULL with a fixed value gives NULLs a defined position on both databases. - `get_item_price()` also sorts by `IfNull(ip.batch_no, "")`, `IfNull(ip.customer, "")` and `IfNull(ip.supplier, "")`, all descending. A price tied to a specific batch, customer or supplier wins over a generic one. - `erpnext/www/payment_setup_certification.py` (L24) sorts by `IfNull(cc.to_date, "0001-01-01")` descending. - `erpnext/controllers/queries.py` (L914) sorts by `IfNull(bin_dt.actual_qty, 0)` descending. The same `IfNull` with a far-past or far-future date also appears in the `WHERE` clauses. For example, `get_item_price()` uses `IfNull(valid_from, "2000-01-01")` and `IfNull(valid_upto, "2500-12-31")`. **A third approach: filter the NULLs out** In the Lead Conversion Time report, `get_communication_details()` in `erpnext/crm/report/lead_conversion_time/lead_conversion_time.py` (L119–126) adds `comm.communication_date.isnotnull()` to the `WHERE`. It then runs `.orderby(comm.communication_date).limit(1)` and reads `[0][0]`. With no NULLs left, the earliest dated communication is always the one returned, whichever database runs the query. `test_first_contact_ignores_null_communication_date` in the same folder tests this.

final answer
ERPNext has no `NULLS FIRST` or `NULLS LAST` clauses anywhere. A regex search of the Python files found none. Instead, it makes NULL ordering the same on both databases by rewriting the `ORDER BY` in portable terms. It does this in two ways. **1. A leading `ORDER BY <col> IS NULL` key (an explicit NULL-bucket sort).** This puts the `IS NULL` flag first: 0/false for non-NULL rows, 1/true for NULL rows. Ascending order therefore always puts NULLs last, on both MariaDB and PostgreSQL. A second key then sorts the real values. Both of these sites follow the pattern, and both pick the most specific, most recently started Item Price: - `get_item_price` in `erpnext/stock/get_item_details.py` (around line 1233) uses `.orderby(ip.valid_from.isnull(), order=asc).orderby(ip.valid_from, order=desc).…limit(1)`. The `limit(1)` means the NULL placement decides which price is picked. Rows with a real `valid_from` beat open-ended rows, and the newest wins. - `erpnext/selling/page/point_of_sale/point_of_sale.py` (around line 234) uses the same two keys, `ItemPrice.valid_from.isnull()` ascending then `valid_from` descending. It picks the item price for POS, and the `.where` clauses beside it use `.isnull()` for the validity window. **2. Wrapping the column in `IfNull(col, sentinel)` (the QB `IfNull`, which compiles to `COALESCE`/`IFNULL`).** The sentinel moves NULLs to a known end of the sort. It also makes the ordering deterministic, so no database default applies. - The same `get_item_price` also sorts on `IfNull(ip.batch_no, "")` descending. It does the same for `IfNull(ip.customer, "")` or `IfNull(ip.supplier, "")` descending, depending on whether a customer or supplier is passed. A party- or batch-specific price therefore beats a generic one, because a NULL becomes `""`, which sorts lowest. The result is cut to one row by `limit(1)`. - `erpnext/setup/doctype/email_digest/email_digest.py` (around line 239) has `.orderby(IfNull(todo.date, "1000-01-01"))`. Its inline comment reads "NULL dates first, as MariaDB `date asc` did". The sentinel date makes PostgreSQL copy MariaDB's behaviour of sorting NULLs first in ascending order. - `erpnext/www/payment_setup_certification.py` (around line 24) has `.orderby(IfNull(cc.to_date, "0001-01-01"), order=desc)`. This puts NULL `to_date` rows last in a descending sort, where PostgreSQL would put them first. - `erpnext/controllers/queries.py` (around line 914), in the warehouse query, has `.orderby(IfNull(bin_dt.actual_qty, 0), order=desc)`. A warehouse with no Bin row is treated as 0 and goes last. It is a paged search result, so this affects which rows land on the page. **Related, but not NULL ordering.** Filters elsewhere use the same sentinel idea with `IfNull(ip.valid_from, "2000-01-01")` and `IfNull(ip.valid_upto, "2500-12-31")` in the `WHERE` clause of `get_item_price`. `.github/helper/postgres_compat.py` is a CI lint for MySQL-only SQL. I only saw its `IF()` rule, so I can't say whether it checks NULL ordering. I found these sites with a regex search for `ORDER BY` keys that use `ifnull`/`coalesce`/`is null`. Raw-SQL `ORDER BY` clauses in files other than `.py` could have been missed.

final answer
**Short answer:** No. ERPNext does not throw on an expired batch for a Stock Entry whose purpose is Material Issue or Material Transfer. The expiry check exists, but those purposes are explicitly skipped. I read the code and did not run it. **Where the check lives** - `SerialBatchBundleService.validate_serialized_batch()` is in `erpnext/stock/services/serial_batch_bundle_service.py`, around lines 106–147. - It throws `BatchExpiredError` with "Row #{0}: The batch {1} has already expired." when all of these hold: - `qty > 0` - the row has a `batch_no` - the document has a `posting_date` - `docstatus < 2` - the batch's `expiry_date` is earlier than the document's `posting_date` - This is a validation on the document's `items` rows, so it fires when the document is validated, not only at submit. **Why Stock Entry issue and transfer are exempt** - At lines 110–112 the code sets `is_material_issue = True` when `doc.doctype == "Stock Entry"` and `purpose in ["Material Issue", "Material Transfer"]`. - At lines 130–131, `if is_material_issue: continue` skips the expiry check for those rows. - The expiry throw therefore only applies to other documents, and to Stock Entry purposes such as Material Receipt, Manufacture or Repack. **Other checks that don't cover it** - `StockLedgerEntry.validate_batch()` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_ledger_entry/stock_ledger_entry.py`, around lines 287–299, throws "Batch {0} of Item {1} has expired." But it begins with `if self.batch_no and self.voucher_type != "Stock Entry"`, so Stock Entry is excluded there too. - It also has early returns for Purchase Receipt and Purchase Invoice returns, and for Delivery Note and Sales Invoice returns. - The bundle picker code in `erpnext/stock/doctype/serial_and_batch_bundle/serial_and_batch_bundle.py` filters expired batches out: - `get_non_expired_batches()` at about line 2570. - `expiry_date >= today() OR expiry_date IS NULL` in the batch queries at about lines 3173 and 3518. - That filtering only affects auto-selection and suggestions. It does not stop a user from picking an expired batch by hand.

final answer
**Mostly no.** ERPNext has no general check that throws on an expired batch for a Stock Entry that issues or transfers stock. It does have such a check, but it only runs for a few purposes. **What does validate (the expiry check).** - `StockEntry.validate()` calls `self.validate_batch()` at `stock_entry.py:310`. - `StockEntry.validate_batch` (`stock_entry.py:1479`) only loops over the rows when `purpose` is one of "Material Transfer for Manufacture", "Manufacture", "Repack" or "Send to Subcontractor". - For those purposes it calls `StockEntryDetail.validate_batch` (`stock_entry_detail/stock_entry_detail.py:88`) on each row. - That method throws "Batch {0} of Item {1} has expired." if `posting_date > batch.expiry_date`. It also throws if the batch is disabled. - It runs on validate, which also happens on submit. It is not a separate submit-only hook. **What does not validate.** - A plain "Material Issue", "Material Transfer" or "Material Receipt" skips the check above. - The ledger-level check, `StockLedgerEntry.validate_batch` (`stock_ledger_entry.py:287`), is explicitly skipped when `voucher_type == "Stock Entry"`. That check is the one that covers Delivery Note, Sales Invoice and similar vouchers. - `get_expired_batches` and `get_expired_batch_items` in `stock_entry/services/serial_batch.py` only identify expired batches, for example for auto-selection. They are not submit validators. I did not trace what calls them. **Other related checks.** - `PickList.validate_expired_batches` throws on expired batches, but only for picked rows in a Pick List. - `get_non_expired_batches` in `serial_and_batch_bundle.py` filters expired batches out when serial numbers are fetched. I did not trace whether it also covers batches chosen by hand. I did not check whether the Serial and Batch Bundle validation separately blocks expired batches for these Stock Entry purposes.

final answer
I've finished checking the code. Here's what I found. **Short answer:** Only partly. Expired batches are blocked on some Stock Entry purposes, but a plain Material Issue or Material Transfer has no submit-time expiry check. **Where the checks are** 1. **`StockEntry.validate_batch`** (`erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/stock_entry.py` ~L1479) - It runs `item.validate_batch()` on each row, but only for these purposes: Material Transfer for Manufacture, Manufacture, Repack and Send to Subcontractor. - `StockEntryDetail.validate_batch` (`stock_entry_detail/stock_entry_detail.py` L88–99) throws "Batch {0} of Item {1} has expired." when the Stock Entry's `posting_date` is later than the batch's `expiry_date`. - It also throws if the batch is disabled. - Material Issue and Material Transfer are not in that list, so this check doesn't apply to them. 2. **`SerialBatchBundleService.validate_serialized_batch`** (`erpnext/stock/services/serial_batch_bundle_service.py` L106–147) - It throws `BatchExpiredError` ("Row #{0}: The batch {1} has already expired."). - It sets `is_material_issue = True` for Stock Entry purposes "Material Issue" and "Material Transfer", then does `continue` before the expiry check. So those two purposes skip it. - It also only looks at rows with both a `serial_no` and a `batch_no`. 3. **`StockLedgerEntry.validate_batch`** (`stock_ledger_entry/stock_ledger_entry.py` L287–299) - It throws "Batch … has expired." when `posting_date` is after `expiry_date`. - It is explicitly skipped when `voucher_type == "Stock Entry"`. It does apply to Delivery Note, Sales Invoice, Purchase Receipt and similar vouchers. **What does apply to plain issue and transfer** - The batch picker for Serial and Batch Bundles only offers unexpired batches. The queries in `serial_and_batch_bundle.py` (~L2580, L3173, L3518) filter on `expiry_date >= today() or expiry_date is null`. Auto-pick also sorts by earliest expiry. - `get_expired_batches()` in `stock_entry/services/serial_batch.py` (~L363) lists batches whose expiry has passed. I didn't trace where it's used. That filtering is a UI and selection safeguard, not a validation. I didn't find a submit-time throw for a manually entered expired batch on Material Issue or Material Transfer. I didn't trace the Serial and Batch Bundle's own validation for an expiry check, so treat the "no check" conclusion as based on the paths above.

final answer
**No. A Stock Entry that issues or transfers stock does not throw on an expired batch. The expiry checks in the code are skipped for Material Issue and Material Transfer.** I read the code and did not run it. **1. The Stock Entry check skips issues and transfers.** - `StockEntry.validate()` calls `sbb.validate_serialized_batch()` (in `erpnext/stock/services/serial_batch_bundle_service.py`, around line 106). - That method does throw `BatchExpiredError` ("Row #{0}: The batch {1} has already expired."). It does so when `expiry_date < posting_date` for a row with `qty > 0` and a `batch_no`. - It returns early first: `is_material_issue` is set to True when the doctype is Stock Entry and the purpose is "Material Issue" or "Material Transfer", and those rows hit `continue` before the expiry check. - So the check only applies to other purposes. Material Receipt, Manufacture and Repack are examples. - Expired batches are therefore allowed to leave a warehouse, which is the normal case for disposing of or moving expired stock. **2. The other `validate_batch` methods don't cover it either.** - `StockEntry.validate_batch()` only loops over purposes "Material Transfer for Manufacture", "Manufacture", "Repack" and "Send to Subcontractor", calling `item.validate_batch()` on each row. Issue and Material Transfer aren't in that list. I didn't open `StockEntryDetail.validate_batch`, so I can't say whether it checks expiry. - `StockLedgerEntry.validate_batch()` (`stock_ledger_entry.py`, line 287) does throw "Batch {0} of Item {1} has expired." when the posting date is after the expiry date. It is guarded by `voucher_type != "Stock Entry"`, so it never fires for Stock Entries. It applies to Delivery Note, Sales Invoice and similar vouchers, with exemptions for returns. **3. Expired batches are only hidden from automatic selection.** - `get_non_expired_batches` and the `expiry_date >= today()` filters in `serial_and_batch_bundle.py` keep expired batches out of auto-pick and the batch picker. - This is a convenience filter and not a validation. A user can still choose an expired batch by hand.

No. As far as I can find, ERPNext does not automatically email the customer a confirmation when a Sales Order is submitted. I searched the Sales Order code and `hooks.py` and found nothing that does it. - **Submit flow:** `SalesOrder.on_submit` in `erpnext/selling/doctype/sales_order/sales_order.py` (around line 450) only does internal work. It calls these, in order: - `update_prevdoc_status` - `check_credit_limit` - `update_reserved_qty` - `DeliveryScheduleService(...).delete_removed_delivery_schedule_items` - the `Authorization Control` check, `validate_approving_authority` - `update_project` - `update_blanket_order` - `update_linked_doc` for inter-company orders - `update_coupon_code_count` - `create_stock_reservation_entries`, when stock reservation is on None of these sends an email. - **No mail calls:** The `sales_order` folder contains no `frappe.sendmail` call. I also found no Sales Order notification fixture in the repo. You can still get an email on submit by setting it up yourself. In the Frappe framework, create a **Notification** document (Setup > Notification) on the Sales Order doctype with the event "Submit" and the customer's contact email as the recipient. That runs in Frappe's document-event layer, not in ERPNext code. The user can also send the order manually with the Email action in the document's Print/Email dialog. I did not check the Frappe framework code, so I haven't confirmed how Notification documents are triggered there.

Q28Group F — Trap / hallucination resistanceSales Order enforces a customer credit limit before submission. Does Purchase Order have an equivalent automatic spend-limit check…
Sales Order enforces a customer credit limit before submission. Does Purchase Order have an equivalent automatic spend-limit check against the supplier before submission? If something does gate Purchase Order submission, what mechanism is it, and how does it differ in nature from a credit limit?

final answer
No. Purchase Order has no automatic spend-limit check against the supplier. What gates it is the Supplier Scorecard standing flag `prevent_pos`. **No credit-limit equivalent.** A search of `erpnext/buying` for `credit_limit` and `check_credit_limit` finds nothing. The Supplier doctype in `buying/doctype/supplier/supplier.py` has no monetary limit field. I did not check the Accounts module, so I can't say whether a credit limit exists there for suppliers. **What does gate Purchase Order submission.** `PurchaseOrder.validate_supplier()` in `buying/doctype/purchase_order/purchase_order.py` (around line 272) runs from `validate()`, which also runs on submit. It reads two flags from the Supplier: - **`prevent_pos`:** if it is set, the method looks up the supplier's Supplier Scorecard status and calls `frappe.throw`. The message is "Purchase Orders are not allowed for {supplier} due to a scorecard standing of {standing}". This is a hard block. - **`warn_pos`:** if it is set, the method shows a `frappe.msgprint` "Caution" message with an orange indicator. This is only a warning and does not block. **Where the flags come from.** `buying/doctype/supplier_scorecard/supplier_scorecard.py` computes a standing for each supplier from periodic performance scoring. Each standing carries `prevent_pos`, `warn_pos`, `prevent_rfqs` and `warn_rfqs` settings, and the scorecard copies these onto the Supplier record. `RequestForQuotation` has a matching `prevent_rfqs` check (`request_for_quotation.py`, around line 125). **Other things that can stop a Purchase Order.** - `check_for_on_hold_or_closed_status("Material Request", ...)` blocks a PO whose source Material Request is on hold or closed. - The Supplier's `on_hold` and `hold_type` fields (All, Invoices or Payments) appear in `test_purchase_order.py`, but those tests cover invoices and payments, not PO creation. **How this differs from a credit limit.** - **Basis:** a customer credit limit is a quantitative financial check. It compares the customer's outstanding receivables plus the new order against a numeric limit set per customer or per company. The supplier check is a qualitative flag, a boolean tied to a performance-based scorecard standing. - **Exposure:** the credit limit manages the company's risk of not being paid. The scorecard controls how much the company relies on a supplier, based on quality, delivery and price scores. - **Amount sensitivity:** the credit limit depends on the order amount and the current balance. The supplier check ignores both. It blocks or warns on every Purchase Order for that supplier, whatever its value. - **Override:** the credit limit has a "bypass credit limit check" role override. The `prevent_pos` check has no override. It clears only when the scorecard standing changes or the flag is edited.

final answer
**No.** Purchase Order has no supplier spend limit. ERPNext has no supplier-side counterpart to `Customer Credit Limit`. I searched the code for one and found none. I did not search the Supplier doctype fields exhaustively. **What gates Purchase Order submission instead** It is the supplier "on hold" block. `AccountsController.ensure_supplier_is_not_blocked()` is in `erpnext/controllers/accounts_controller.py`, and `AccountsController.validate()` calls it. Purchase Order inherits this through its controller chain. The check works like this: - It applies when the doctype is Purchase Order or Purchase Invoice, and also to Payment Entry when the party is a Supplier. - It loads the Supplier and looks at `on_hold`, `hold_type` and `release_date`. - For a Purchase Order or Purchase Invoice, it blocks when `on_hold` is set and `hold_type` is "All" or "Invoices". For a Payment Entry to a supplier, `hold_type` must be "All" or "Payments". - The block stops applying once `release_date` has passed. If there is no `release_date`, it applies indefinitely. - When it applies, it calls `frappe.msgprint(..., raise_exception=1)` with "{supplier} is blocked so this transaction cannot proceed". This means a Purchase Order cannot be saved or submitted for that supplier. - `test_po_for_blocked_supplier_all` and `test_po_for_blocked_supplier_invoices` in `test_purchase_order.py` cover this. `get_supplier_block_status` is a helper that returns the hold fields. It is used by Payment Entry. **How it differs from a credit limit** - **Binary vs. quantitative:** The supplier block is a flag with an optional expiry date. It does not compare any amount to a limit. `check_credit_limit` in `erpnext/selling/doctype/customer/customer.py` compares `get_customer_outstanding` plus `extra_amount` against `get_credit_limit`, which is set per company in the `Customer Credit Limit` table. - **Who sets the limit:** The credit limit sits on the Customer, per company, and limits exposure to receivables. The supplier hold is a manual status on the Supplier. It is set by a user, not derived from payables or order totals. - **Override:** A credit-limit breach can be overridden by a user with the "credit controller" role (Accounts Settings). Customers can also bypass it per company with `bypass_credit_limit_check`. A user who lacks that role gets an exception plus an option to email the credit controllers. The supplier block has no role override. It is lifted only by clearing `on_hold` or by the `release_date` passing. - **Where it runs:** Sales Order calls `check_credit_limit` from `on_submit` and `on_update_after_submit`. The supplier block runs in `validate`, so it fires on every save, not only at submit. Separately, `erpnext/accounts/utils.py` has `_check_credit_limit_warn`, which gives a pre-submit warning for the customer limit on Sales Order, Sales Invoice and Delivery Note. It has no purchase-side equivalent. Purchase Orders can also be limited by Budget checks (`test_monthly_budget_crossed_for_po`). That is a cost-center and account budget, not a per-supplier limit. I did not trace that code.

**Short answer:** No. Purchase Order has no supplier-side equivalent of the customer credit limit. I found no spend-limit check against the supplier, no supplier credit limit and no outstanding-balance check anywhere in `purchase_order.py`. **The Sales Order side** - `SalesOrder.check_credit_limit` (`sales_order.py:514`) calls `customer.check_credit_limit` (`customer.py:514`). - That function compares the customer's outstanding receivables and open orders, plus the new amount, against a per-customer, per-company limit. - Delivery Note, Sales Invoice and Journal Entry have the same kind of method. There is no `check_credit_limit` in the buying code. **What gates Purchase Order submission** There are three mechanisms. I read the first two in `purchase_order.py`. For the third I only checked where it is defined. 1. **Supplier Scorecard standing (`PurchaseOrder.validate_supplier`, line 272)** - It runs on validate, so it applies before submit. - If the Supplier has `prevent_pos` set, it throws an error that names the scorecard standing. - If `warn_pos` is set, it only shows a caution message. - These are flags on the Supplier, driven by the scorecard status. They are not tied to any amount. 2. **Budget check (`validate_budget`, called in `PurchaseOrder.on_submit`, line 386)** - The method is in `buying_controller.py:978`. It calls `validate_expense_against_budget` in `accounts/doctype/budget/budget.py`. - It checks the PO's expense against the Budget set for the cost center, project or account. - That is a spend limit, but it is set on the company's own accounting dimensions, not on the supplier. 3. **Authorization Control** - `on_submit` also calls `Authorization Control.validate_approving_authority(doctype, company, base_grand_total)`. - It checks the submitting user's role or approver against the PO value. **Other pre-submit validations** These are not limits on the supplier: - minimum order quantity per item - blanket order limits - `validate_with_previous_doc` consistency with the Supplier Quotation and Material Request - the inter-company party check **How this differs from a credit limit** | | Customer credit limit | Purchase Order gates | |---|---|---| | Limit set on | The customer, per company | Supplier (scorecard flag), or the company's budget and user authorization | | What it measures | The customer's outstanding receivables plus open orders (money owed to you) | A scorecard standing, a budget allocation, or the submitting user's authority | | Nature | Counterparty exposure limit, a financial risk control | Supplier performance and qualitative standing, internal budget control, and approval rules | A credit limit protects you from lending to a customer who may not pay. The Purchase Order gates don't cap what you owe a supplier. Any PO-side "limit" comes from your own budget or approval rules, or from the supplier's scorecard. Also, the budget and authorization checks run in `on_submit`. An exception there still aborts the submission, so they block it, but they are not a validate-time warning.

final answer
The premise doesn't hold: ERPNext has no method that cancels the linked Purchase Invoice when you cancel a Purchase Receipt. It blocks the cancellation instead. I found this by searching the codebase, and I didn't run it. **What happens on cancel** - `PurchaseReceipt.on_cancel` is in `erpnext/stock/doctype/purchase_receipt/purchase_receipt.py`, at about line 437. - It runs `frappe.get_all("Purchase Invoice Item", filters={"purchase_receipt": self.name, "docstatus": 1}, fields=["parent"], limit=1)`. - That is how it finds the linked invoice. It looks up submitted Purchase Invoice Item rows whose `purchase_receipt` field points at this receipt, then reads the row's `parent`, which is the invoice name. - If a row comes back, it calls `frappe.throw(_("Purchase Invoice {0} is already submitted"))`. The receipt is not cancelled, and the invoice is not touched. **The same check in a second method** - `PurchaseReceipt.check_next_docstatus`, at about line 426, runs the same query with the same error message. - A test in `test_purchase_receipt.py` (around line 6123) covers it. **What you have to do** Cancel the Purchase Invoice first, then the Purchase Receipt. The invoice is the later document in the chain, and Frappe's own linked-document check on cancel also enforces this. **Where cancel does reach into other documents** - After the check passes, `on_cancel` calls `update_billing_status` and `update_prevdoc_status`. These reverse billed amounts and Purchase Order quantities. - It also calls `make_gl_entries_on_cancel`, which reverses the accounting entries. - It sets `ignore_linked_doctypes` to GL Entry, Stock Ledger Entry, Repost Item Valuation and Serial and Batch Bundle, so those links don't block the cancel. I did not find any `.cancel()` call on a Purchase Invoice anywhere in the non-test code.

final answer
I checked, and the premise doesn't hold: ERPNext doesn't cascade-cancel the invoice here. Cancelling a billed Purchase Receipt is blocked with an error, and no method auto-cancels the Purchase Invoice. The check is in `erpnext/stock/doctype/purchase_receipt/purchase_receipt.py`. There are two places: - **`PurchaseReceipt.on_cancel`** (line 437) runs the check itself, inline. - **`PurchaseReceipt.check_next_docstatus`** (line 426) is a separate method that does the same query. It is called only from a test (`test_check_next_docstatus_blocks_with_submitted_invoice`). I didn't find a production caller. In both, it finds the linked invoice by querying the child table, not the parent: ```python frappe.get_all("Purchase Invoice Item", filters={"purchase_receipt": self.name, "docstatus": 1}, fields=["parent"], as_list=True, limit=1) ``` It looks for a submitted `Purchase Invoice Item` row (`docstatus=1`) whose `purchase_receipt` field points at this receipt. The `parent` of that row is the Purchase Invoice name. If one exists, it calls `frappe.throw(_("Purchase Invoice {0} is already submitted"))`, which stops the cancel. You have to cancel the Purchase Invoice yourself first, then the Purchase Receipt. I searched `purchase_receipt.py` and the wider codebase for any cancel-the-invoice logic and found none. The other places I looked were not cascades: - `BuyingController.on_cancel` only updates the last purchase rate and handles linked assets. - `PurchaseInvoice.on_cancel` runs when the invoice itself is cancelled and updates the receipt's billing status through `BillingStatusService.update_billing_status_in_pr`. - `BillingStatusService.cancel_provisional_entries` cancels the receipt's provisional GL entries when an invoice is posted. It doesn't cancel any document. I didn't check for custom apps or hooks that might add a cascade.

final answer
I haven't found a method that does this. I've searched the purchase receipt, the buying and accounts controllers, and every non-test `.cancel()` call site in those areas. I'm answering from what I found. **Nothing cancels the Purchase Invoice automatically.** ERPNext does the opposite. `PurchaseReceipt.on_cancel` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/purchase_receipt/purchase_receipt.py` (around line 437) blocks the cancellation. It runs this query: ```python submitted = frappe.get_all( "Purchase Invoice Item", filters={"purchase_receipt": self.name, "docstatus": 1}, fields=["parent"], as_list=True, limit=1, ) if submitted: frappe.throw(_("Purchase Invoice {0} is already submitted").format(submitted[0][0])) ``` **How it finds the linked invoice:** it queries the `Purchase Invoice Item` child table for rows whose `purchase_receipt` field equals the receipt's name and whose `docstatus` is 1. The `parent` of the first match is the Purchase Invoice. The user has to cancel that invoice first. `PurchaseReceipt.on_submit` has a similar check near line 430, which throws "Purchase Invoice {0} is already submitted". The only cascading `.cancel()` calls I found in the controllers are for system-generated Credit/Debit Note Journal Entries, in `AccountsController.cancel_system_generated_credit_debit_notes`. Others are for exchange gain/loss and common-party journals, and for Unreconcile Payment records. None of them cancel Purchase Invoices. I didn't search the whole repo exhaustively, so a custom app or a patch could still do a cascade. In core ERPNext, the receipt cancel path just raises an error.

I couldn't find a method that does this, because ERPNext doesn't do it. In this codebase, cancelling a billed Purchase Receipt is blocked, not cascaded. `PurchaseReceipt.on_cancel` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/purchase_receipt/purchase_receipt.py` (around line 437) shows this. It looks for a submitted invoice linked to the receipt with this query: ```python submitted = frappe.get_all( "Purchase Invoice Item", filters={"purchase_receipt": self.name, "docstatus": 1}, fields=["parent"], as_list=True, limit=1, ) if submitted: frappe.throw(_("Purchase Invoice {0} is already submitted").format(submitted[0][0])) ``` So the linked invoice is found by querying the child table `Purchase Invoice Item`. It matches rows whose `purchase_receipt` field equals the receipt's name and whose `docstatus` is 1. The invoice name is taken from the row's `parent`. If one exists, cancellation stops with an error, and the user has to cancel the Purchase Invoice first. `PurchaseReceipt.check_next_docstatus` (around line 426) runs the same query and raises the same error. I also searched the code for `.cancel()` calls and for functions with names like `cancel_*invoice*`. The only cascade-style method I found is `POSInvoiceMergeLog.cancel_linked_invoices`. It cancels the consolidated Sales Invoice and credit note, which is a different flow and has nothing to do with Purchase Receipts. I did not read every `.cancel()` hit in the results (the output was truncated), so a cascade I missed is unlikely but not ruled out.

final answer
The check is `SellingController.validate_selling_price()` in `erpnext/controllers/selling_controller.py` (defined at line 295). I read that file but didn't run anything. **Where it runs.** `SellingController.validate()` calls it at line 65. Sales Invoice and Delivery Note both inherit from `SellingController`, so it runs when either is saved or submitted. Sales Order and Quotation use it too. `erpnext/accounts/services/child_item_update.py:174` also calls it when items are updated after submit. **What it checks.** For each item row, in order: 1. **Skipped cases:** It returns early for returns (`is_return`) or when "Validate Selling Price" is off in Selling Settings. It skips rows with no item code and free items. 2. **Last purchase rate:** If `base_net_rate` is below the item's `last_purchase_rate` (multiplied by `conversion_factor`), it throws. 3. **Valuation rate:** This step is skipped for internal customers and non-stock items. Otherwise it compares `base_net_rate` against `incoming_rate × conversion_factor` and throws if the rate is lower. On Sales Order and Quotation the field is `valuation_rate` instead of `incoming_rate`. Delivery Note and Sales Invoice rows carry `incoming_rate`, the actual cost from the stock ledger. **How it blocks.** The inner helper `throw_message` calls `frappe.throw` with the title "Invalid Selling Price". The message reads "Selling rate for item X is lower than its valuation rate / last purchase rate…". It also says the check can be disabled in Selling Settings. **Setting.** The check is controlled by the `validate_selling_price` checkbox in Selling Settings (`selling_settings.json`). `test_delivery_note.py::test_validate_selling_price` covers the Delivery Note case.

final answer
The block is in `SellingController.validate_selling_price()` in `erpnext/controllers/selling_controller.py` (lines 295–349). Sales Invoice and Delivery Note both inherit from `SellingController`. `SellingController.validate()` calls this method, and so does `ChildItemUpdater._post_update` in `erpnext/accounts/services/child_item_update.py`. I did not open `validate()` or `_post_update` to check where in their flow the call sits. **When the check runs** - It does nothing for return documents (`is_return`). - It does nothing unless **Selling Settings → "Validate Selling Price"** (`validate_selling_price`) is enabled. The error message tells the user they can switch this off to bypass the check. - It skips rows with no `item_code` and rows marked `is_free_item`. **What it compares for each item row** 1. **Last purchase rate:** it takes the item's `last_purchase_rate` and converts it to the selling UOM by multiplying by `conversion_factor`. If `item.base_net_rate` is lower than that, it throws. 2. **Valuation cost:** this is skipped for internal customers and for non-stock items. For Sales Order and Quotation the comparison field is `valuation_rate`. For Sales Invoice and Delivery Note it is the row's `incoming_rate`, which is the stock valuation cost at the time of sale. That value is also multiplied by `conversion_factor`. If `base_net_rate` is lower, it throws. **How it blocks** The inner helper `throw_message` calls `frappe.throw`. The message reads "Row #N: Selling rate for item X is lower than its valuation rate / last purchase rate. Selling net rate should be at least Y." The dialog title is "Invalid Selling Price". Because the throw happens during validation, the document cannot be saved or submitted.

final answer
The check is `SellingController.validate_selling_price()` in `erpnext/controllers/selling_controller.py` (line 295). Sales Invoice and Delivery Note both inherit it, and it runs from `SellingController.validate()` (line 65). It runs when the document is saved or validated, so it also stops submission. **How it works** - It is switched on by the **Validate Selling Price** checkbox in Selling Settings (`validate_selling_price`). If that is off, or the document is a return (`is_return`), the method returns without checking anything. - Free items (`is_free_item`) and rows with no item code are skipped. - **Last purchase rate:** for each row it compares `base_net_rate` with the item's `last_purchase_rate`, converted to the sales UOM by multiplying by `conversion_factor`. If the selling rate is lower, it throws. - **Valuation rate:** this check is skipped for internal customers and for non-stock items. For the rest, it compares `base_net_rate` with the row's rate field, again multiplied by `conversion_factor`. The field is `valuation_rate` for Sales Order and Quotation, and `incoming_rate` for Sales Invoice and Delivery Note. `incoming_rate` is the stock valuation cost of the item at that point. If the selling rate is lower, it throws. - **The error:** both failures go through the inner `throw_message()`, which raises "Row #N: Selling rate for item X is lower than its valuation rate / last purchase rate…" with the title "Invalid Selling Price". The message tells the user they can disable the setting in Selling Settings to bypass the check. `validate_selling_price` is also called from `_post_update` in `erpnext/accounts/services/child_item_update.py`. That is the path for updating items on a submitted Sales Order.
